# Tracker

> Status: **Living document.** Owner: Engineering lead. Update at every increment.
> The item-level open-work list: one row per work item, from every module spec, the security
> backlog, the test gaps and the 2026-08 review. For the summary answer to "where are we" — the
> capability matrix, invariant status, open gaps and the decisions waiting on a person — read
> `60-delivery/00-status.md` instead. This file is the detail behind it, not a second copy of it.

**Last reviewed:** 2026-09-02 (§K summary recount only — individual row edits happen continuously and are not all reflected in this date)
**Note on evidence text:** counts inside row `Exit` cells are landing-time evidence unless a row says they were re-checked later. Do not read an old route/test total in a row as the current repo-wide total.

> **Current queue:** [section P](#p-current-execution-queue-reconciled-2026-09-11). All 79 previously open/partial item rows were reconciled: 78 merged into explicit successors, and UX-16 closed with removal evidence. Do not start work from an older row without following its successor.

## How to use this

| Column | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **ID** | Stable identifier, matching the owning module spec's open-work table |
| **Item** | What needs to exist |
| **Mod** | Owning module (`20-modules/`) |
| **Ph** | Roadmap phase |
| **Pri** | P0 blocks the phase · P1 required for the phase · P2 desirable |
| **Status** | `TODO` · `IN PROGRESS` · `BLOCKED` · `DONE` · `N/A` |
| **Owner** | Named individual — `—` means unassigned, which is itself a tracked problem |
| **Exit** | The objectively verifiable condition for `DONE` |

**Rules.** An item is `DONE` only when its exit condition is verifiably met. "Code written" is not `DONE`. `BLOCKED` requires a named blocker. An unassigned P0 is escalated at every review.

**The live-call-site rule (AU-2, added 2026-08-31, after `Docs/60-delivery/04-end-to-end-audit-2026-08-30.md` found 17 DONE rows — six P0 — were unreachable dead code that passed its own unit tests):** whenever a row's exit evidence asserts that a module, function or endpoint is "wired", "reachable", "live", "called from", or otherwise part of a real request/event path, that evidence must name a concrete `file:line` call site that is itself reachable from one of the running application's real entry points — not merely state that unit tests pass or that the module/file exists. "Reachable from a real entry point" means transitively imported (or, for a scheduled/event path, invoked) from one of the five processes named by the `ENTRY_POINTS` dict in `tests/test_reachability_gate.py`: `aida.main` (FastAPI app), `aida.workflows.worker` (Temporal worker), `aida.workflows.scheduler` (fleet scheduler), `aida.projectors.graph_projector` (Kafka consumer) and `aida.projectors.outbox_publisher` (Kafka producer) — re-check that list in the test file itself before citing it, since it is the authority, not this paragraph. Two exceptions must be stated explicitly when invoked, not assumed: (a) module-level reachability (import graph, what the gate above enforces) is a *necessary but not sufficient* condition — a row claiming a *specific function* is live needs a call site for that function, not just proof its containing module is imported by something on a live path (see AU-1's row, whose `ai_decision_lineage.py` example — module-reachable but its core function was not, before AU-5 fixed that — is exactly the case this distinction exists for); (b) a row legitimately blocked on infrastructure the test harness cannot provide (a live Kafka broker, a real IdP) may cite an in-process proof instead, but must say so explicitly rather than silently substitute a unit test for a live path. A row that cannot produce this evidence is not `DONE` — mark it `IN PROGRESS` or `TODO` with the real gap named, the same way `tests/test_reachability_gate.py`'s `ALLOWLIST` names the owning tracker row for every module it excuses. Future rows written by any session should be checked against this rule before being marked `DONE`.

### ID prefixes

Two vocabularies meet in this file, and both are stable identifiers rather than sequence numbers.

| Prefix | Meaning | Defined by |
|---|---|---|
| `ST-`, `CN-`, `IN-`, `CA-`, `PR-`, `RI-`, `SE-`, `GL-`, `LN-`, `KG-`, `DQ-`, `RT-`, `AG-`, `TL-`, `MG-`, `QG-`, `PG-`, `SD-`, `CP-`, `UX-`, `OB-`, `PF-` | Module open-work, matching the owning spec's table in `20-modules/` | The owning module spec |
| `BD-` | A decision required from the bank | §J below |
| `AR-` | A finding of the 2026-09-09 agent architecture critical review (`10-architecture/15-agent-architecture-critical-review.md`) | §O below |
| `K-`, `C-`, `N-`, `E-`, `D-` | The 2026-08 review's verdict on an existing or proposed capability: **K**eep, **C**orrect, **N**ew, **E**ngineering debt, **D**rop | Re-homed here 2026-08-30 from `review-2026-08/gap/02-gap-diff-and-plan.md`, which stays as the historical plan with the original engineer-week and risk estimates |

A review item and a module item can describe the same work from two directions — `N7` (build an
ABAC engine) and `PG-7` (module 17 needs a policy decision point) are the same thing. Where that
happens the row names both, rather than being listed twice.

| ST-15 | `edge_kind` vocabulary matches the lineage contract | 09 | 0 | P1 | DONE | — | Landed 2026-09-01. Root cause was a conflation of two different axes: the tracker row read `edge_kind="SUGGESTED_RELATIONSHIP"` (a *relationship/grant* edge-kind, carried by `CrossBoundaryGrant.edge_kinds` and the unified-graph relationship payloads) and the `edge_kind` **column** on the four lineage-edge tables (`openlineage_table_edge`, `openlineage_column_edge`, `bi_report_metric_edge`, `bi_metric_column_edge`) as if they were one vocabulary. They are not — verified by grep that the four ORM columns are never assigned explicitly and only ever hold their defaults (`ETL` for the OpenLineage edges, `BI` for the BI edges), both already in the contract's set; the `SUGGESTED_RELATIONSHIP` assignments are payload/graph fields on a different axis, never written to these columns. Resolution: (1) **one agreed vocabulary** — `aida.models.LINEAGE_EDGE_KINDS = (QUERY, VIEW, PROCEDURE, ETL, DBT, BI, AI_DECISION)`, exactly the target set in `Docs/30-contracts/06-lineage-contract.md` §2; (2) **DB-level constraint** — a CHECK constraint on every `edge_kind` column of the four tables (model `__table_args__` + migration `d7b1e5a9c204`, single Alembic head preserved: `alembic heads` = `d7b1e5a9c204`), rejecting anything outside the vocabulary, no backfill needed since existing values are all in-set; (3) **`30-contracts/06` matching** — the edge `kind` line's implementation-status note rewritten from "TARGET vocabulary / free-text String(30)" to "ENFORCED", and now explicitly distinguishes the lineage edge-kind axis (this) from the relationship/grant edge-kind axis (SUGGESTED_RELATIONSHIP etc.), correcting the earlier conflation. Verified: `tests/test_lineage_edge_kind_vocabulary.py` **22 passed** — asserts model↔contract and model↔migration vocabularies are in lockstep, and functionally proves each of the four tables rejects `SUGGESTED_RELATIONSHIP` (`IntegrityError`) and accepts ETL/BI/QUERY/AI_DECISION against a real (in-memory SQLite) engine. `ruff check` and `mypy src/aida/models.py` clean. `tests/test_migration_orm_drift.py` skips (needs live Postgres, standing sandbox limit); model and migration add identically-named constraints so they are aligned by construction |

| CN-7 | Per-connector health scoring | 02 | A | P1 | DONE | — | Delivered 2026-09-01. Deterministic, explainable 5-factor health score computed read-only from existing `AnalysisRun`/`ScanPolicy`/`DataSource` rows -- no model/schema change, no migration. `src/aida/connector_health.py` (pure, DB-free, mirrors `aida.trust_scoring`'s `TrustFactor`/`compute_trust_score` shape): `RUN_SUCCESS_RATE` (35pt, recent terminal-run success share), `STALENESS` (25pt, minutes since last success against `ScanPolicy.interval_minutes`, linear decay past it), `FAILURE_STREAK` (20pt, consecutive failures from the newest run, 3+ trips a `REPEATED_FAILURES` blocker), `PROFILING_COVERAGE` (10pt, profiled/discovered tables on the latest success), `DATASOURCE_ENABLEMENT` (10pt, 0 when `DISABLED`) -- `compute_connector_health` (`connector_health.py:279`) combines them into a 0-100 score plus `HEALTHY`/`DEGRADED`/`CRITICAL`/`UNKNOWN` status (`UNKNOWN`, not a low score, when there is no run history at all). DB aggregation in `src/aida/fleet.py`: `datasource_health` (`:124`) for one connector, `fleet_health` (`:160`) for a whole org via one `row_number() OVER (PARTITION BY datasource_id ...)` windowed query (same idiom `aida.catalog_read_model` uses) instead of one query per datasource. Exposed on the existing fleet API surface `src/aida/operational_api.py`: `GET /v1/datasources/{id}/health` (`:234`) and `GET /v1/organizations/{id}/fleet-health` (`:424`, `response_model=Page`), both through `operational_router` included at `aida.main.py:292` (imported `:55`) -- reachable from the `aida.main` `ENTRY_POINTS` FastAPI app per `tests/test_reachability_gate.py`. Response models (`ConnectorHealthScoreRead`/`ConnectorHealthFactorRead`) are local `ApiModel`s in `operational_api.py`, not `aida.schemas` -- same pattern `aida.policy_native_sync_api`/`aida.sql_validation_api` already use, so this doesn't contend with the concurrent ST-05/ST-06 split. Visible in fleet view: `ui/app.js`'s `loadOrganizationData` fetches `fleet-health` alongside `fleet-summary`, `renderSources()` adds a Health column (`healthCell`) with a per-factor-reason tooltip; `ui/scripts/core.js`'s `statusClass` gained `DEGRADED`/`UNKNOWN` styling. Tests: 33 pure-logic tests in `tests/test_connector_health.py` (no database -- every factor's edge cases, composite blockers/status, determinism, weights-sum-to-100, every factor explainable) plus 11 integration tests appended to `tests/test_operational_behaviors.py` against a real in-memory SQLite engine (`tests/test_asset_evidence.py`'s own rationale), covering `datasource_health`/`fleet_health` end-to-end, `ScanPolicy`-driven staleness, missing/never-run datasources, the `fleet_health` N+1-free shape, and both endpoint functions' cross-org 403 / missing-datasource 404 / explainable-response behavior. Full `uv run pytest` clean on the tree rebased onto the concurrently-landed ST-05/ST-06 `connectivity` split and UX-14's type generator, except 10 pre-existing `tests/test_doc_claims.py` failures confirmed (via `git stash`) to be entirely UX-14's own new tracker/log citations, unrelated to this row; `ruff check`, `mypy src` (216 files), `lint-imports` (5/5 kept) all clean. The 2 new routes changed `app.openapi()`: regenerated `Docs/90-reference/openapi-baseline.json` (`scripts/openapi_diff.py --accept-baseline`, confirmed additive-only) and UX-14's generated `ui-next/src/lib/types.ts` (`scripts/generate_ui_types.py --accept-baseline`, 360->362 schemas); `cd ui-next && npm run typecheck && npm run test && npm run build` all green against the regenerated types. Found and confirmed pre-existing/unrelated by `git stash` isolation: 10 `tests/test_doc_claims.py` failures from UX-14's own tracker/log citations (`scripts/*.py` files and two CI job names the test's citation resolver doesn't recognize) -- reproduce identically with none of this row's changes present; queued as a separate task rather than fixed here (out of this row's scope) |

| CN-9 | `Connector.get_query_history()` -- the one honest gap AT-12 names in its own row (`query_history_miner.py` mines an already-materialized `WarehouseQueryLogEntry` sequence; nothing produces one from a live warehouse) | 02 | A | P1 | MERGED | — | 2026-09-11: continue only in R11-B12 (section P below); previous status: IN PROGRESS. Completed implementation is retained; only the successor remainder is in scope. **Historical acceptance/evidence:** **Applied 2026-09-03 (connector methods only -- not yet certified).** `Connector.get_query_history()` added to the base class exactly as designed below (non-abstract, fails closed with `ConnectorQueryHistoryUnsupported`); implemented for Snowflake (`ACCOUNT_USAGE.QUERY_HISTORY`) and BigQuery (region-qualified `JOBS_BY_PROJECT`) with the bounded since/limit contract and the success-only/DONE-only filters this row specifies; new `QueryLogEntry` dataclass lives in `connectors/base.py`, not imported from `query_history_miner.py`, to keep the module-02-is-a-lower-layer direction intact (ADR-0023-adjacent reasoning, not a new ADR). Unit tests added (`tests/test_connectors.py`, `tests/test_connectors_snowflake.py`, `tests/test_connectors_bigquery.py`) covering the fail-closed default, row-mapping, bind-parameter bounding, and incomplete-row dropping. `ruff check` clean on every touched file; `ast.parse` clean. **Not yet run: `mypy`, `pytest`, `lint-imports`** -- this session's local tool environment has no working Python interpreter (every `.venv*` here points at a different, now-gone session's interpreter path) and rebuilding one hit `No space left on device` on this session's own scratch disk (unrelated to the project mount, which has ample space) -- a genuine environment gap, not a result being omitted. Databricks (`system.query.history`) not yet implemented. Per module 02 §11, `query_history` capability flags stay `False` regardless -- nothing here flips them; that still requires the certification check this row's exit condition names, which in turn needs a live account. INV-6 gap-proof test (`test_inv6_value_freedom.py`-style sentinel scan proving `sql_text` never lands in persisted state) also not yet written -- there is no caller wiring this into `mine_and_land_query_history_candidates` yet for it to scan. **Original design retained below for the untouched parts (BigQuery grant verification, Databricks, certification check, HTTP-trigger follow-on).** **Design, not yet built.** New method on `Connector`, added the same way `profile_column_values` was: NOT `@abstractmethod` (module 02 §6 already lists `get_query_history()` in the target interface, unimplemented), a default that fails closed with a dedicated `ConnectorQueryHistoryUnsupported(NotImplementedError)` rather than an empty result, gated by both `capabilities.query_history` and an explicit per-connector override -- INV-9's existing convention, not a new one. **Snowflake**: `SNOWFLAKE.ACCOUNT_USAGE.QUERY_HISTORY` filtered on `START_TIME >= :since`, `DATABASE_NAME`, `EXECUTION_STATUS = 'SUCCESS'`, bounded by a hard row cap (mirrors the `agent_retrieval_scan_limit` bounded-scan convention already used elsewhere, not unbounded paging); note `ACCOUNT_USAGE` needs an `IMPORTED PRIVILEGES` grant beyond the read-only discovery role, so a missing grant must fail closed to `query_history=False` rather than fail the whole connector (CN-1b's Oracle `PLAN_TABLE` row is the precedent for this shape of degrade). **BigQuery**: region-qualified `INFORMATION_SCHEMA.JOBS_BY_PROJECT`, `job_type = 'QUERY'`, `state = 'DONE'`, same bounded window/cap. **Databricks**: `system.query.history` (named directly in AT-12's own module docstring as the intended source). All three map to `query_history_miner.WarehouseQueryLogEntry(query_id, sql_text, executed_at)` and read only the query's own SQL text and metadata -- never the rows or bytes that query itself returned. **INV-6 without a separate redaction pass**: entries are handed in-memory, directly, to `mine_and_land_query_history_candidates` and the caller must never persist `entry.sql_text` verbatim to any table -- only `query_id` and the value-free `QueryStructure` it derives are allowed to land, exactly as AT-12's own candidates already do (`sample_query_ids`, never SQL text). This needs its own gap-proof test in the `test_inv6_value_freedom.py` sentinel-scan style, not just a docstring warning. **Exit**: a new certification check (module 02 §11's six checks become seven) proves one live, end-to-end call into `mine_and_land_query_history_candidates` without error; `query_history` flips from `False` to `True` (`connectors/snowflake.py:775`, `connectors/databricks.py:328`) only on that certification passing, per module 02 §11 ("capability flags are derived from certification results, never hand-declared") -- never by hand-editing the default. Wiring an HTTP-triggered mining run (AT-12's own honest gap #3) is a separate follow-on, out of this row's scope. Per ADR-0023: this is a deterministic job, not an agent -- no model in this path; its guardrail tier is bounded scope + evidence + INV-9 certification, not the confidence-gate/maker-checker tier that applies to generative producers. |

| IN-2 | Batch pause/cancel/replay controls | 03 | A | P1 | DONE | — | Landed 2026-09-01. Operator `pause`/`resume`/`cancel`/`replay` for a `MetadataIngestionBatch` as real HTTP endpoints (`POST /v1/.../batches/{id}/{pause,resume,cancel,replay}`) on the existing ingestion router, with a bounded state machine (new states `PAUSED`, `CANCELLED`; illegal transitions → 409). Pause: QUEUED/RUNNING/PROCESSING→PAUSED (idempotent); cancel: any non-terminal→CANCELLED (also cancels the in-flight analysis run); resume: PAUSED→fresh QUEUED run+workflow; replay: FAILED/SUBMISSION_FAILED/CANCELLED→fresh run+workflow (COMPLETED refused with 409 — chunk payloads are physically cleared on success per §7.8, an honest, code-accurate deviation). Each action mirrors `finalize`'s role deps + tenant-scoped `_load_batch(for_update=True)`, `record_audit` in-txn (INV-7), and a `record_outbox` event (4 new `metadata.ingestion.batch.{paused,cancelled,resumed,replayed}.v1` events, documented in `Docs/30-contracts/04-event-catalog.md`). Cooperative worker: `batch_ingestion.py`'s activity re-reads batch status at entry and between chunks and returns cleanly on PAUSED/CANCELLED (no FAILED, no retry, no partial FULL reconciliation); `_mark_batch_failed` no longer overrides an operator stop. Verified: `tests/test_in2_batch_controls.py` **39 passed** (every legal + illegal transition→409, audit row per action, cross-tenant denial, wrong-role denial, cooperative abort on CANCELLED against the real activity over in-memory SQLite). Endpoints auto-swept green by the data-driven gates (INV-5 tenant isolation, AU-7 RBAC, INV-7 attributability, reachability, OpenAPI diff — all pass; baseline regenerated additive-only, 4 new paths). ruff/mypy/lint-imports clean; no migration needed (states are values in the existing `String(30)` column). Honest gap: the live-Temporal signal path (operator cancels while a real worker is mid-flight) is proven by the cooperative status-check logic, not by a running Temporal worker (standing sandbox limit, AU-2 exception b). Delivered by a worktree subagent; integrated + re-verified by the orchestrator |

| IN-5e | `source_description` on `metadata_column` | 04 | B | P2 | DONE | Claude (worktree claude/vibrant-mclaren-recovered, claimed 2026-09-01) | Delivered 2026-09-01, exactly per exit criteria. `MetadataColumn.source_description` (Text, nullable) added, mirroring `MetadataTable.source_description`. Migration `c8fcafc5856a`: backfills the new column from every ACTIVE `metadata_object_description` row with `object_type = 'COLUMN'`, deletes those rows (ACTIVE and DEPRECATED), drops `column_id`/its FK/unique/index from `metadata_object_description`, and recreates `object_type_is_describable`/`exactly_one_subject` without the COLUMN case; `DESCRIBABLE_OBJECT_TYPES` narrowed to `('CATALOG', 'SCHEMA')`. Write path: `workflows/activities.py::_get_or_create_column` (native pull, and the shared 1.0 pass every push-envelope ingestion also runs) now sets `source_description` directly, same as tables already do. This made the *separate* 1.1-envelope-extensions column-description path in `ingestion.py::persist_envelope_extensions` (which used to write `object_type="COLUMN"` rows into `metadata_object_description`) genuinely dead code -- by the time that pass runs, `persist_discovery_snapshot`'s 1.0 pass (which always runs first, per that function's own docstring, and reads the identical `discovered.source_description` field) has already written the same value directly onto the column. Removed that dead branch and narrowed `_table_carries_extensions` to stop gating on it. `MetadataColumnRead` gained `source_description: str | None = None` so the column isn't immediately write-only again the way the old row-based mechanism always was (zero consumers ever read `metadata_object_description` for a column in `src/aida` -- verified by grep before this row started). `tests/test_envelope_v11.py` updated (two count assertions that counted the now-removed column-description write, `described == {"CATALOG","SCHEMA"}` not `{...,"COLUMN"}`) plus a new direct assertion that `MetadataColumn.source_description` is actually populated end to end. `pytest tests/test_envelope_v11.py tests/test_catalog_pagination.py tests/test_catalog_bulk_actions.py tests/test_catalog_bulk_actions_endpoints.py tests/test_inv1_single_authoritative_store.py tests/test_inv6_value_freedom.py tests/test_profiling_dag_wiring.py tests/test_profiling_exception_policy.py tests/test_connectors.py tests/test_connectors_oracle.py tests/test_connectors_snowflake.py tests/test_connectors_bigquery.py`: 240 passed. `ruff check` clean; `mypy --strict` clean (0 new errors; the pre-existing 15 Temporal-decorator `"object" not callable` findings in `activities.py`, none on lines this row touched). Offline `alembic upgrade/downgrade --sql` verified both directions produce the expected DDL; no live Postgres in this sandbox to run it for real (same standing limitation as CN-1c/CN-2a/DQ-4/QG-5/QG-6/MG-3/GL-6 above). Found and left alone, unrelated: the committed OpenAPI baseline already had one stale, unrelated diff (`ValidationError.ctx`/`.input`, a pydantic-version artifact) before this row touched anything -- `MetadataColumnRead`'s new field produces no OpenAPI diff at all, since `list_columns`'s `CursorPage` response model does not register that class as a named component in the spec in the first place |
| IN-5f | Fold the three later connectors onto the shared 1.1 assembly helpers | 02 | B | P2 | DONE | Claude (worktree feature/snowflake-dbt-lineage-mcp, claimed 2026-09-01) | Oracle/Snowflake/BigQuery's envelope-1.1 axes now go through `connectors/discovery.py`'s shared `apply_table_descriptions`/`apply_column_descriptions`/`apply_view_definitions` (plus a new small shared `view_definition_row` adapter) on the `TableMap` before one `assemble_catalog(...)` call, replacing each connector's ~90-130 line hand-rolled walk-and-`replace()` rebuild of the frozen catalog tree (old `_apply_envelope`/`_assemble_snowflake_catalog`). Grants fold onto `build_grants` for Oracle and Snowflake (BigQuery has none). Routines stay local (`_envelope_routines`/`_build_routine`, unchanged) and are attached via `assemble_catalog`'s `routines=` kwarg rather than through `build_routines`: all three connectors set `DiscoveredRoutine.attributes` (Oracle's wrapped/packaged-subprogram flags, Snowflake's argument_signature/is_secure, BigQuery's routine_body) which `build_routines` has no parameter for, and Snowflake's routines have no `specific_name`/overload discriminator at all (arguments are parsed from one signature string, not row-grouped) so forcing them through `build_routines`'s `(routine_schema, specific_name)` parameter grouping would silently cross-attach parameters between same-named overloads — a real per-dialect blocker, left local and documented rather than silently unified. One incidental behavior difference caught and fixed during the fold: Oracle's local `_optional_text` collapsed whitespace-only ALL_TAB_COMMENTS/ALL_COL_COMMENTS text to `None`, but the shared `apply_table_descriptions`/`apply_column_descriptions` don't blank-collapse on their own — caught by `test_a_blank_comment_is_normalized_to_absent`, fixed by normalizing before the row reaches the shared helper. Also found and *deliberately preserved rather than fixed*: all three connectors' old rebuild only ever walked schemas derived from the table query, so a schema holding only routines/grants/a schema comment and zero tables was silently dropped — `assemble_catalog`'s own routines=/grants=/schema_descriptions= contract would correctly synthesize such a schema (per its own docstring and `test_a_schema_with_only_routines_survives_assembly`), so each connector now explicitly filters those dicts back down to schemas already in the table map to keep this a pure dedup; a follow-up task was queued to fix that gap on its own. Verification: `tests/test_connectors_oracle.py` + `tests/test_connectors_snowflake.py` + `tests/test_connectors_bigquery.py` + `tests/test_connectors.py` (discovery helpers) — 125 passed before and after, identical count and zero diffs in outcome. `ruff check`/`mypy` clean on all four touched files (`connectors/oracle.py`, `connectors/snowflake.py`, `connectors/bigquery.py`, `connectors/discovery.py`); `lint-imports` 7 kept, 0 broken. Full repo `pytest tests/` run clean apart from one pre-existing, `git stash`-confirmed-unrelated failure (`test_config.py::test_environment_must_be_explicit_outside_tests`, fails identically on unmodified `HEAD`). `test_doc_claims.py` clean (1877 passed, 5 skipped). |
| IN-5g | Oracle/Snowflake/BigQuery: surface schemas known only through a routine, grant or comment | 02 | B | P2 | DONE | Claude (worktree claude/vibrant-mclaren-recovered, claimed 2026-09-01) | Real INV-9 gap, not a IN-5f dedup regression: IN-5f's fold onto the shared 1.1 helpers deliberately kept each connector's pre-existing `if schema_name in tables` (Oracle/BigQuery)/`if schema_name in table_map` (Snowflake) filter on its routines/grants/schema_descriptions dicts, reproducing the behavior of each connector's *original* rebuild-based assembly (which only ever walked schemas derived from the table query) rather than changing it silently. That original behavior was itself the gap: a schema holding only a stored routine, only a grant, or only its own schema-level comment, with zero tables, has always been invisible to discovery on these three sources, even though `assemble_catalog` has always unioned schema names across every 1.1 axis for exactly this case (its own docstring; `test_a_schema_with_only_routines_survives_assembly` in `tests/test_connectors.py`) and postgres/sqlserver already get it right by passing `routines=`/`grants=`/`schema_descriptions=` unfiltered. Fix: removed all three filters (two in `oracle.py`, two in `bigquery.py`, three in `snowflake.py`) so the dicts built from each connector's own envelope rows reach `assemble_catalog` unfiltered, same as postgres/sqlserver. Added 7 reproduction/regression tests (2 Oracle, 2 BigQuery, 3 Snowflake — one per axis each connector supports) that assert a schema built with zero table rows but a routine/grant/comment row still appears in the assembled catalog with an empty `tables` tuple; all 7 failed before the fix (confirming the gap) and pass after. `tests/test_connectors_oracle.py` + `tests/test_connectors_snowflake.py` + `tests/test_connectors_bigquery.py` + `tests/test_connectors.py`: 125 passed before, 132 passed after (the 7 new tests), zero other diffs in outcome. `ruff check`/`mypy --strict` clean on all six touched files. Note on delivery history: this fix was implemented and verified twice before landing here — once in worktree `claude/vibrant-mclaren-93e006` (lost when a concurrent merge onto that branch discarded its uncommitted state), once redone in that same worktree moments later (lost when the worktree itself was deleted mid-session by an external process, along with its git-worktree registration). Recovered by creating a fresh worktree from `claude/vibrant-mclaren-93e006`'s last surviving commit (`e9386de`, still reachable in the object database after the branch ref itself was gone) and redoing the fix a third time, this time committed immediately rather than left as uncommitted working-tree state. |

Completed implementation is retained; only the successor remainder is in scope. **Historical acceptance/evidence:** Implemented 2026-08-30, but still IN PROGRESS because the live Temporal scale drill is open. `plan_profile_tasks` is keyset-paginated (bounded page, fetch-N+1 to avoid the exact-fit-final-page off-by-one), `DatasourceDiscoveryWorkflow` loops pages and calls `workflow.continue_as_new` once a bounded table count is processed in the current execution, carrying forward only a compact `ProfilingProgress` checkpoint (never a table-id list), and `profile_max_tables_per_run` is raised to 1,000,000. Docker's default 64 MiB `/dev/shm` first caused a `DiskFullError`; the harness now sets `shm_size: 1gb`, preserving the source volume. The corrected run remained CPU-active at ~1 GiB for 20 minutes, exceeded `discover_datasource`'s 20-minute limit with zero snapshot objects, and Temporal began retry 2; it was then cancelled to avoid four identical retries. This exposes the remaining blocker: `PostgresConnector.discover()` materializes the full source graph before the workflow's paginated planning/continue-as-new loop begins. The literal 1M proof remains open; the initial discovery must become bounded/paginated before it can be attempted honestly. **Bounded-discovery fix landed 2026-09-02** (this is the blocker the paragraph above names): `Connector.discover_streaming` (`connectors/base.py:274`) is a new, non-abstract method on the connector interface -- every connector but Postgres inherits the default, which just wraps its existing `discover()` as one batch, zero behaviour change (`test_connectors_bigquery.py`, `test_connectors_databricks.py`, `test_connectors_oracle.py`, `test_connectors_snowflake.py` all still pass unmodified). `PostgresConnector.discover_streaming` (`connectors/postgres.py:786`) is the real implementation: it pages a lightweight (schema, table) roster in batches of the new `Settings.discovery_stream_batch_size` (default 500, `atlas/platform/config.py:170`) and re-issues each of the ~10 per-axis queries (columns, materialized-view columns, constraints, view definitions, table/column comments, grants, indexes, partition keys, partitions) scoped to just that batch's tables via asyncpg-bound positional parameters, reusing the same `connectors/discovery.py` row-assembly helpers per batch rather than once over the whole source; catalog-scoped axes (routines, schema/catalog comments) are fetched once and attached to the first batch only. `discover_datasource` (`workflows/activities.py:923`) now drives `connector.discover_streaming()` and calls `persist_discovery_snapshot`/`persist_envelope_extensions` once per batch with `deprecate_missing=False` and a `SnapshotScope`/`EnvelopeScope` accumulator threaded across the whole stream, session committed after every batch -- reusing exactly the accumulator pattern `batch_ingestion.py`'s `_process_chunk`/`_complete_batch` (lines 166/224) already established for the push-ingestion chunked path (INV-11), rather than inventing a second one. The single FULL-mode deprecate-missing pass now runs exactly once, after the complete stream is exhausted, against the fully accumulated scope -- not per batch, which would have wrongly tombstoned every table outside whichever batch was current. Verified: `ruff check`/`mypy src` (strict) clean on all four touched files; `test_connectors.py`, `test_discovery_continue_as_new.py`, `test_continue_as_new_checkpoint.py`, `test_profiling_dag_wiring.py`, `test_envelope_v11.py`, `test_high_stakes_behaviors.py`, `test_index_partition_discovery.py`, `test_index_partition_inventory.py`, `test_ingestion.py`, `test_postgres_version_fixtures.py`, `test_rename_and_cross_source_resolution.py`, `test_task_tracking.py`, `test_profile_plan_pagination.py`, `test_reachability_gate.py` and `test_inv9_capability_honesty.py` all still pass unmodified -- all against in-memory SQLite/mocked fixtures, this repo's established convention for connector/workflow-activity tests (no live Postgres reachable from this session either). New `tests/test_discover_datasource_streaming.py` drives the real `discover_datasource` activity body (via `temporalio.testing.ActivityEnvironment`, against a real in-memory SQLite `AnalysisRun`/`MetadataTable` tree) through a fake `Connector.discover_streaming` implementation and proves the two things this fix is actually for: a batch already landed stays committed after a later batch fails mid-stream (and the pre-existing FULL-mode deprecate-missing pass does not fire at all against that partial scope), and once the full stream lands, deprecate-missing fires exactly once (call-counted) without wrongly deprecating a table committed in the *first* batch just because it landed long before the last one arrived. What is NOT verified: the actual `_COLUMN_BATCH_SQL`/etc. queries have never executed against a real Postgres server -- this session has no live-DB/Docker path, the same limitation every prior PR-5 entry and `scripts/scale_harness/README.md` already states -- so the literal rerun of the 2026-09-02 100K-table drill (`scripts/scale_harness/README.md` Phase C) against this bounded/paginated path is still the open item. PR-5 stays IN PROGRESS. Next command for the user: re-run `scripts/scale_harness/README.md`'s Phase C (`docker compose -f compose.yaml -f scripts/scale_harness/compose.pr5-env.override.yml up -d`, then re-trigger an analysis-run against the already-standing 100K-table source from Phase B) and confirm `discover_datasource` now commits partial snapshot objects incrementally, batch by batch, instead of exceeding the 20-minute activity timeout with zero rows ever persisted. |

| SM-1 | Governed dimension authoring | 07 | B | P1 | MERGED | Claude (worktree feature/snowflake-dbt-lineage-mcp, claimed 2026-09-01) | 2026-09-11: continue only in R11-C12 (section P below); previous status: BLOCKED. Completed implementation is retained; only the successor remainder is in scope. **Historical acceptance/evidence:** 2026-09-01: investigated before writing any authoring/service code, per this session's own instruction not to fabricate a workaround. No `SemanticDimension`/`SemanticDimensionVersion`/`SemanticDimensionProposal`-shaped model exists anywhere in `models.py` (grepped `class Semantic` and `-i dimension` across the whole file) — the only "dimension" surface today is `SemanticMetricVersion.allowed_dimension_column_ids`, a plain JSON list of raw `metadata_column` UUIDs a metric may be grouped by, not a governed, versioned, independently-authored business object with its own identity/slug, name, description, source binding and maker-checker lifecycle. `TermSemanticBinding`'s own docstring (models.py ~L4197) says this in as many words: "a future governed-dimension type from SM-1 binds the same way without a schema change" — i.e. as of today's trunk, that type still does not exist. Confirmed no dimension endpoints exist either (`semantic_api.py` has `create_metric_version`/`list_metric_versions`/metric-glossary-binding routes but nothing dimension-shaped) and no `SemanticDimension*` symbols anywhere under `src/atlas/modules/**`. The metric pattern this row must mirror (`SemanticMetric` id/slug row + `SemanticMetricVersion` immutable versioned content row + `SemanticMetricProposal` evidence/maker-checker row, all in `models.py`) has no dimension counterpart, and adding one is a `models.py` + `schemas.py` + Alembic migration change — out of bounds for this worktree's hard constraints. Declining the workaround this row's own brief warns against: stuffing a dimension definition into an existing generic JSON field on an unrelated table (e.g. `SemanticMetricVersion.allowed_dimension_column_ids`, or `CatalogBulkActionRun.parameters`) would not give a dimension its own identity, its own version history, or its own maker-checker review record — it would be a fabricated signal wearing this row's exit criterion, not a working implementation of it. No code changed. Needs, precisely: (1) `SemanticDimension` (id, organization_id, project_id, slug — mirrors `SemanticMetric` exactly), (2) `SemanticDimensionVersion` (semantic_model_version_id FK, dimension_id FK, version, status DRAFT/PUBLISHED/etc, name, description, source_table_id, source_column_id, fingerprint, created_by — mirrors `SemanticMetricVersion`), (3) `SemanticDimensionProposal` or equivalent maker-checker entry path into the shared `governance_review` queue (mirrors `SemanticMetricProposal` + `metric_suggestion_service.apply_metric_suggestion_proposal`/`semantic_api.decide_governance_review`'s dispatch). A future session with `models.py`/`schemas.py` write access can build the service/API layer in an afternoon once these three tables exist — the authoring/versioning/review logic is a near-verbatim port of `metric_suggestion_service.py` + `semantic_api.py`'s metric-version endpoints. See `Docs/60-delivery/06-accomplishment-log.md`'s 2026-09-01 SM-1 entry |

Create/list (both directions)/delete endpoints in `semantic_api.py`. Wired into `retrieval.py::hybrid_retrieve`: an ACTIVE binding folds the bound term's definition/synonyms into the semantic metric's retrievable/rankable text, and a glossary-term hit surfaces its bound semantic objects; a binding that never activated does not participate — proven against a real hybrid_retrieve call over a real in-memory database, not just a DB row check. **2026-08-31**: that proof was already real, but `hybrid_retrieve` itself was not reachable from any live path at the time (RT-1/RT-2/RT-3/RT-9's gap, §N) — `agent_intelligence.GovernedRetriever`, the class the real orchestrator calls, ran its own separate lexical scan that never read `TermSemanticBinding` at all. Now that `GovernedRetriever.retrieve()` delegates to `retrieval.py` (`agent_intelligence.py:101`), SM-2's binding-participates-in-retrieval behaviour is live end-to-end through `GovernedAgentOrchestrator.run()`, not just through a direct `hybrid_retrieve` call |
| SM-3 | Confidence calibration + bank-domain corpus | 07 | D | P1 | DONE | — | Delivered 2026-09-01. AG-8's exact pattern: a committed, labelled corpus + a script that runs it through the REAL scoring function + published real numbers. `tests/fixtures/confidence_calibration_corpus/bank_domain_metric_corpus.json` -- 28 hand-authored bank-domain (table, column) cases (14 true positives / 14 false positives), every case a numeric column with an EXACT/SUFFIX `MEASURE_KEYWORDS` match, the same gate `metric_suggestion_api.generate_metric_suggestions` applies before it ever calls `score_evidence`, so every case is one the real production pipeline would actually score. False positives are real bank-domain ambiguity the algorithm has no signal for: pre-aggregated/cumulative balances (`avg_daily_balance`, `running_balance`, `closing_balance`), per-unit rates (`unit_cost`, `weighted_avg_cost`), policy thresholds (`minimum_balance`, `balance_limit`), and precomputed `*_count` columns where the keyword's fixed COUNT aggregation is systematically wrong. `scripts/confidence_calibration_benchmark.py` reconstructs each case into a real `aida.metric_suggestion_service.MetricEvidence` (re-deriving keyword/match-kind via the real `match_measure_keyword`, never trusting a hand-typed value) and scores it with the real, unmodified `score_evidence` (SM-4) -- never a mock or reimplementation -- buckets by confidence into fixed-width 0.1 reliability-diagram bins, and computes Expected Calibration Error and Brier score. **Measured with real numbers** (`Docs/90-reference/confidence-calibration-results.md`/`.json`): ECE **0.2722**, Brier **0.2184** (worse than the 0.25 an uninformative constant-0.5 predictor scores on this balanced corpus) -- `score_evidence` is measurably not calibrated as a probability of correctness; the miscalibration concentrates exactly where the score has no signal (aggregation correctness), not as random noise -- e.g. `txn_count`/`daily_fraud_alert_count`/`daily_active_user_count`, each with the two richest evidence signals the function has (bound glossary term + description mention), score 0.7542 while being wrong, ahead of genuinely-correct but evidence-sparse `deposit_amount_true` (0.5625). Needs **no live infrastructure** -- `score_evidence` is a pure function, so unlike AG-8 there is no framework-only section; every number is a full real result. Found and fixed one real bug in the harness itself while building it: naive `int(confidence / 0.1)` bucketing misfiles a confidence of exactly 0.6 into the wrong bucket (`0.6 / 0.1 == 5.999999999999999` in IEEE 754 double precision) -- fixed with an epsilon nudge, caught by its own test. 19 tests (`tests/test_confidence_calibration_benchmark.py`): pure bucketing/ECE/Brier logic against hand-computed expected values, the float-boundary bug proven fixed, corpus-integrity checks (`build_evidence` refusing a non-numeric/no-match/CONTAINS-only case a production run would never score), the real corpus run end-to-end through the real `score_evidence`, a named-case spot check (not just an aggregate), and a deterministic CLI round trip. Same ground as `TS-10` ("Labelled semantic/relationship corpus — Calibration published"), still TODO and unclaimed at time of writing -- not claimed or closed here to avoid touching a row this session has no ownership of, but its exit criterion is fully satisfied by this row's artifacts; a future session closing TS-10 can point to this row rather than duplicating the corpus/script. Out of scope: `semantic_inference.infer_table_semantics`'s own `confidence` field (a coarse binary 0.82/0.66 choice, not gradated enough for a reliability diagram) and SM-1 (dimension authoring, not yet built) -- named plainly in the report, not silently skipped. `ruff check`/`mypy src` clean; `tests/test_doc_claims.py` green |

A table with no declared/approved relationship to the rest of the set raises `UnjoinableTablesError` (422) rather than guessing a join key. | LN-8 | Large-DAG virtualization | 09/21 | C | P1 | DONE | — | Delivered 2026-09-01. `ui/scripts/graph-engine.js` (`AtlasGraph`, shared by Knowledge graph, Transformations DAG, Unified lineage, and the AI dependency graph) wraps Cytoscape.js: nodes/edges already draw on `<canvas>` (cheap at any size), but every node's rich HTML card was mounted unconditionally via `cytoscape-node-html-label`, with no notion of viewport -- at `unified_lineage_api.py`'s full-graph route (`node_limit` up to 4,000, `edge_limit` up to 20,000), and even on first load since `runLayout()` fits the whole graph into view by default. Non-windowed nodes fall back to a uniform, DOM-free canvas placeholder rectangle -- same position, no clustering/aggregation/simplification at the time this row was delivered (KG-3, delivered later the same day, added that layer on top -- see KG-3's own row). | KG-3 | Level-of-detail rendering | 10/21 | C | P1 | DONE | — | Delivered 2026-09-01. Composes with (not duplicates) LN-8's `node[agWindowed]` HTML-card windowing in the same shared `ui/scripts/graph-engine.js` `AtlasGraph`: below a `cy.zoom()` threshold (`DEFAULT_CLUSTER_ZOOM_THRESHOLD = 0.45`), a new pure function `computeClusterView` (no Cytoscape/DOM dependency) groups nodes by a caller-derived key -- `defaultClusterKey`'s default derives it from the `qualified_name` prefix every node the API already returns carries (e.g. `unified_lineage_api.py`'s `f"{catalog.name}.{schema.name}.{table.name}"`), so no new API field is needed -- and collapses each group of `DEFAULT_CLUSTER_MIN_SIZE = 3`+ same-key nodes into one synthetic cluster node at the group's centroid, sized by `sqrt(count)`, carrying a count badge; the selected/focused node is always pinned out of clustering. `AtlasGraph._refreshClusterState()`/`_applyClusterPlan()` hide real member nodes/edges (`display: none`, never removed, so re-expanding past the threshold is instant and lossless) and add/update/remove the synthetic cluster nodes and deduplicated aggregate cluster edges, running in the same coalesced per-animation-frame pass as LN-8's `_scheduleWindowRefresh` (clustering first, then windowing). Composition with LN-8 is structural, not incidental: `_computeWindowedIds()` now only boxes up *visible* Cytoscape nodes, so a real node hidden behind an active cluster costs 0 window slots and its one cluster node costs exactly 1, regardless of how many members it represents. Pure client-side rendering only -- `unified_lineage_api.py` and `intelligence_api.py::get_knowledge_graph` untouched (verified by grep in the test below), ADR-0010's bounded/truncated contract unchanged. Proven by executing (not mocking) the clustering function: `ui/scripts/graph-engine.clustering.test.mjs` (plain Node, no npm dependency) against a synthetic 4,000-node graph (`unified_lineage_api.py`'s own full-graph `node_limit` ceiling, 100 groups of 40) asserts clustering is inactive at/above the threshold (all 4,000 nodes render individually), active below it (100 cluster nodes, each `100 < 4000`), that a group under the minimum size and a pinned node both stay individual, and that zooming back past the threshold recovers all 4,000 nodes exactly. `tests/test_ui_lineage_graph_clustering.py` (8 tests, following `tests/test_ui_accessibility.py`'s established ui/-source-assertion convention) shells out to that script plus structurally asserts the windowing-composition point and the hide-never-remove invariant. `node --check ui/scripts/graph-engine.js` clean; `AIDA_ENVIRONMENT=development uv run pytest tests/test_ui_lineage_graph_clustering.py tests/test_ui_lineage_graph_virtualization.py tests/test_ui_accessibility.py tests/test_doc_claims.py -q` clean, no regression in LN-8's own windowing test. No HTTP route added or changed, so `tests/test_openapi_diff_gate.py`/`ui-next` generated types untouched. See the 2026-09-01 KG-3 accomplishment-log entry for full detail |

| **DQ-1** | **Notification and escalation routing** | 11 | A | **P0** | DONE | — | Delivered 2026-08-30. Rules, escalation, dedup, and ITSM routing (`notification_routing.py`, `notification_api.py`). Reused directly by GL-6. Its `notification_rule`/`notification_event` tables shipped as ORM models with no migration creating them (found while integrating GL-6, which depends on `notification_rule`); fixed 2026-08-30 (`f3a8c62d9e17_notification_rule_and_event_tables.py`). 2026-09-02: the 2026-08-30 DONE was reference-only for actual *quality* incidents -- GL-6/KG-7 reused the pure engine for unowned-assets/graph-drift, but no code path ever created a `NotificationEventRecord` for a real `DataQualityIncident`, and no code path anywhere (`siem_routing.route_to_siem` included) performed the outbound HTTP call an "ITSM webhook emitter" implies; `GET /v1/notifications`/`POST /v1/notifications/{id}/acknowledge` were live endpoints with no writer. Closed in `quality_service.py`: `route_and_notify_incident` matches a newly-opened/reopened `DataQualityIncident` against the org's enabled `NotificationRuleRecord` rows and persists a real `NotificationEventRecord` per match; for `ITSM`-channel matches, `emit_itsm_webhook` performs a real `httpx` POST of `notification_routing.format_itsm_payload`'s body to a configurable, off-by-default `dq_itsm_webhook_url` (mirrors `entitlements.apply_entitlement`'s pattern), recording SENT/FAILED on the event without ever failing the analysis-run transaction. Wired into `evaluate_analysis_run` (`quality_service.py`), guarded so an organization with no notification rules configured (today's default) sees zero behavior change. 14 tests in `tests/test_dq1_itsm_webhook.py` (real in-memory sqlite + `httpx.MockTransport`, no real network) covering: default-disabled fail-closed, unconfigured-URL fail-closed, successful POST with idempotency key, HTTP-error handling, end-to-end incident-opens-triggers-webhook, webhook-failure-does-not-fail-the-run, EMAIL-channel persisted without a network call, and the no-rules-configured regression guard. `ruff check`/`mypy` clean; `lint-imports` 8/8 contracts kept. |
| **DQ-2** | **Approved watermark contracts → freshness** | 11 | A | **P0** | DONE | — | Delivered 2026-08-30. Watermark config, maker-checker, ADR-0016 freshness monitoring (`freshness.py`, `quality_api.py`). 2026-09-02: the 2026-08-30 DONE was reference-only -- "maker-checker" existed only as a status field and a reset-on-update side effect; `upsert_freshness_config` always left a config in PENDING_APPROVAL (or reset an ACTIVE one back to it) but no endpoint anywhere ever moved a config *out* of PENDING_APPROVAL, so every configured table was permanently stuck reporting AWAITING_APPROVAL, never FRESH/STALE -- confirmed by `tests/test_freshness.py`'s pre-existing coverage never exercising anything but hand-built ACTIVE configs. Closed with `POST /v1/datasources/{datasource_id}/freshness-config/{table_id}/approve` (`quality_api.py`): sets `status=ACTIVE`/`approved_by`/`approved_at`, rejects a config's own author approving it (403, same self-approval-by-proxy guard PG-4 uses), rejects re-approving an already-ACTIVE config (409). 5 tests in `tests/test_dq2_freshness_approval.py` (real in-memory sqlite) prove: a freshly-upserted config awaits approval even with a fresh watermark on file, the maker cannot approve their own config, a different principal approving genuinely activates FRESH/STALE evaluation from the real watermark, double-approval conflicts, and editing an ACTIVE config resets it to PENDING_APPROVAL (regression guard). No schema change -- reuses `FreshnessWatermarkConfig`'s existing `status`/`approved_by`/`approved_at` columns, so no new migration. `ruff check`/`mypy` clean. |

The third, `demote_in_retrieval` as a ranking factor, landed later the same day as RT-7: `retrieval.py::hybrid_retrieve_enhanced`'s Stage 4 (`retrieval.py:923`) now calls `demote_in_retrieval` per candidate against the same real incident rows, reached from the live orchestrator's actual retrieval path (`agent_intelligence.GovernedRetriever.retrieve()`), proven end-to-end in `tests/test_rt7_quality_trust_ranking.py`. (1) **Impact surfacing** (module 11 §9's fourth coupling row, never wired): `unified_lineage_api.build_unified_lineage_impact_payload` now attaches each TABLE node's `quality_state` (PASSING/STALE/UNKNOWN/INCIDENT_OPEN, reusing `catalog_read_model`'s exact batched incident/observation lookups) to `UnifiedLineageImpactNodeRead`, applied after either graph-store backend produces its result and before the Redis cache write, so a cached impact response never serves stale/missing quality state (`tests/test_unified_lineage.py`). (2) **Tool-gating fail-closed gap**: `check_tool_gate` gated only `tool_api.py::execute_tool`'s HTTP route -- `GovernedAgentOrchestrator.run`'s own `GOVERNED_TOOL` branch (the path `mcp_server.py`'s tool-call handler reaches) had no such gate, so the same tool version answered differently (and could reach the warehouse before quality was considered) depending on which surface asked for it. Added the identical `check_tool_gate` check on the tool's declared `referenced_tables`, before `render_tool_sql`, raising `AgentPolicyRejected` on BLOCK and recording `plan_evidence["tool_quality_gate"]` on WARN (`tests/test_agent_orchestrator_decision_lineage.py`). ui-next surfaces both existing wiring points that had no UI representation: `AskScreen`'s answer panel renders `plan_evidence.trust.warnings` as a visible banner (not just raw JSON) and each retrieval-evidence row shows its own `quality_trust_demotion` reason/factor; `QualityScreen`'s incident detail panel states which runtime effects an OPEN/ACKNOWLEDGED incident is currently having. `Docs/00-product/04-competitive-feature-matrix.md`'s two "○ → planned" DQ-3 rows and `Docs/20-modules/11-data-quality.md` §9/§13/§14 were still stale relative to this row and are updated alongside it. **2026-09-03 (later same day)**: closed the fifth and last coupling row, certification expiry. `quality_coupling.should_expire_certification` was a real, unit-tested pure function with zero call sites anywhere in `src/aida` — nothing ever transitioned an `AssetCertification` row because of a quality incident. New `quality_coupling.expire_sustained_incident_certifications` calls it per table, then flips each matching ACTIVE `AssetCertification.status` to `EXPIRED` (the same single-field-write shape this codebase already uses for `SUPERSEDED` — never deleted, never backdated), recording an audit row (`catalog.asset.certification_expired`) and an outbox event (`catalog.asset.certification_expired.v1`, documented in `Docs/30-contracts/04-event-catalog.md`) per expiry. Called from `quality_service.evaluate_analysis_run`, gated behind a new, off-by-default `Settings.quality_certification_expiry_enabled` (+ `quality_certification_sustained_threshold`, default 3; `src/atlas/platform/config.py`) — deliberately off by default, unlike this row's other four coupling points, because it *writes* a real governance action the first time it runs rather than only shaping a read-time response; flipping it on retroactively could decertify existing tables en masse the instant it's enabled, so that's an explicit operator decision, not a silent default. Proven against a real in-memory database in `tests/test_dq3_certification_expiry.py`: disabled-by-default leaves an ACTIVE certification untouched even though the incident still opens (regression guard, same shape as DQ-1's own disabled-by-default test); enabled expires the certification and records exactly one audit row and one outbox row with the expected reason/payload; enabled with no certification on the table is a no-op, not a fabricated row. Caught and fixed one bug surfaced by the new test: `DataQualityIncident.status`'s `mapped_column(default="OPEN")` is a flush-time default, not a construction-time one, so the new code needed an explicit `await session.flush()` before reading `incident.status` off freshly-created-but-unflushed rows — the existing DQ-1 block only flushed conditionally on `notification_rules` being non-empty, which this path doesn't share. `Docs/20-modules/11-data-quality.md` §9/§13/§14 updated again alongside it — the module doc's own long-standing "certification expiry still unwired" caveat is now false and is removed. |

| DQ-5 | Data SLA/SLO definitions | 11 | A | P1 | DONE | — | Delivered 2026-08-30. Runtime data contracts with schema drift, quality breach, and SLA enforcement (`runtime_contracts.py`, `runtime_contracts_api.py`) |
| DQ-6 | Seasonality-aware thresholds | 11 | E | P2 | DONE | — | Delivered 2026-09-01. `quality_service.evaluate_analysis_run`'s VOLUME_CHANGE control previously compared a table's row count only to its single most recent prior `TableProfile` (a `baseline_rank == 1` window function), so a table with a genuine weekly cycle (e.g. weekday ~1000 rows, weekend ~400, both normal) tripped it on every Friday->Saturday and Sunday->Monday transition. `data_quality.day_of_week_baseline` (new, pure, DB-free -- takes only a sequence of already-observed `(timestamp, row_count)` points and a timestamp) groups a table's own history by ISO weekday and returns that weekday's own mean/stdev; `evaluate_quality` gains optional `row_count_history`/`current_observed_at`/`seasonality_*` parameters and, when given enough same-weekday points, judges VOLUME_CHANGE by z-score against the table's own day-of-week baseline instead of the raw previous-value comparison, falling back automatically (same verdict as before) whenever there isn't enough same-weekday history yet or the flag is off; `evidence.threshold_strategy` (`ROLLING_PREVIOUS` vs `SEASONAL_DAY_OF_WEEK`) always records which one decided. Baseline data used: the already-persisted `TableProfile` rows every profiling scan writes (`models.py`'s `TableProfile` is immutable, `created_at`-timestamped, and no retention/pruning job was found touching it -- confirmed by grepping the whole `src/aida` tree for any `table_profile`/`TableProfile` deletion or retention code before assuming this needed new persisted state) -- genuinely usable real history, not synthetic-only; `quality_service.evaluate_analysis_run` now optionally reads each table's own bounded (120-row) history back out of the same table via one extra query, issued only when the new flag is on. Wired in behind a new, off-by-default `Settings.quality_seasonal_thresholds_enabled` (+ `quality_seasonal_min_samples`, `quality_seasonal_zscore_threshold`; `src/atlas/platform/config.py`) so both the extra query and the changed verdict are opt-in, matching this repo's established per-strategy rollout convention (e.g. AG-7's `agent_query_memory_enabled`) -- deliberately kept out of the DB-backed `DataQualityPolicy`/`DataQualityPolicyUpsert` override dict instead, since that dict's keys are asserted 1:1 against the Pydantic contract in `test_data_quality.py::test_quality_contracts_validate_bounds_and_routes`, and `schemas.py` is off-limits for this item. Feeds the exact same `DataQualityObservation`/`DataQualityIncident` creation call site DQ-1's notification routing and DQ-3's runtime coupling already consume, unchanged. **Reduced false positives, measured** (`tests/test_data_quality_seasonality.py`): on a synthetic 12-week weekly cycle (8 weeks of history, 4 held-out weeks of weekend transitions), the existing rolling-previous comparison flags 8/8 (100%) of the normal weekend transitions as VOLUME_CHANGE; the seasonal comparison flags 0/8 (0%) of the same transitions, while still correctly flagging a genuine same-weekday collapse (seasonal z-score > 3, severity CRITICAL) -- a true positive is not lost. One transition in detail: a normal ~400-row Saturday scores `volume_change_percent` ~60% against the prior Friday under the old logic (flagged, WARNING/CRITICAL) but a `seasonal_zscore` under 1.5 against its own history (not flagged, HEALTHY). `tests/test_quality_seasonality_wiring.py` proves the same effect through the real `evaluate_analysis_run` call, against an in-memory database seeded with 61 real persisted `TableProfile` rows (not a mock): flag off opens 1 VOLUME_CHANGE incident for a normal Saturday (`incidents_opened == 1`); flag on, same shape of data, opens 0 (`incidents_opened == 0`, `counts["healthy"] == 1`), with the persisted `DataQualityObservation.evidence` recording `threshold_strategy: SEASONAL_DAY_OF_WEEK` and `seasonal_sample_count >= 3` as the auditable reason. Full existing `test_data_quality.py`/`test_quality_coupling.py`/`test_quality_runtime_coupling.py`/`test_custom_quality_rules.py`/`test_rt7_quality_trust_ranking.py`/`test_dbt_quality_bridge.py` re-run green, including the exact-evidence assertions in `test_data_quality.py` (untouched by default since the flag is off and `threshold_strategy` is additive). `ruff check` and `mypy src` clean on every changed/added file. Honest gaps: only day-of-week grouping is implemented (the exit condition's "broader seasonality if the existing scan-history data supports it" -- e.g. day-of-month, holiday calendars -- is not attempted; `TableProfile.created_at` granularity and the unbounded persisted history support it, but is a larger follow-up, not needed for the weekly-cycle case this row's exit condition names); no live Postgres verification of the new query (standing sandbox limitation shared with CN-1c/CN-2a/DQ-4); the 120-row-per-table lookback bound is a deliberate cost cap, not tuned against a real production history size |

| DQ-9 | Month-end seasonal baseline (DQ-6 follow-up) | 11 | E | P2 | DONE | — | Delivered 2026-09-01. DQ-6's own "Honest gaps" note named this exact case as unattempted: its day-of-week `SeasonalBaseline` cannot see a *month-end* pattern, because a recurring month-end close spike (e.g. a reconciliation batch) lands on a different weekday every month and so is spread across several weekday buckets instead of forming one there. `data_quality.day_of_month_baseline` (new, pure, DB-free, same shape as DQ-6's `day_of_week_baseline`) groups a table's already-persisted scan history by `days_before_month_end` (`0` = a month's last calendar day, `1` = the second-to-last, ...) via `calendar.monthrange`, so a 28-day February's last day lines up with a 31-day March's — a raw day-number match would miss that. `evaluate_quality` gains `month_end_seasonality_enabled`/`month_end_window_days` parameters, additive alongside (not replacing) DQ-6's own `seasonality_enabled`: when a reading falls inside the window and has enough same-position history, the month-end baseline decides the VOLUME_CHANGE verdict (it is the more specific signal for that day); otherwise DQ-6's day-of-week baseline is used when enabled, falling back automatically to the unchanged rolling-previous comparison exactly as before whenever neither has enough history — `evidence.threshold_strategy` now also records `SEASONAL_MONTH_END` alongside DQ-6's `ROLLING_PREVIOUS`/`SEASONAL_DAY_OF_WEEK`. The z-score/percent-fallback verdict math itself was factored out of DQ-6's inline day-of-week branch into a shared `_seasonal_verdict` helper so both strategies use one tested code path instead of duplicating it. Same baseline data DQ-6 already established is genuinely usable (the already-persisted, unpruned `TableProfile` rows); `quality_service.evaluate_analysis_run` reads the *same* bounded 120-row history query DQ-6's flag already issues (`_SEASONALITY_HISTORY_LOOKBACK`, now gated on either flag being on, not just DQ-6's) — enabling both strategies together adds no second query. Wired in behind a new, off-by-default `Settings.quality_seasonal_month_end_enabled` (+ `quality_seasonal_month_end_window_days`, default 3; `src/atlas/platform/config.py`), reusing DQ-6's existing `quality_seasonal_min_samples`/`quality_seasonal_zscore_threshold` rather than adding parallel knobs — deliberately kept out of `DataQualityPolicy`/`DataQualityPolicyUpsert` for the same reason DQ-6 gave (that override dict's keys are asserted 1:1 against the Pydantic contract in `test_data_quality.py::test_quality_contracts_validate_bounds_and_routes`, and `schemas.py` is off-limits for this item). Feeds the exact same `DataQualityObservation`/`DataQualityIncident` creation call site DQ-1/DQ-3/DQ-6 already consume, unchanged. **Reduced false positives, measured** (`tests/test_data_quality_month_end_seasonality.py`, 10 tests): a synthetic table with a flat, exact ~3x month-end close spike over 5 months of history (Jan-May 2026) evaluated at each of the next 3 months' close-window entries (Jun/Jul/Aug 2026, spanning a 30-, 31-, and 31-day month) — the existing rolling-previous comparison flags 3/3 (100%) of the normal close-window entries as VOLUME_CHANGE; the month-end comparison flags 0/3 (0%) of the same entries, while still correctly flagging a genuine close-window collapse to near-zero (`seasonal_change_percent` > 90%, severity CRITICAL) and, in a separate hand-computed case, a genuine z-score-based anomaly (`seasonal_zscore` > 3) — a true positive is not lost either way. One transition in detail: June 29, 2026 (June's month-end window's first day) scores `volume_change_percent` > 100% against June 28 under the old logic (flagged) but `seasonal_change_percent` 0% against its own 5 prior month-ends (not flagged, HEALTHY). Also proves the two strategies are additive, not exclusive: with both `seasonality_enabled` and `month_end_seasonality_enabled` on, the same run's month-end-window reading takes `SEASONAL_MONTH_END` while an ordinary mid-month reading still takes DQ-6's `SEASONAL_DAY_OF_WEEK`. `tests/test_quality_month_end_wiring.py` (2 tests) proves the same effect through the real `evaluate_analysis_run` call, against an in-memory database seeded with 180 real persisted `TableProfile` rows (not a mock): flag off opens 1 VOLUME_CHANGE incident entering a normal month-end window (`incidents_opened == 1`); flag on, same shape of data, opens 0 (`incidents_opened == 0`, `counts["healthy"] == 1`), with the persisted `DataQualityObservation.evidence` recording `threshold_strategy: SEASONAL_MONTH_END` as the auditable reason. Full existing `test_data_quality.py`/`test_data_quality_seasonality.py`/`test_quality_seasonality_wiring.py`/`test_quality_coupling.py`/`test_quality_runtime_coupling.py`/`test_custom_quality_rules.py`/`test_rt7_quality_trust_ranking.py`/`test_dbt_quality_bridge.py` re-run green, including DQ-6's own exact-evidence assertions (untouched by default since both new parameters default off and `threshold_strategy`'s new value is additive). `ruff check` and `mypy src` clean on every changed/added file. So the "hybrid retrieval orchestration" clause of this row's original exit text overstated what `retrieval.py` does; that overstatement is unrelated to the reachability gap this session fixed and is called out here rather than quietly left. **2026-09-02 (Group A worktree)**: clause (2)'s gap is closed. New `retrieval.py::hybrid_retrieve_cross_source` takes a *list* of datasources and one query genuinely spans all of them: each datasource is independently policy-scoped and fused by the unchanged `hybrid_retrieve_enhanced` (so per-datasource FK/dbt/annotation boundaries are respected exactly as before), then the per-datasource fused results are merged and re-sorted by their already-computed `final_score` — a merge-and-sort over independently-fused rankings (same `fusion_method`/weights on every call, so scores are on a comparable scale), not a second joint RRF pass over the combined candidate pool; that distinction is named in the function's own docstring rather than presented as identical to a true joint fusion, which would need every signal stage rerun against the union of all datasources' candidates and was judged out of this row's scope. | AG-7 | Query memory similarity + safe adaptation | 13 | C | P1 | DONE | — | Delivered 2026-09-01. `AgentRun`/`QueryMemoryEvidence` are deliberately value-free ("raw user questions are intentionally not persisted"; `QueryExecution.normalized_sql` is literal-redacted before it is ever written, per `sql_redaction.redact_sql_literals` -- "the executable form never leaves the gateway"), so there is no question text or literal-bearing SQL to embed or replay; new `src/aida/query_memory.py` builds similarity on the structural signal that *is* already persisted and value-free: Jaccard overlap between the new question's retrieved `MetadataTable` ids (`retrieval.py`'s live pre-generation retrieval stage) and a past successful run's `QueryExecution.referenced_tables`. Version-aware: a candidate is rejected the moment its stored `semantic_version` (module 13's own "PUBLISHED SemanticModelVersion, else technical-metadata" computation) no longer matches the current run's, OR any table it referenced has `updated_at` later than the candidate run's own completion (`check_candidate_staleness`, `query_memory.py:138`) -- a dropped/renamed table counts as changed, not as absent evidence. Feedback suppression (`QueryMemoryEvidence.status == "ELIGIBLE"`, already-landed ST-05-era infra) is checked before either. Wired into `agent_orchestrator.py`'s existing `MODEL_GENERATION` branch (`agent_orchestrator.py:617-704`) as a new `generation_source="QUERY_MEMORY_ADAPTATION"` candidate path, gated behind `Settings.agent_query_memory_enabled` (default `False`): a match's redacted SQL shape is added to the *same* `structured_completion` call's payload as `query_memory_template` grounding, and the SQL the model returns reaches the identical `self.query_gateway.execute(...)` call (-> `sql_guard.validate`) every other strategy uses -- no second, parallel, or weaker validation call was added. `tool_first_rate.py` (TL-6) updated to count the new source as freeform, same reasoning as `DEVELOPMENT_OVERRIDE`. 39 tests: `tests/test_query_memory.py` (25, pure `jaccard_similarity`/`check_candidate_staleness`/`select_best_match`/`retrieved_table_ids_from_hits`, no database) plus `tests/test_agent_orchestrator_query_memory.py` (5, real in-memory-sqlite orchestrator run -- a fresh eligible match is offered and labelled; a table touched after the candidate run completed is excluded and falls back to `MODEL_GATEWAY`; no prior memory falls back cleanly; the feature-disabled default changes nothing; and the validation-bypass proof: a memory match is found and offered, the fake model returns a mutating `DELETE`, and the run is rejected with `MUTATING_OR_ADMIN_STATEMENT_FORBIDDEN` -- `generation_source` on the rejected row is still `QUERY_MEMORY_ADAPTATION`, proving the guard, not an absent match, stopped it), plus a `tool_first_rate.py` update (1 new + 1 fixed test). `ruff check` and `mypy src` clean on every touched file (255 files, `mypy src` overall). `AIDA_ENVIRONMENT=development uv run pytest tests/test_doc_claims.py` clean; `tests/test_openapi_diff_gate.py` clean (no HTTP route added or changed -- `agent_orchestrator.py` and `query_memory.py` are internal orchestration, `Settings` gained three fields, no schema/route). Full-repo `uv run pytest`: zero failures (the one failure seen when `AIDA_ENVIRONMENT=development` is exported for the *whole* suite, `test_config.py::test_environment_must_be_explicit_outside_tests`, is that test's own known sensitivity to the env var being set at all -- reproduced identically on unmodified `HEAD` via `git stash`/`stash pop`, not introduced here). A separate pre-existing `test_doc_claims.py` gap (7 failures, TL-5's own tracker/accomplishment-log entries citing several sdk/aida_tool_sdk source filenames bare rather than module-qualified, landed in commit 3a62be5 just before this row's final rebase) reproduces identically with this diff entirely reverted -- confirmed via the same stash/pop check, untouched by and out of scope for this item. No `models.py`/`schemas.py`/`platform_schemas.py`/`contracts.py` file or Alembic migration touched |

| **QG-1** | **Adversarial SQL corpus per dialect** | 16 | D | **P0** | DONE | — | Delivered 2026-08-30. 100 structured, versioned adversarial cases across postgres/tsql/oracle/snowflake/bigquery (`tests/fixtures/adversarial_sql_corpus/*.json`), each driven through the real `QueryExecutionGateway.validate()` → `_run_validation` pipeline (ST-16/N14) — no parallel checker. Found and fixed real bypasses in `sql_guard.py` rather than dropping cases: locking reads (`FOR UPDATE`/`FOR SHARE`, T-SQL `UPDLOCK`/`HOLDLOCK`/`XLOCK`/`TABLOCKX` hints) as a contention/timing side-channel; vacuous join conditions (`ON true`, `ON 1=1`) that satisfied "has an ON clause" without relating either side; table-valued sources (T-SQL `OPENQUERY`/`OPENROWSET`, Snowflake/BigQuery `TABLE(...)`) that structurally bypass the catalog allowlist; and a much larger per-dialect forbidden-function/package-prefix denylist (Postgres `dblink`*/`lo_`*/`pg_read_file` family, T-SQL `xp_cmdshell`/`sp_oa*`/linked-server functions, Oracle `DBMS_*`/`UTL_*` package prefixes wholesale rather than enumerated, Snowflake `SYSTEM$*`, BigQuery `EXTERNAL_QUERY`). "Zero bypasses" asserted, not hoped for: any case the pipeline accepts fails the test loudly by name, no skip/xfail path |
| **QG-2** | **Source-native row/column policy** | 16 | B | **P0** | DONE | — | Live-certified 2026-09-02. A disposable PostgreSQL 16 source applied the generated RLS plan, forced RLS for the table owner, returned only the scoped tenant's row, and proved re-sync idempotency. A disposable SQL Server database applied and re-applied dynamic data masking; a least-privileged reader received `xxxx`, not the raw value. The live SQL Server run found and fixed a compilation defect in conditional `DROP MASKED`: the drop now checks `mc.is_masked = 1` and uses dynamic SQL so an unmasked first sync does not fail. `tests/test_policy_native_sync.py` passes (32 tests). SQL Server RLS and PostgreSQL native column masking remain explicitly unsupported source capabilities, not unfinished QG-2 paths. |
| QG-3 | Per-LOB quotas + concurrency controller | 16 | B | P1 | DONE | — | Delivered 2026-09-01. `SecurityContext` carries no LOB dimension (confirmed absent); `DataSource.line_of_business_id` (mandatory, ADR-0018) is this platform's real per-LOB dimension for a query execution -- the same key `aida.cost_showback` already groups `QueryExecution` rows by -- so `aida.lob_concurrency.LobConcurrencyController` (new module) keys on it too. One `asyncio.Semaphore` per LOB, process-wide via `resolve_lob_concurrency_controller`'s cache-by-config-tuple (the shape `aida.security._oidc_verifiers` already uses; `QueryExecutionGateway` is built fresh per call site, so an instance-owned registry would enforce nothing). Wired into `QueryExecutionGateway.execute` (`query_gateway.py`) held only around the real dispatch to the source (`connector.execute_read_query`); a wait past the configurable bound (`Settings.query_gateway_lob_max_concurrent` default 8, `query_gateway_lob_queue_timeout_seconds` default 5.0, no new table) raises `LobConcurrencyRejected(QueryRejected)`, the same shape `AuthorizationRejected` uses -- REJECTED status, DENIED audit, distinguishable `error_class`. `tests/test_lob_concurrency.py` (3 tests, repeatably green) proves fairness at both the controller level and the real `QueryExecutionGateway.execute()` level: LOB A floods 8 concurrent requests against a quota of 2, LOB B submits 2 (its own quota) at the same moment, and LOB B's requests complete on their own bound, untouched by LOB A's queue depth, while LOB A's excess is rejected, not silently queued forever. Honest scope: in-process only, not cross-replica -- `09-deployment-topology.md` names `atlas-api` as N replicas behind a load balancer, so the platform-wide per-LOB total in production can reach (replica count x limit); `redis` is a real dependency already used for the analogous per-consumer budget problem (`aida.mcp_budget`), and a Redis-backed sibling is the named follow-up, deliberately not built here because this row's fairness had to be provable with no external services (this repo's suite runs with none) and a distributed limiter's crash-safety is not something an in-process test can prove. Per-LOB custom quota overrides (vs. one organization-wide default) is the other named follow-up, needing a persisted table this item's no-schema-change constraint does not allow. See `Docs/60-delivery/06-accomplishment-log.md`'s 2026-09-01 QG-3 entry for full verification evidence |

| PG-3 | Bulk decisions with per-item rationale | 17 | C | P0 | DONE | — | Delivered 2026-08-30. `POST /v1/governance/reviews/bulk-decision` on the unified cross-object governance review queue: explicit `review_ids` (capped at 10,000 by schema, 422 above it) or a filter selection (SQL-`LIMIT`-capped, reports `truncated=True` rather than silently dropping excess, mirroring RL-6/CT-1's cap pattern at a larger number). `rationale_by_review_id` gives each decided item its own rationale, with a shared `reason` as fallback. Both the single-item and bulk paths call the same extracted `_apply_governance_review_decision` core so they cannot drift; each bulk item runs in its own SAVEPOINT so a multi-table side-effect failure partway through one item's dispatch (several of the ~15 object-type branches call into other modules) can never leak into an item reported FAILED. Verified at real scale: 10,050 seeded PENDING reviews decided down to exactly 10,000 with `truncated=True`; SQL statement counts compared at 300 vs. 3,000 items via a real `before_cursor_execute` listener to rule out super-linear growth. Honest trade-off, not a gap: per-item dispatch stays O(n) queries by design (each item's target row lives in a different one of ~15 tables) — only the *selection* step is O(1); true O(1) dispatch would require rewriting all 15 branches away from the shared core this reuse deliberately preserves |

New `Delegation` table (org, delegator/delegate principal ids, `delegated_roles`, `[starts_at, expires_at)` window, `CheckConstraint` on ordering; migration `3683caa58f58`) with a pure engine (`delegation.py`, mirroring the now-deleted abac.py's engine/API split) and `delegation_api.py` grant/revoke/list endpoints (window capped at 180 days, rejects delegating a role the delegator doesn't itself hold; both actions audited via `AuditEvent`+`OutboxEvent`). Genuinely enforced, not inert data: `security.py::require_roles_or_delegated` is a new dependency falling back to an active-delegation check when the caller's own roles don't satisfy a role gate, widening `SecurityContext` with `active_delegation_id`/`active_delegator_principal_id`; `semantic_api.py`'s `decide_governance_review`/`bulk_decide_governance_reviews` now depend on it instead of plain `require_roles`, record `via_delegation_id` on the decision's audit row, and deny a delegate deciding something its own delegator proposed (self-approval by proxy). A real bug was found and fixed proving this end-to-end: `delegation_api.py`'s `from __future__ import annotations` broke `require_roles_or_delegated`'s runtime `is SecurityContext` type check under postponed annotations, silently binding to a poisoned test double — fixed by dropping the postponed-annotations import. 4 tests in `tests/test_delegation.py` (real in-memory DB): denied before grant, permitted and exercised for a real decision during the active window, denied again once expired, revocation immediately effective, over-broad grant rejected, self-approval-by-proxy blocked |

| ID-4 | Token revocation and replay policy | 01 | B | P0 | DONE | — | Delivered 2026-08-30. A durable `revoked_token` row keyed by `jti` (or a deterministic `sub`/`iat`/`exp` fingerprint when the issuer omits one) is checked on every OIDC request after signature/issuer/audience/expiry verification but before claims are trusted; a match or a DB error during the lookup both fail closed to the same generic 401 (INV-4: no which-check-failed leak). `POST /v1/security/tokens/revoke` takes the raw JWT (never a bare asserted identifier, so nobody can revoke — or probe — a token they don't hold); self-revocation needs no role, revoking someone else's requires `PlatformAdmin`/org-scoped `OrganizationAdmin`; every revocation audited. `prune_expired_revocations` bounds table growth, keyed off the token's own `exp` (never `revoked_at`) so it can't un-revoke a still-valid token. "Replay" scoped explicitly to reuse-after-revocation (this repo has no single-use/nonce token type to replay in the stricter sense — documented as a deliberate scoping decision, not an oversight). 13 tests, including the full `get_security_context` path with a real signed JWT. Honest gap: `prune_expired_revocations` is a tested, callable function but nothing invokes it on a schedule yet |
