| ST-A7 | Context product builder | 18/19 | A | P1 | DONE | — | Delivered 2026-09-02. A new `CONTEXT_PRODUCT` change-set item type authors module-19 context products through the existing Studio change-set -> test-gate -> impact-preview -> submit pipeline (ST-A1/ST-A2/ST-A5's pattern), producing real `ContextProduct`/`ContextProductVersion` rows through the *existing* maker-checker path rather than a parallel one. Shape validation (`validate_context_product_contract`, `studio.py`) parses the raw snapshot as a real `ContextProductDefinition` (module 19's own pydantic contract) instead of a hand-rolled dict-shape check — the same "reuse the real domain schema" upgrade ST-A4 made for TOOL items — and is wired into the CONTEXT_PRODUCT test gate (`studio_test_harness.py::_validate_context_product_item`, replacing its previous name/description/purpose/allowed_consumer_roles-only check) and exposed standalone at `POST /v1/studio/context-products/validate` for incremental validation while drafting. On `submit_change_set` (`studio_api.py`), every CONTEXT_PRODUCT item that passed the gate is materialized (`aida.studio_context_product.materialize_context_product_item`): CREATE makes a new `ContextProduct` + version 1, UPDATE makes a new DRAFT version via `based_on_version_id` off the latest, DELETE requests deprecation of the current PUBLISHED/SUPPORTED version — each by calling `context_product_api.py`'s own `apply_context_product_definition`/`replace_context_product_role_bindings`/`validate_context_product_references` (all three made public, unchanged, no reimplementation) and then creating a `GovernanceReview(object_type="CONTEXT_PRODUCT_VERSION")` row exactly like a human author's `POST .../submit`/`.../deprecate` call — approval and publication remain exclusively `decide_governance_review`'s (`_apply_governance_review_decision`, `semantic_api.py:1498`) job, not bypassed or duplicated; self-approval is rejected the same way for a Studio-originated review as any other (proven directly in tests, not just asserted). Reference existence (tables/semantic model versions/glossary terms/tool versions) is re-checked for real against the database at materialization time, since the pure test harness has none to check against — a bad reference or a duplicate `product_key` fails the whole submission with the same 404/409/422 `context_product_api.py` itself would raise, before the change set's status changes. A new `StudioContextProductMaterialization` table (migration `7e6460d905fe`, chained cleanly off `a1c7e4d2f9b3`, single alembic head confirmed) links each successful change item to the real product/version/review it produced — durable evidence, with no new columns on the shared `StudioChangeItem`/`StudioChangeSet` models — readable via `GET /v1/studio/change-sets/{id}/context-product-materializations`. Exit proven by `tests/test_studio_context_product.py` (21 tests): pure contract-validation coverage (CREATE's `product_key`/`project_id` requirements, the real pydantic contract rejecting a missing governed reference or a bad `owner_type`, UPDATE/DELETE UUID requirements); test-harness dispatch wiring; and a real in-memory-sqlite scenario proving the actual exit condition end to end — `test_create_item_materializes_a_real_draft_context_product` submits a CREATE item, asserts the resulting version is `REVIEW_REQUIRED` with a `PENDING` `GovernanceReview`, asserts a self-approval attempt is rejected with the standard 409 "maker-checker separation" error, then approves as a different principal and asserts the version flips to `PUBLISHED` — the same `decide_governance_review` path, not a shortcut; `test_update_item_creates_a_new_draft_version_based_on_the_latest` and `test_delete_item_requests_deprecation_of_the_published_version` cover the other two operations end to end (including the version actually reaching `DEPRECATED`); `test_create_with_duplicate_product_key_fails_submission`, `test_create_with_unresolvable_table_reference_fails_submission`, and `test_shape_invalid_item_never_reaches_materialization` cover the three failure modes, each asserting the change set's status was never flipped and (for the shape-invalid case) that no materialization row was written. the `TestHarnessContextProduct` class in `tests/test_studio.py` (5 cases, +1 new: `tests/test_studio.py::test_delete_with_non_uuid_object_id_fails`) updated in place to prove the harness dispatch itself still works post-upgrade; `tests/test_inv5_tenant_isolation.py`/`tests/test_inv7_attributability.py` updated with the same documented stateless-endpoint exemption ST-A4 established for `POST /v1/context-compiler/validate`, applied here to `POST /v1/studio/context-products/validate`. Full verification on this delivery: `ruff check src tests` and `mypy src` (286 files) both clean; `lint-imports` 8/8 contracts kept; `alembic heads` single head (`7e6460d905fe`); `scripts/openapi_diff.py` reports no breaking changes against the regenerated `Docs/90-reference/openapi-baseline.json` (two new additive endpoints, no version bump needed); full `pytest` suite 6740 passed, 11 skipped, 0 failed, 0 errors (6751 collected, 336.6s) — the prior in-progress session's "OTel export error at the end looked like teardown noise" read was correct, confirmed by a clean `--junitxml` run with `errors="0" failures="0"`; `tests/test_doc_claims.py` (3065 passed, 6 skipped) separately confirms every file/test path cited in this row actually exists. Honestly still open, matching ST-A8's candor: no UI authoring surface for context products exists yet, only the API path through the Studio change-set pipeline (matching every other change-item type's current state); a Studio UPDATE/DELETE item's `object_id` must be an existing `ContextProduct` UUID supplied by the caller — there is no lookup-by-`product_key` convenience for UPDATE/DELETE, only for CREATE. |

Extends the module 01 claims-to-roles pipeline (`aida.oidc.context_from_claims`) rather than duplicating it: two new `Settings` fields, `oidc_groups_claim` (configurable claim path, default `groups`, same mechanism as `oidc_roles_claim`) and `oidc_persona_mappings` (group -> persona dict, same shape as `oidc_role_mappings`), plus `oidc_default_persona`. `SecurityContext` gained a `persona: str \| None` field; `_persona_from_groups` picks the first group (in claim order) with a configured mapping, falling back to `oidc_default_persona` when set, else `None`. New `GET /v1/me` (`persona_api.py`) echoes `persona` and `identity_provider` (`Settings.identity_provider.upper()` -- module 01's own, pre-existing prod/dev gate, not a new flag) so the shell defers to the server's own mode rather than inferring one. `ui-next`'s `PersonaNav` component (`ui-next/src/components/PersonaNav.tsx`) renders three ways off that one prop: `identityProvider === "OIDC"` -> read-only persona text, no `<select>` in the DOM at all; `"DEVELOPMENT"` -> the pre-existing manual switcher, unchanged and still labelled dev-only; `null` (mode not yet known) -> nothing, fail-closed per module 01 INV-4. `App.tsx` fetches `/v1/me` on mount and wires the result straight through. Exit: `tests/test_persona_derivation.py` -- group->persona mapping, multi-group claim-order priority, unmapped-group fallback to default / to `None`, an out-of-catalog persona name in either the mapping or the default being ignored, a custom (dotted-path) groups claim, comma-separated groups, malformed-claim rejection, and the full sign-verify-derive path through a real RS256 token; plus `GET /v1/me` reporting the right `persona`/`identity_provider` pair in both modes. `ui-next/src/components/PersonaNav.test.tsx` (9 cases) and `ui-next/src/App.test.tsx` (3 cases, `/v1/me` mocked) assert the rendered DOM directly -- no `<select>`/`combobox` role present under OIDC, present under development, absent while loading -- not internal state, so a hidden-but-present control would have failed these. `uv run ruff check .` / `uv run mypy src` / `uv run lint-imports` / `uv run alembic heads` (single head, no migration -- no new tables) / `uv run pytest` all green (same one pre-existing, unrelated failure as CX-9's row: test_doc_claims.py's citation check for ST-A4's TestParameterContractDesigner test-path citation, reproduced identically on the unmodified branch). `ui-next`: added `vitest`/`@testing-library/react` (previously no UI test runner existed) -- `npm run typecheck`, `npm test` (12/12), and `npm run build` all green. OpenAPI baseline regenerated: `GET /v1/me` is a new, additive path (non-breaking). **2026-09-02 addendum (Group F):** the above covers `ui-next` only; the separate `ui/` legacy shell (module 21, this wave's own scope) had no `/v1/me` wiring at all -- persona there was a pure `localStorage` browser selector with no server derivation. `ui/scripts/features/persona-nav.js` (`resolvePersonaMode`/`loadPersonaFromIdentity`/`applyPersonaMode`) now fetches the same `GET /v1/me` on load: `identity_provider === "OIDC"` hides the `<select>` entirely and renders the persona as read-only text (`#persona-readonly`); anything else (including `/v1/me` being unreachable, e.g. not yet deployed to an environment) fails open to the pre-existing dev-only `<select>` (`#persona-switcher-dev`, unchanged behavior, still labelled dev-only) rather than locking out every persona. An out-of-catalog persona name from the server falls back to `"all"` rather than being trusted verbatim. `ui/scripts/features/persona-nav.test.mjs` (`node ui/scripts/features/persona-nav.test.mjs`, no npm dependency, following LN-8/KG-3's established ui/ test convention) proves the mode-resolution and DOM-toggle logic directly: mapped/case-varied/unmapped/null persona values, DEVELOPMENT-provider deference, and a rejected `/v1/me` call all resolve without throwing. `node --check` clean |

| UX-3 | List virtualization | 21 | A | P1 | DONE | — | UI half delivered 2026-09-02 (Group F). Backend keyset pagination is CT-2's own row (still IN PROGRESS on the literal 1M/30M certification) — this row is the client rendering half, module 21's `ui/` (legacy shell). `ui/scripts/virtual-table.js` gained a second, separate entry point, `renderRemoteTable`, alongside the pre-existing in-memory `renderTable` (unchanged, still used by every small/bounded list): three new pure functions -- `computeVisibleRange` (viewport -> row window, independent of total row count), `pagesForRange` (row window -> the small set of fixed-size server pages that cover it), `evictPages` (bounds a page cache to a fixed size, always keeping the pages currently in view) -- compose into a paint loop that fetches pages lazily on scroll and never holds more than `maxCachedPages` (default 40 x 200-row pages = 8,000 rows) resident, regardless of `totalCount`. Wired into a real view: the Audit evidence screen (`loadAudit`/`renderAudit` in `ui/app.js`) previously pulled the whole filtered audit trail into memory via `fetchAll` (capped at 10,000) before virtualizing only the DOM; it now fetches one page (200 rows) at a time against the existing `GET /v1/organizations/{id}/audit-events` (`operational_api.py`, unchanged, already offset/limit `Page`-shaped) -- the one governed record set genuinely unbounded over time. A fresh `fetchPage` closure per `loadAudit()` call (rather than one shared function reference) makes a filter change register as a new session in `renderRemoteTable`'s own identity check, clearing the stale cache instead of mixing pages fetched under different filters. Row detail no longer needs a full in-memory `state.audit` array to resolve a click -- it reuses the pre-existing generic `data-record`/`data-record-title` pane pattern (`state.semanticMetrics`'s own "Details" button already established it), embedding each row's own JSON. Proven the same way LN-8/KG-3 proved their pure windowing functions on this exact codebase: `ui/scripts/virtual-table.scale.test.mjs` (`node ui/scripts/virtual-table.scale.test.mjs`, no npm dependency) asserts the rendered row window and the set of pages needing a fetch both stay bounded at a synthetic 1,000,000-row scale regardless of scroll position, plus an end-to-end scripted scroll sweep (a fake minimal DOM, no browser) across a 1,000,000-row remote table that never grows the page cache past its bound and issues nowhere near one fetch per page (fewer than 200 fetches to sweep the full 1M-row range, vs. 5,000 real server pages). No regression: `ui/scripts/graph-engine.virtualization.test.mjs`/`graph-engine.clustering.test.mjs` (LN-8/KG-3) and every existing `renderTable` call site re-verified unchanged; `node --check` clean on every touched file |

What a purely client-side orchestrator over the *existing* synchronous endpoints can honestly deliver, it now does: `ui/scripts/features/bulk-execution.js` (`chunkIds`, `createSelectionState`, `runBulkOperation`) splits a selection of up to 10,000 items (`createSelectionState(10000)`, fails closed at the cap rather than growing past it) into chunks at the server's real 500-item cap (`CATALOG_BULK_ACTION_MAX_ITEMS`), dispatches them one at a time through a non-blocking async loop against the real `POST .../tables/bulk-{tag,classify,own,certify}` endpoints (`catalog_bulk_actions.py`, unchanged), reports progress after every chunk, and supports real cancellation checked before each chunk is issued (a chunk already in flight still completes and is honestly counted, mirroring CT-1's own partial-success contract). Honest limitation stated plainly: this is client-orchestrated non-blocking execution (the UI stays responsive and the run can be stopped), not a server-persisted async job survivable across a page reload -- the row's "background execution" is satisfied for a single-page app without the backend change this session was scoped out of. Proven the same way this codebase already proves pure UI logic (LN-8/KG-3's own convention): `ui/scripts/features/bulk-execution.test.mjs` (`node ui/scripts/features/bulk-execution.test.mjs`, no npm dependency) -- a scripted 10,000-item run against a fake `api()` issues exactly 20 chunk requests of <=500 each with no id lost or duplicated; a scripted cancellation after the 3rd chunk stops dispatching further chunks while still honestly counting the completed 1,500; a scripted mid-run chunk failure counts that chunk's items as failed and continues the remaining chunks, never aborting the whole run. `node --check` clean on every touched file |

| ID | Item | Ph | Pri | Status | Owner | Exit |
|---|---|:--:|:--:|:--:|:--:|---|

| TS-12 | Doc-claim regression test for named artefacts | 0 | P1 | DONE | — | Delivered 2026-08-30. `tests/test_doc_claims.py` checks every backtick-quoted test path/name, `src/aida` module path, and import-linter contract name cited across `Docs/` against the real repo; found and fixed 9 stale "planned, not written" invariant-test claims plus one dead file reference, and carries a self-checking `KNOWN_UNRESOLVED_CONTRACT_CITATIONS` baseline for the remaining aspirational/renamed contract-name citations |
| TS-3 | Sentinel value-leak scan | 0 | P0 | DONE | Claude (worktree claude/vibrant-mclaren-recovered, claimed 2026-09-01) | Logs closed 2026-08-30 (OB-8: `tests/test_log_scrubbing.py`, live `structlog` redaction processor — this is distinct from and additional to the in-process control-plane scan below, since neither touches rendered log output). Tables and events (audit rows, outbox payloads, persisted SQL) closed in-process by `tests/test_inv6_value_freedom.py::test_no_source_values_in_control_plane` against a fake executor — narrower than the specced fixture in the way that test's own docstring states: it proves the query-execution path is value-free, not the ingestion/profiling pipelines, which need a live source. Traces closed 2026-09-01: `tests/test_inv6_value_freedom.py::test_no_source_values_reach_exported_trace_spans` drives real `@traced` calls against a real `TracerProvider` + `InMemorySpanExporter` and scans every exported span's name/attributes/events for a sentinel. Writing the scan found a real gap, not a hypothetical one: `observability.py`'s `@traced` was the only span-attribute writer in `src/aida` (verified by grepping every `set_attribute`/`start_as_current_span`/`add_event` call site) and only ever set 3 known-safe keys (`organization_id`/`principal_id`/`correlation_id`) plus `duration_ms` — but `tracer.start_as_current_span(...)` was called with OpenTelemetry's own default `record_exception=True`, which automatically attaches a raised exception's full message and stack trace to the span. A database constraint violation naming the offending value, raised inside any `@traced` function, would have exported that value verbatim. Fixed by passing `record_exception=False` and recording only `error_class` (`type(exc).__name__`) instead — the same convention `query_gateway.py` already uses for exactly this reason, never `str(exc)`. Negative control (`test_the_trace_span_scan_would_notice_a_leak`) reproduces the pre-fix SDK-default behavior directly and confirms the scan actually catches it. `pytest tests/test_inv6_value_freedom.py tests/test_observability.py`: 65 passed. `ruff check`/`mypy --strict` clean; `from aida.main import app` still imports cleanly (379 routes) confirming the request-dispatch `@traced` call site is unaffected in its exception-propagation behavior (only what reaches the span changed) |

Scope is explicitly the CI-runner regression-gate *mechanism*, not bank-scale load/soak/spike testing -- PF-1, PF-2, PF-4, PF-7 and TS-7 remain open, infra-dependent items untouched by this. `scripts/perf_baseline.py` follows the exact ST-02/TS-4 ratchet pattern: times 4 real, already-tested, in-process hot paths (`SqlGuard.validate()` over QG-1's own adversarial SQL corpus; `policy_engine.evaluate()` over 500 policies -- PG-1's own p95<50ms scenario, reused rather than reimplemented (retargeted from `abac.evaluate()` 2026-08-31 when `abac.py` was deleted under PG-1/PG-6/PG-8/AU-11 -- `abac.py` does not exist any more); `fuse_results()`, hybrid retrieval's RRF combiner, over a synthetic 500-candidate catalog; `app.openapi()`, TS-4's own gate input, with its cache cleared each iteration), takes the median (p50, chosen over p95/p99 as more stable on a noisy shared runner) over several warmed-up iterations, and compares against a committed baseline (`Docs/90-reference/perf-baseline.json`) with a 20% regression threshold -- chosen because local run-to-run variance measured comfortably under 15% even for the slowest benchmark, leaving headroom above noise while still catching a real slowdown. Any benchmark that crosses the threshold is re-measured once before the gate fails, so a single transient CI-runner blip doesn't fail the build by itself. `--accept-baseline` regenerates the baseline deliberately, exactly like `scripts/openapi_diff.py`. Wired as a new perf-baseline job in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` alongside the existing five gates. 16 tests (`tests/test_perf_baseline_gate.py`): pure `find_regressions()` threshold/edge-case coverage, plus (per this item's own definition of done) `aida.policy_engine.evaluate` (retargeted 2026-08-31 from aida.abac.evaluate, which does not exist any more) wrapped with an artificial `time.sleep` to prove the gate actually flags a real regression in a real benchmarked function, and the unmodified path proven to pass. `ruff check .` clean, `mypy src` clean (184 files), `lint-imports` 4 contracts kept. Caveat stated plainly in the script's own docstring: the baseline reflects the wall-clock speed of the machine it was captured on (this sandbox); if real GitHub Actions runners differ meaningfully, the first live run of this job may need one deliberate `--accept-baseline` re-capture -- not yet observed on an actual Actions run (verified locally only), the same open caveat TS-4 recorded for its own job |

Six defects found post-ship (per-statement `FILTERED`/`AGGREGATED`, `SELECT *` dropped, `<UNKNOWN>` magic string, hard-coded confidence, no unique constraint, unpopulated `*_table_id`) tracked and closed separately as **AT-D2** (2026-09-01) — see that row for exit evidence |
| N3 | Procedure body parsing (T-SQL, PL/SQL first) | 8–10 | **High** | **DONE** | **Applied 2026-09-02 (Atlas Wave-2 Group I).** New `src/aida/procedure_lineage.py` — a real, procedure-aware replacement for the `_parse_sql`-under-a-different-name gap AT-D5 documented. Never hands a whole `CREATE PROCEDURE ... BEGIN ... END` body to `sqlglot.parse` in one call (proven wrong: `sqlglot` silently falls back to an opaque `Command` node at the first unsupported token and drops everything after it — pinned by `tests/test_procedure_lineage.py::test_the_generic_flat_parser_silently_truncates_the_same_body`). Instead: (1) quote/comment-aware header+`BEGIN...END` stripping (`_extract_body`, careful to never let a bare `END LOOP`/`END IF` decrement the same nesting counter a `BEGIN` opened — an early bug that silently truncated everything after the first nested loop, caught by `test_plsql_cursor_for_loop_select_source_is_extracted_as_a_real_read` failing during development); (2) quote/comment-aware `;`-splitting into top-level statement chunks; (3) regex-based control-flow *header* peeling (`IF...BEGIN`/`IF...THEN`/`ELSIF`/`WHILE...BEGIN`/`WHILE...LOOP`/`CASE...WHEN...THEN`/bare `BEGIN`/`END`/`ELSE`/cursor `FOR rec IN (SELECT...) LOOP` — the parenthesised SELECT is extracted as its own real read) off each chunk, leaving the real DML statement (if any) to parse on its own; (4) per-statement-kind extraction — SELECT/UNION/`SELECT...INTO`/INSERT (including explicit column lists, positionally mapped — a pre-existing gap in `sql_lineage_parser.py`'s `_extract_target_table`/`_extract_edges_from_select` found and worked around, not fixed in that shared file: an `INSERT INTO t (a,b) SELECT x,y` silently resolved to no target at all, and even once resolved would have named the target columns after the select list's own names rather than the explicit list)/UPDATE (including T-SQL's `UPDATE alias SET ... FROM real_table alias` shape, alias resolved through the FROM clause)/DELETE/MERGE (column-level per `WHEN MATCHED UPDATE`/`WHEN NOT MATCHED INSERT` branch — `sql_lineage_parser`'s own MERGE handling only walks the `USING` subquery, not per-branch column mapping)/CREATE. **The core invariant (INV-9/AT-C4):** dynamic SQL (`EXEC(@sql)`, `sp_executesql`, `EXECUTE IMMEDIATE`), a nested `EXEC other_proc`/`CALL other_proc(...)` (its own body not analyzed), a sqlglot parse error, and a statement shape sqlglot itself could not parse (its own `Command` fallback) each produce an explicit `UNPARSED` marker **edge** (`transformation_type="UNPARSED"`, `source_resolved=False`, `unparsed_reason` naming exactly which of `DYNAMIC_SQL`/`NESTED_PROCEDURE_CALL`/`UNSUPPORTED_STATEMENT_SHAPE`/`PARSE_ERROR`/`UNRESOLVED_CONTROL_FLOW`) — never a silent drop, and never inferred from a side-channel flag a consumer could ignore. (5) Temp-table/variable intermediate-write resolution: a write to a T-SQL `#temp`/`##temp`/`@table` variable or a `SELECT...INTO` target is tagged `is_intermediate`; a later read from that same intermediate additionally gets a synthesised *transitive* edge (`via_temp_table` set, confidence capped `PARTIAL`) linking the true upstream source straight to the real final target, iterated to a fixed point (bounded 5 passes) so a two-hop temp chain collapses too — direct hop-by-hop edges are kept alongside it, nothing is replaced. Deliberately reuses `sql_lineage_parser.py`'s own tested helpers (`_extract_edges_from_select`, `_collect_table_aliases`, `_classify_transformation`, `_extract_star_edges`, confidence rollup) rather than reimplementing them — `sql_lineage_parser.py` itself is untouched (zero edits), so AT-D2's six fixes are inherited, not at risk of regression. 15 tests in `tests/test_procedure_lineage.py`: a real T-SQL `CREATE PROCEDURE` with `SET`/`DECLARE`/`SELECT...INTO`/`IF...BEGIN...UPDATE`/`INSERT`-with-explicit-columns/temp-table transitive propagation all in one body; the AT-D5 generic-parser-truncates-silently pin; five UNPARSED-marker tests (EXEC dynamic SQL, `sp_executesql`, PL/SQL `EXECUTE IMMEDIATE`, nested `EXEC other_proc` naming the callee, an unparseable garbage statement not swallowing the real statement before it); the N12 read-only-eligibility invariant tests (a fully-resolved read-only procedure marked `is_read_only=True`, and — the one this row exists to get right — a procedure the parser gave up on is *never* mistaken for read-only just because no write statement happened to be found); PL/SQL MERGE (both WHEN branches), cursor `FOR` loop, `IF/ELSIF/ELSE`; T-SQL `UPDATE...FROM` and `DELETE`. Plus `tests/test_procedure_lineage_api.py` (8 tests, real in-memory-SQLite ORM session, no mocking) for the new routine-identity-aware persistence endpoint (`procedure_lineage_api.py`) — see that file's row below for detail, and the `AT-19`-adjacent gap it closes. `ruff check`/`mypy src` (292 files) clean; `lint-imports` 8/8 contracts kept |
| N4 | Lineage proposal / review / negative-knowledge workflow | 5 | Medium | **DONE** | Delivered 2026-09-02. | N12 | Tool generator C — procedure → tool | 4 | Medium | **DONE** | **Applied 2026-09-02 (Atlas Wave-2 Group I).** New `src/aida/procedure_tool_blueprint.py` + `src/aida/procedure_tool_api.py` (`POST /v1/projects/{project_id}/tool-blueprints/from-procedure`), following N11's exact shape: a new, standalone router (not an edit to the heavily-concurrent `tool_api.py`) that imports and reuses `tool_api._load_project_and_datasource`/`_persist_tool_version_draft` **verbatim** — the literal same objects, not a copy, so this path can never quietly diverge from N11/SM-5's maker-checker posture (INV-3/INV-10): every draft this generates is an ordinary `DRAFT` `GovernedToolVersion`, `approved_by`/`approved_at` NULL, requiring `submit_tool_for_review` and independent approval like every other tool-generator path. **Eligibility (`find_single_read_only_result_statement`)** is N3's own `walk_procedure_statements`, required to be: fully parsed (zero `UNPARSED` chunks — "no write statement found" because the parser gave up is never accepted as proof of read-only, the exact invariant this row exists to enforce, pinned by `test_an_unparsed_chunk_refuses_eligibility_even_with_zero_writes_found`), zero INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE/MERGE/CREATE statements, and exactly one standalone terminal result SELECT (zero is "nothing to expose"; more than one is refused as ambiguous — which one is "the" output). **Why the generated SQL is the procedure's own reconstructed result statement, not a fresh query (unlike N11's `SELECT <cols> FROM <view>`):** a procedure has no live, queryable, column-typed catalog surface the way a view does, so the tool's SQL *is* the parsed AST of the routine's own result statement, re-rendered — the only text this platform has for a procedure body is already literal-redacted (INV-6, envelope 1.1), so **a literal anywhere in the reconstructed statement refuses generation outright** (`'<REDACTED>'`/`<NUM>` would otherwise render as an executable-looking but silently-wrong WHERE predicate) rather than publish a tool that looks fine and returns wrong rows. Procedure `IN`/`INOUT` parameters referenced in the result statement are remapped to real `ToolParameterDefinition`s (matched by name against `MetadataRoutineParameter`, typed via `view_tool_blueprint`'s own `_PARAMETER_TYPE_BY_PHYSICAL_FAMILY`) when their type is filterable; a variable reference that cannot be mapped this way refuses generation (never left as inert unbound text in the SQL, which — unlike an unexposed *column* in N11 — would be genuinely broken SQL, not merely a filter not offered). New `ProcedureToolGenerationRecord` provenance table (own migration, see N3's row) records which routine + redacted-body hash + statement count the read-only proof was computed against, independent of whatever later happens to the tool version itself. 11 tests in `tests/test_procedure_tool_blueprint.py`: pure eligibility-gate tests (write/UNPARSED/zero-results/multiple-results each refused, exactly-one accepted), pure builder tests (literal refusal, IN-parameter mapping, unmapped-variable refusal, determinism), and one real in-memory-SQLite endpoint test proving a genuinely read-only procedure produces a `DRAFT` `GovernedToolVersion` plus its provenance row (and a second proving a procedure with a real write is refused 422 before anything is persisted — `GovernedTool` row absent afterward). **Honest gap:** this does not extend `unified_lineage_api.py`'s `PROCEDURE_DEFINITION` edges with the `transformation_reference` AT-19 documents as missing for procedures — that requires editing `unified_lineage_api.py` (heavy concurrent edit across this Wave-2 pass) and was judged out of this row's scope; `DeepProcedureLineageEdge`'s real `routine_id` FK is exactly the identity AT-19 said was missing, so a follow-on pass can close it without further schema work. `ruff check`/`mypy src` clean; `lint-imports` 8/8 kept; OpenAPI baseline regenerated (3 new paths, purely additive per `scripts/openapi_diff.py`) |

almost all of it closed by iterative sessions on `feature/snowflake-dbt-lineage-mcp`, not upfront.

items concentrate in live-infrastructure certification (bank OIDC/secret-manager/Oracle-BigQuery-
Snowflake-Databricks connectors against real accounts), timed drills (`I. Drill currency` — six
still marked **OVERDUE**: projection rebuild, PITR restore, temporal failover, credential rotation,

local/in-process, not a deployed gateway or production Postgres), and 1M-object/1000-source scale
benchmarks (Phase D). A separate cross-cutting vocabulary — the "2026-08 review items" section's

the review's own angle; 8 of its 42 rows are explicit duplicates of a row above (e.g. `C4`=`ST-11`,
`N1`=`IN-5a`, `E9`=`MG-2`) and are not double-counted here, but the other 34 are additional
standalone scope this table's totals do not include. The architecture is well ahead of the
operational evidence — which is exactly the pattern named in `50-security/04-compliance-and-evidence.md`
§7. Phase D is not a formality; it is where the product's claims become defensible.

| AT-6 | Context receipts: hash every grounding fragment at assembly; history table for `MetadataBusinessAnnotation` | 13/19 | — | P0 | DONE | — | An `AgentRun` can be replayed against exactly the content it was grounded on: every fragment digest stored on the run, `metadata_business_annotation` versioned rather than mutated in place. **Not tradeable** — history cannot be backfilled, and N10 multiplies unversioned fragments, so building this after N10 means retrofitting a hash boundary through a compiler. Decision: `Docs/review-2026-08/atlan-context/00-decisions.md` §1. **Exit evidence (2026-09-01):** `MetadataBusinessAnnotation` (`src/aida/models.py:2381`) is identity/pointer-only (no content columns); `MetadataBusinessAnnotationVersion` (`src/aida/models.py:2425`) is the append-only content table, written only by `business_annotation_versions.write_annotation_version` (`src/aida/business_annotation_versions.py:97`), which flips the prior `APPROVED` row to `SUPERSEDED` and inserts a new one, never editing content in place. `semantic_inference.apply_enrichment_proposal`'s old `else:` branch that mutated `annotation.business_name`/etc. in place on re-approval is gone — it now calls `write_annotation_version` (`src/aida/semantic_inference.py`, verified by grep: `MetadataBusinessAnnotation` has exactly one construction site in `src/`, in that function). Fragment hashing happens at the real grounding-assembly call site, `GovernedAgentOrchestrator.run()` right after `retrieval_hits` is finalized: `_compute_grounding_fragment_digests` (`src/aida/agent_orchestrator.py:146`, wired in at `:460`) computes a SHA-256 digest per retrieval hit and stores it in the new `AgentRun.grounding_fragment_digests` JSON column (`src/aida/models.py:1494`); a `BUSINESS_ANNOTATION` fragment's digest is computed from its actual `MetadataBusinessAnnotationVersion` content (`retrieval.py`'s `hybrid_retrieve` joins the current version via `business_annotation_versions.current_version_alias` and stamps `metadata["annotation_version_id"]`), and the version id is recorded alongside the digest. Replay proof: `agent_run_replay.resolve_grounding` (`src/aida/agent_run_replay.py:63`) resolves each digest back to its `MetadataBusinessAnnotationVersion` by id (regardless of current status) and recomputes the digest to verify it still matches, exposed at `GET /v1/agent-runs/{agent_run_id}/grounding-receipts` (`src/aida/api.py:3043`). Migration `f8a3c1d97e42` (`migrations/versions/f8a3c1d97e42_at6_context_receipts_and_annotation_versioning.py`) creates `metadata_business_annotation_version`, copies every existing annotation's live content forward as its version 1 `APPROVED` row, drops the content columns from `metadata_business_annotation`, and adds `agent_run.grounding_fragment_digests`; verified against `Base.metadata` by `tests/test_migration_orm_drift.py` against real Postgres (`AIDA_MIGRATION_DRIFT_TEST_DATABASE_URL`). End-to-end proof in `tests/test_at6_context_receipts.py`: `test_write_annotation_version_supersedes_instead_of_mutating`, `test_apply_enrichment_proposal_versions_on_reapproval`, `test_orchestrator_run_hashes_business_annotation_grounding_fragment`, and `test_resolve_grounding_replays_against_superseded_version_not_current_one` — the last runs a real grounded question through `GovernedAgentOrchestrator.run()`, re-approves the annotation afterward (real write path, new version, old one superseded), then asserts `resolve_grounding` still returns the *original* content with `digest_verified=True` and `current_status="SUPERSEDED"`, not the live content. Full suite (`uv run pytest -q`, foreground) exits 0 with zero `FAILED`/`ERROR` lines (this environment's pytest does not print a final summary count line even on `--collect-only`, so exit code + absence of failure lines is the check; the same command surfaced exactly 3 real `FAILED` lines before the INV-6 exemption and OpenAPI baseline were fixed, confirming the harness does report failures when present). `ruff check .` and `mypy src` (191 files) both pass clean. |
| AT-7 | Context-product version support window and consumer bindings | 19 | — | P0 | DONE | Claude (worktree claude/vibrant-mclaren-recovered, claimed 2026-09-01) | (a) **Applied 2026-09-01.** A superseded `ContextProductVersion` now enters `SUPPORTED` (its own configured `support_window_days`, `None` = until explicit retirement) instead of jumping straight to `SUPERSEDED` in the publish transaction; `_can_read_context_product_version`, `_read_context_product_resource` (the MCP `atlas://context-products/{key}/versions/{n}` URI), and `_resolve_context_product_scope` all now serve `SUPPORTED`-within-window content identically to `PUBLISHED`, while `list_context_products`/`list_context_product_versions`/MCP `resources,prompts list` stay `PUBLISHED`-only so discovery still surfaces only the current version. A retired version (`SUPERSEDED`/`DEPRECATED`, or `SUPPORTED` past its window) now returns a distinguishable signal (REST `410` naming the current version to re-pin to; MCP a `{"status":"RETIRED",...}` payload) instead of the anti-enumeration "not found" -- but only to a caller who holds a real prior `ContextProductConsumptionEdge` for that exact version (`was_previously_authorized_consumer`), not merely a matching role, so a role-eligible-but-never-consumed caller and a genuinely unauthorized caller both still get the identical anti-enumeration response. Three-way test coverage (`tests/test_context_products.py`): pinned-and-supported reads fine, pinned-and-retired-with-proof gets the distinguishable signal, pinned-and-retired-without-proof and role-ineligible both stay indistinguishable from "never existed". `DEPRECATE` maker-checker widened to accept a `SUPPORTED` version (early explicit retirement, not just `PUBLISHED`). Migration `c1a4d7e9f062`, verified zero-drift against `Base.metadata` by `tests/test_migration_orm_drift.py` against real Postgres. (b) **Delivered 2026-09-01**, per the sketch in `06-accomplishment-log.md`'s AT-7(a) entry: new `ContextProductConsumerBinding` table (migration `eaf120430212`) pins one named consumer to one specific version -- `PUT .../context-products/{id}/bindings/{consumer_id}` creates or moves it (upsert, not append-only history; rejects a version from a different product with 422), `GET .../bindings` lists them with the bound version number joined in, `DELETE .../bindings/{consumer_id}` unpins (404 if absent). Deliberately a single explicit version reference, never a percentage/weight, per the tracker's own "blind A/B splits declined" instruction. New pure `context_product_policy.resolve_bound_version` resolves what an unversioned read should serve a given consumer: their binding if it's still servable (`can_serve_pinned_version` -- PUBLISHED or SUPPORTED-within-window), else the current PUBLISHED version -- proven with 6 new tests in `tests/test_context_products.py` against a real in-memory sqlite DB (create-then-move is idempotent by unique constraint, cross-product rejection, list/delete, and both resolution branches). Found and fixed in passing: `ContextProductVersion`'s "one PUBLISHED version per product" unique index only carried `postgresql_where`, so SQLite's `Base.metadata.create_all()` (every in-memory test fixture in this codebase) compiled it as a bare `UNIQUE(product_id)`, wrongly forbidding any second version of a product from coexisting in a test regardless of status -- added the matching `sqlite_where`, zero effect on Postgres/Alembic (SQLite is never migrated). Honest gap, explicitly out of this pass's scope per the original sketch: no unversioned "give me the current one for me" read endpoint/MCP URI exists yet for `resolve_bound_version` to be wired into -- that concept ("atlas://context-products/{key}/latest") doesn't exist as a resource type at all, building it would mean replicating `get_context_product_version`'s full quality/purpose/consumption-lineage machinery for a second read path, and is separate, larger scope beyond what "a consumer-binding registry with staged rollout" itself asks for. `pytest tests/test_context_products.py tests/test_context_product_negative_knowledge.py tests/test_openapi_diff_gate.py`: 70 passed. `ruff check`/`mypy --strict` clean. OpenAPI baseline and `ui-next/src/lib/types.ts` regenerated, confirmed purely additive via `scripts/openapi_diff.py`'s own "no breaking changes" check before accepting |

| AT-9 | Scope-aware definitions and refusal on ambiguity | 07/08 | — | P1 | DONE | — | **Applied 2026-09-02.** `glossary_term` uniqueness widened from `(organization_id, term_key)` to `(organization_id, term_key, business_node_id)` (`models.py:1903`, migration `c3f7a1b9e2d4_at9_at12_scoped_glossary_terms_and_.py`) — nullable `business_node_id` (ADR-0018's business graph) stands in for an enterprise default. A plain composite constraint alone would let Postgres admit more than one enterprise-default row (NULLs are distinct in a UNIQUE constraint), so a second partial-unique index (`uq_glossary_term_org_key_enterprise_default`, `postgresql_where`/`sqlite_where`, mirroring `ContextProductVersion`'s own `uq_context_product_version_one_published` pattern) caps that slot at exactly one row per term_key — proven by `test_enterprise_default_is_capped_at_one_row` (a second NULL-scoped row raises `IntegrityError`). Resolution (`semantic_inference.resolve_scoped_glossary_term`, `semantic_inference.py:731`) reuses N9's own business-graph primitives (`business_graph.classification_scope`/`ancestor_closure`, the recursive-CTE walk over `business_node`) for most-specific-wins: a node-scoped definition applies only inside the caller's scope (the datasource's assigned nodes plus ancestors), a more specific (descendant) node beats an ancestor scoped definition, and two incomparable node-scoped definitions (neither an ancestor of the other — e.g. the datasource is scoped to two unrelated LOBs, each with its own definition) resolve `AMBIGUOUS`, carrying every tied candidate (term id, business node, definition, owner) for the caller to surface as a refusal. Wired into the real grounded-question path, not a parallel check: `agent_orchestrator._check_definition_ambiguity` (`agent_orchestrator.py:147`) runs on every distinct `GLOSSARY_TERM` retrieval hit's `term_key` right after grounding-fragment digests are computed inside `GovernedAgentOrchestrator.run()` (`agent_orchestrator.py:508`) and, on `AMBIGUOUS`, persists the run `REJECTED` with reason `AMBIGUOUS_DEFINITION` and raises the existing `AgentClarificationRequired` — the same exception `POST /datasources/{datasource_id}/agent-analyses` (`api.py:2933`, calling `orchestrator.run()` at `api.py:2950`, a live FastAPI route) already catches and maps to HTTP 409, so no route or schema change was needed for the refusal itself. Verified end-to-end against real seeded data, `tests/test_at9_scoped_definitions.py` (9 tests): `test_orchestrator_refuses_on_ambiguous_definition` runs a real `GovernedAgentOrchestrator.run()` (two `GlossaryTerm` rows, `term_key='exposure'`, scoped to two sibling `BusinessNode`s the datasource is assigned to via real `BusinessAssignment` rows, each bound to a real `SemanticMetric` via an `ACTIVE` `TermSemanticBinding` so `retrieval.hybrid_retrieve` actually surfaces both as `GLOSSARY_TERM` hits — nothing mocked) and asserts the raised message names both owners, the persisted `AgentRun.status=='REJECTED'`/`failure_reason=='AMBIGUOUS_DEFINITION'`, and exactly one `REFUSAL` `AiDecisionRecord`; `test_orchestrator_does_not_refuse_when_scope_disambiguates` proves the identical two-definition setup, scoped to only one business node, never trips the ambiguity refusal (the run still raises `AgentClarificationRequired`, but for the unrelated missing-tool-parameter reason, matching `test_at6_context_receipts.py`'s own scaffolding trick — no `REFUSAL` record with reason `AMBIGUOUS_DEFINITION` exists for that run). Direct-unit coverage of `resolve_scoped_glossary_term` proves `NOT_FOUND` (no assignment, no enterprise default), enterprise-default fallback, and most-specific-wins across a parent/child node pair. `ruff check .`, `mypy src` (287 files), `lint-imports` (8/8 contracts kept, no new violation) all clean; `Base.metadata.create_all` against sqlite confirmed schema-consistent. Honest gaps: no HTTP endpoint lets a steward set `business_node_id` on a term yet — `GlossaryTermCreate` (`schemas.py:1541`) and the existing glossary CRUD routes are untouched, so scoping a term today is a direct DB write, not an API call (adding that field is a natural, low-risk follow-up, deliberately left out here to minimize collision surface with `glossary_api.py`/`schemas.py`); the ambiguity check only considers terms the retrieval evidence itself already surfaced for the question (a `GLOSSARY_TERM` hit requires a bound semantic object, `retrieval.hybrid_retrieve`'s existing, unmodified shape), not a full-vocabulary scan of every governed term against the question text |

Completed implementation is retained; only the successor remainder is in scope. **Historical acceptance/evidence:** **Investigated 2026-09-01.** Re-checked all six producers `Docs/review-2026-08/atlan-context/03-lineage.md` §2 named as invisible to `unified_lineage_api.py`'s graph/impact traversal. **Two of the three "shipped afterwards without joining it" are already joined** — `ViewLineageEdge`/`ProcedureLineageEdge` were folded into `_build_unified_graph` as `VIEW_DEFINITION`/`PROCEDURE_DEFINITION` edges by LN-7 on 2026-08-31, before this row was ever claimed (see LN-7's tracker row and AT-19's evidence on top of it) — the row's own framing was stale, not the code. **The remaining four — gateway executed-query lineage, BI report/metric edges, AI-decision edges, consumption edges — investigated and found genuinely blocked, not deferred for convenience:** `UnifiedLineageEdgeSource` and `UnifiedLineageNodeKind` (`schemas.py`) are closed `Literal`s — proven directly, `UnifiedLineageEdgeRead(edge_source="GATEWAY_QUERY_LINEAGE", ...)` raises a pydantic `ValidationError`, it does not pass through — and every one of the four needs a *new* member on one or both Literals to be represented honestly (reusing an existing tag, e.g. tagging an AI decision `SUGGESTED_RELATIONSHIP`, would misrepresent provenance, the opposite of this row's purpose). This pass's hard constraint forbids editing `schemas.py`, so all four stay out of scope: **BI** (`BiMetricColumnEdge.matched_table_id` resolves cleanly to the catalog, but `BiReportNode`/`BiMetricNode` are not `MetadataTable` rows and have no node kind — already tracked separately as LN-11 for exactly this reason, confirmed independently by LN-7's own row); **AI-decision** (`AiDecisionRecord.source_node` is always a synthetic AI-component tag — `"governed_retriever"`, `"governed_planner"`, etc. — never a catalog entity, and the table carries no `datasource_id` at all); **consumption** (`ContextProduct`/`ContextProductVersion` are `project_id`-scoped with no `datasource_id`/`MetadataTable` link even though `table_ids` names real tables, and `ContextProductConsumptionEdge`'s other side is a bare `principal_id` string, not an asset); **gateway query lineage** (`QueryExecution.column_lineage`'s source side resolves to real tables, but its "target" is an ephemeral query result, not a catalog table, and the platform's own decision record already routes query-log-derived lineage through the *candidate* review queue — AT-12, still `TODO` — rather than asserting it directly, a different, not-yet-built mechanism this row does not build). Pinned with four new gap-proof tests in `tests/test_unified_lineage.py` (a BI gap test since replaced by R11-B13's `test_r11b13_a_bi_report_reaches_the_graph_with_its_provenance`, `test_at10_ai_decision_edges_do_not_appear_in_the_unified_graph_or_impact`, `test_at10_consumption_edges_do_not_appear_in_the_unified_graph`, `test_at10_gateway_query_lineage_does_not_appear_in_the_unified_graph`) — each seeds real rows in the producer's real table alongside a real 2-table FK graph and proves the producer's data does not surface as a graph edge, a `counts_by_source` entry, or a node, and (AI-decision) does not appear in that table's own impact traversal either; the gateway test also proves the underlying tables themselves are real, resolved nodes, isolating the gap to edges specifically. No `models.py`/`schemas.py`/`platform_schemas.py`/any `contracts.py` or Alembic migration touched. `ruff check` clean on the touched test file; `mypy src` unaffected (no `src/` file touched; pre-existing 44 "object not callable" errors confirmed present before this change, same set AT-13 already documented). `AIDA_ENVIRONMENT=development uv run pytest tests/test_unified_lineage.py -q` (21 passed) and `tests/test_doc_claims.py -q` both clean. No HTTP route added, no new `record_outbox` event type, so no OpenAPI/event-catalog changes needed. **Exit not met**: 0 of 4 remaining orphaned producers joined; all four require extending the closed `UnifiedLineageEdgeSource`/`UnifiedLineageNodeKind` Literals in `schemas.py`, which this pass's hard constraints forbid touching. Unblocking needs a session authorized to extend those Literals (a normal, precedented step — `VIEW_DEFINITION`/`PROCEDURE_DEFINITION` were added to the same Literal exactly this way for LN-7) plus, for BI/AI-decision/consumption, a `UnifiedLineageNodeKind` for their non-table side. See `Docs/60-delivery/06-accomplishment-log.md`'s 2026-09-01 AT-10 entry for full detail |

| AT-12 | Semantic mining of warehouse query history | 05/07/12 | — | P1 | MERGED | — | 2026-09-11: continue only in R11-B12 (section P below); previous status: DONE, PARTIAL. Completed implementation is retained; only the successor remainder is in scope. **Historical acceptance/evidence:** **Applied 2026-09-02.** New `src/aida/query_history_miner.py` mines value-free query-log structure (INV-6/AT-C3: joins, group-bys, filter columns -- never a filtered *value*) into bounded candidates that land in the existing maker-checker queue, never auto-authoritative -- both the join and the metric shape are proven never to reach `APPROVED` on creation (`test_mined_candidates_are_never_auto_approved`). Structure extraction (`extract_query_structure`, `query_history_miner.py`) parses one query's SQL text with `sqlglot` and walks only `exp.Column`/`exp.Table`/`exp.Join`/aggregate-function nodes -- every `exp.Literal` a filter predicate carries is walked past and never read or copied into the returned `QueryStructure`, proven by `test_extract_query_structure_never_captures_literal_values` (asserts the literal values from a seeded `WHERE` clause never appear in the extracted structure's own `repr`); an unparseable statement degrades to `None` (mirrors `sql_lineage_parser.py`'s own posture), proven by `test_extract_query_structure_degrades_gracefully_on_unparseable_sql`. Two candidate shapes: **join candidates** reuse `RelationshipCandidate` unmodified (new `detection_rule=QUERY_LOG_JOIN_V1` only -- no schema change, and no new decision route: the existing `decide_relationship_candidate` endpoint already reviews it); **metric candidates** are a new, clearly-scoped model, `QueryHistoryMetricCandidate` (`models.py:4629`, migration `c3f7a1b9e2d4_at9_at12_scoped_glossary_terms_and_.py`) -- checked first and confirmed `SemanticMetricProposal` (SM-4) cannot represent this: it requires a `source_annotation_id` pointing at an approved business annotation and carries NL-description-quality scores that do not apply to a candidate whose only evidence is recurrence in the query log. Metric candidates land in the platform's other existing maker-checker queue, `GovernanceReview` (object_type `QUERY_HISTORY_METRIC_CANDIDATE`), dispatched from `semantic_api._apply_governance_review_decision` (`semantic_api.py:2101`) exactly the way AT-11's `COLUMN_CLASSIFICATION_PROMOTION` branch is -- an independent APPROVE publishes a real `SemanticMetric`+`SemanticMetricVersion` (`apply_query_history_metric_candidate_decision`, `query_history_miner.py:706`), REJECT publishes nothing and retains the candidate as negative evidence, proven by `test_approving_a_metric_candidate_publishes_a_real_metric` / `test_rejecting_a_metric_candidate_publishes_nothing` / `test_deciding_an_already_decided_candidate_conflicts`. AT-C2 lane 3 (model judgements are proposal-only, capped at 0.70 confidence, maker != checker): every candidate's confidence is computed by `_lane3_confidence`, which cannot exceed `QUERY_HISTORY_CONFIDENCE_CAP=0.70` for any occurrence count, proven by an exhaustive sweep in `test_lane3_confidence_never_exceeds_the_cap`. Bounded output on two axes, not merely ranked: `QUERY_HISTORY_MIN_OCCURRENCES=3` drops a shape seen only once or twice as noise before ranking even considers it, and `QUERY_HISTORY_MAX_JOIN_CANDIDATES=50`/`QUERY_HISTORY_MAX_METRIC_CANDIDATES=25` cap the ranked, occurrence-sorted survivors actually landed per mining run -- the documented rationale and the same rank-then-cap shape `generate_composite_relationship_candidates` (RL-3) already uses; proven by `test_mine_join_pair_frequencies_is_bounded_and_ranked`. Re-mining the same log is idempotent (`test_mining_is_idempotent_against_the_same_log` -- no duplicate rows, dedups against existing `RelationshipCandidate` pairs, the AT-C3 negative-knowledge suppression list via `relationship_candidate_review.load_suppressed_relationship_predicate_hashes`, and existing `QueryHistoryMetricCandidate` shapes). Tests: `tests/test_at12_query_history_mining.py` (15, real in-memory-sqlite seeded data for the DB-facing layer, pure for extraction/mining). `ruff check .`, `mypy src` (287 files), `lint-imports` (8/8 contracts kept) all clean. **Per `00-decisions.md` §4, still explicitly not a lineage source**: an approved join candidate is a `SUGGESTED_RELATIONSHIP`-tier candidate in the unified lineage graph exactly like every other `RelationshipCandidate`, never an asserted `DECLARED`/`VIEW_DDL`-tier edge. **Honest gaps (why `PARTIAL`, not a clean `DONE`):** (1) no connector implements `get_query_history()` -- this module takes an already-materialized `WarehouseQueryLogEntry` sequence as input, connector-agnostic by design, so mining is real and tested end-to-end against that input contract, but nothing yet calls a live warehouse's `ACCOUNT_USAGE.QUERY_HISTORY` (Snowflake) or `system.query.history` (Databricks) to produce it; (2) per AT-D3's own instruction, Snowflake's and Databricks' `query_history` capability flags are therefore left exactly as AT-D3 left them (`False`, checked directly: `connectors/snowflake.py:775`, `connectors/databricks.py:328`) -- flipping either was explicitly out of scope until a connector genuinely and fully consumes real query history end-to-end, which this pass does not build; (3) no HTTP endpoint triggers a mining run yet (mirroring `discover_relationship_candidates`'s own admin-triggered-scan shape would be the natural follow-up) -- `mine_and_land_query_history_candidates` is tested by direct call, the same convention `tests/test_at11_classification_propagation.py` uses for `_apply_governance_review_decision`, deliberately to avoid a new route/schema/OpenAPI-baseline surface while `ST-05`/`06`/`07` split `api.py`/`schemas.py` concurrently. Per INV-9, no customer-facing material should claim query-history mining is *live against a connector* until (1)-(2) close |

| AT-D2 | `sql_lineage_parser.py` defects — reopens LN-2/N2 | 09 | — | P0 | **DONE** | — | Applied 2026-09-01. All six defects fixed in `sql_lineage_parser.py`, `dbt_column_lineage.py`, `view_lineage_api.py`, `models.py` and migration `31a73643a697`, each with a real test proving the fix (not just "existing tests still pass" — several existing tests encoded the defect as correct and were rewritten): **(1) FILTERED/AGGREGATED per-statement:** `_classify_transformation` now takes only the SELECT-list column's own expression — `has_aggregation` is evaluated per-column, never statement-wide, and WHERE-clause presence no longer overrides a column's own SELECT-list classification at all (`SELECT col_a FROM t WHERE col_a > 0` now types `col_a` `DIRECT`, correctly). A column referenced *only* in WHERE (never selected) now gets its own `FILTERED` evidence edge targeting the reserved `FILTER_EVIDENCE_TARGET_COLUMN` marker instead of producing nothing. Each select's own `.args["where"]` is read directly (not a recursive `find`), so a UNION branch's WHERE can no longer leak onto a sibling branch. Corrected the test that used to be named test_where_clause_produces_filtered_transformation (asserted the inversion as correct) — renamed to `tests/test_sql_lineage_parser.py::test_where_clause_does_not_override_a_selected_columns_own_classification` — plus four new tests. **(2) `SELECT *` dropped:** a bare/qualified star now resolves to one `TABLE_STAR` edge per source table in scope (`source_column`/`target_column` = `*`, `PARTIAL` confidence) — honest table-level evidence instead of `continue`; falls back to a `LOW`-confidence `UNRESOLVED` edge only when no source table can be identified at all. `dbt_column_lineage.extract_column_lineage` (LN-5) explicitly drops `TABLE_STAR`/`FILTERED` edges as non-column-level evidence outside its contract — LN-5's own star tests (`tests/test_dbt_column_lineage.py::test_select_star_produces_no_fabricated_edge`, `::test_mixed_star_and_explicit_columns_only_emits_the_explicit_one`) still pass, now for the right reason. **(3) `"<UNKNOWN>"` magic string:** replaced by `LineageEdge.source_resolved: bool` — a real, typed, structurally-collision-proof signal (`source_table == "UNRESOLVED"` display text is cosmetic only; a customer table genuinely named `UNRESOLVED` is still distinguishable via the boolean, proven by `test_a_real_table_actually_named_unresolved_is_still_distinguishable`). **(4) hard-coded `Confidence.FULL`:** every edge now carries confidence computed from what was actually resolved (`FULL` only when the source table resolved cleanly; `PARTIAL` for an unresolved reference, filter-only evidence, or `SELECT *`; `ParseResult.confidence` rolls up from the edges it contains) — a view and a procedure parsing equally certain SQL now agree, and one leaning on unresolved/guessed evidence is honestly lower, without an arbitrary per-entry-point rule. **(5) no unique constraint:** migration `31a73643a697` adds `uq_view_lineage_edge_natural_key`/`uq_procedure_lineage_edge_natural_key` on `(datasource_id, source_table, source_column, target_table, target_column, transformation_type)`; verified drift-free against a live local Postgres 16 via `tests/test_migration_orm_drift.py`. `view_lineage_api.py`'s persistence path changed from blind `session.add` to delete-then-insert scoped to the target table(s) a parse actually produced edges for (an empty/failed parse never wipes prior good lineage; an unrelated view's rows are untouched). **(6) `source_table_id`/`target_table_id` never populated:** new `_resolve_table_ids` looks up the parser's raw table-name strings against `MetadataTable` (case-insensitive, catalog/schema/bare-name match) and populates both FKs wherever the underlying table exists in the catalog — `_build_unified_graph` (LN-7) already filtered on both being non-NULL and got nothing; this is what makes that traversal real for the first time via the actual endpoint, not just hand-set test fixtures. New `tests/test_view_lineage_api.py` (no test file existed for this endpoint before) proves 5/6 and 6/6 end-to-end. **Known, pre-existing, un-widened limitation:** the endpoint takes only raw SQL with no procedure-identity field, so a standalone SELECT inside a procedure body buckets under the parser's shared `PROCEDURE_RESULT_TARGET` sentinel — two different procedures producing an identical standalone-SELECT edge are indistinguishable under that shared bucket; documented in `_persist_edges`'s docstring, not fixed here (no identity field to fix it with, out of AT-D2's scope). One genuinely adjacent bug found and fixed in passing (same file, near-zero blast radius): `_extract_from_statement` searched `find(exp.Select)` before `find(exp.Union)`, so a UNION at the top of a `CREATE VIEW`/`INSERT` silently matched only its first branch and dropped every other branch's edges entirely — order swapped, proven by `test_union_branch_where_clause_does_not_leak_into_sibling_branch`. `ruff check .`, `mypy src` (189 files) and the full `pytest` suite (foreground) pass, including LN-2's own suite, LN-5's (`test_dbt_column_lineage.py`, `test_dbt_column_lineage_integration.py`), and the new `test_view_lineage_api.py` |

| AT-19 | Transformation code rendered on the lineage edge | 09/21 | — | P1 | **DONE** | — | **Applied 2026-09-01.** `get_transformation_detail` (EE.10) covered only dbt-compiled SQL; it never read envelope 1.1's connector-discovered `MetadataViewDefinition`/`MetadataRoutine` at all, and the unified graph's `VIEW_DEFINITION`/`PROCEDURE_DEFINITION` edges carried no pointer back to it. ADR-0010's bounded-response contract holds: a reference id, not inlined DDL. (2) `mcp_server.py`'s `_transformation_detail` gained a fallback (`_view_definition_transformation_detail`) that resolves that reference to the actual redacted definition SQL, `redaction_status`, and stored `screening_status` (gating the text the same way the existing dbt-description live-screen does) -- proven to be the *same* fact as the edge's evidence, not two representations that could drift, by a round-trip test. FK and `PROCEDURE_DEFINITION` edges get neither field: an FK is not transformation-code-backed, and `ProcedureLineageEdge` carries no FK/specific_name/any identity back to the `MetadataRoutine` row it was parsed from (`view_lineage_api.py`'s parse endpoints take only raw SQL, no routine-identity parameter) -- documented as a genuine, un-fabricated gap in both modules rather than papered over; a real fix needs a schema change (adding routine identity to `ProcedureLineageEdge`) that AT-19's scope (no `models.py` edits) doesn't cover. Four new tests in `tests/test_unified_lineage.py`: graph-shape (VIEW_DEFINITION carries the fields, sibling FOREIGN_KEY/PROCEDURE_DEFINITION edges in the same graph do not), no-fabrication (no ingested `MetadataViewDefinition` yet -> no reference), the round-trip, and a quarantine test. No `models.py`/`schemas.py`/migration touched -- `evidence`/the MCP tool's return are both already-untyped `dict`s, extended in place. No HTTP route added, so no OpenAPI regen. `ruff check`/`mypy` clean on both touched src files; `tests/test_unified_lineage.py`, `tests/test_mcp_server.py`, `tests/test_view_lineage_api.py`, `tests/test_doc_claims.py` all pass. See `Docs/60-delivery/06-accomplishment-log.md`'s 2026-09-01 AT-19 entry |
| AT-20 | Lineage evidence export as a signed artifact | 09/20 | — | P1 | DONE | — | **Applied 2026-09-01.** New `GET /v1/datasources/{datasource_id}/unified-lineage/impact/{node_id}/export` (`aida.lineage_evidence_export_api`), composed by `aida.lineage_evidence_export.compose_lineage_export_artifact` -- point-in-time lineage for a chosen asset (`node_id`) and traversal `depth`, packaged as one downloadable JSON artifact. Reuses, never re-derives: EA.14's `build_unified_lineage_graph_payload`/`build_unified_lineage_impact_payload` for the diagram-shape node/edge set, bounded to the exact same traversal the live `.../impact/{node_id}` route runs for the same asset/depth; AT-19's per-edge `transformation_reference`/`redaction_status`/`edge_source` verbatim; AT-16's pin shape and canonicalization algorithm (`answer_provenance._canonical_json`, SHA-256 over sorted-key JSON) for `graph_version.{pinned_at, datasource_id, traversal, graph_content_fingerprint}`; `context_compiler_api`'s (EE.9) and UX-7's `Content-Disposition`/`X-Artifact-SHA256` attachment idiom for delivery. Asserting principal for human edges: `RelationshipCandidate.reviewed_by` (`models.py`, read-only) -- the steward who approved a `SUGGESTED_RELATIONSHIP` candidate through `intelligence_api`'s maker-checker decision endpoint, the only edge kind in this graph that is a human assertion rather than a mechanical read of a constraint/dbt manifest/OpenLineage event/parsed SQL; every other edge kind's `asserting_principal` is `None`, never fabricated. Same authorization as every other unified-lineage route: the export route imports `UNIFIED_LINEAGE_READER_ROLES`/`_load_datasource` directly from `unified_lineage_api.py` -- the literal same objects, not a copy, proved by identity assertion in tests, so it can never diverge into a separate/weaker export-only gate. "Signed" honestly means hash-verified integrity (SHA-256, recomputable by the recipient against `X-Artifact-SHA256`, plus the pinned `graph_content_fingerprint` independently reproducible from the artifact's own nodes/edges) -- not a cryptographic signature; no key-management infrastructure exists on this platform to back one, and this row's hard constraints forbid adding one. JSON, not PDF: `pyproject.toml` pins no PDF/diagram-rendering library, and JSON is the dependency-free choice already established twice on this branch today (EE.9, UX-7). Six new tests in `tests/test_lineage_evidence_export.py`: content fidelity against the live graph/impact routes including AT-19 evidence passthrough, the asserting-principal population (maker != checker), permission parity (same gate objects, identical cross-org 403 and unknown-node 404), and hash verification at both the artifact-bytes layer and the pinned content-fingerprint layer (including determinism across two exports of an unchanged graph). | AT-22 | Publish the parser capability matrix | 02/09 | — | P2 | **DONE** | — | **Applied 2026-09-02 (Atlas Wave-2 Group I), unblocked now that AT-D2 is done.** New `src/aida/procedure_capability_matrix.py` (`build_capability_matrix`/`render_markdown`) + `scripts/generate_procedure_capability_matrix.py`, publishing `Docs/90-reference/procedure-lineage-capability-matrix.md` (+ a `.json` sibling for programmatic consumers). The point of this row, restated in its own module docstring, is that the matrix must not itself become the "marketed vs. actual" drift this codebase criticises elsewhere — so every fact in it is *read out of the parsers' own source at generation time*, not hand-typed prose that can quietly drift: (1) **dialects** — `sql_lineage_parser._SQLGLOT_DIALECT_MAP`'s keys, read directly (both `sql_lineage_parser.py` and N3's `procedure_lineage.py` share the map); (2) **which SQL statement shapes each parser's dispatcher recognises** — `_dispatched_node_types` walks the real Python AST (via `inspect.getsource`) of `sql_lineage_parser._extract_from_statement` and `procedure_lineage._classify_and_extract` and extracts every `isinstance(node, exp.<Type>)` check each one actually makes, so a dispatch branch added or removed is picked up next regeneration with nothing to remember to update by hand; (3) **which of those shapes the procedure parser explicitly degrades on rather than extracting real lineage from** — `_explicitly_unparsed_node_types` walks the same AST and, per `isinstance` branch, checks whether that branch's own body calls `_unparsed_statement(...)` (N3's one and only path to an UNPARSED marker); (4) **control-flow/dynamic-SQL recognition** (regex-based text peeling, not an AST node type — sqlglot cannot parse a whole procedure body's control flow to begin with, see N3's row) — enumerated from `procedure_lineage`'s own live module-level `_..._RE` compiled-pattern names, itself proof a recognition rule exists in code rather than merely being claimed. 19 construct rows published (SELECT/UNION/CREATE-AS-SELECT/INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE/MERGE/EXEC-dynamic-or-nested/`sp_executesql`/unrecognised-Command-fallback, plus 10 control-flow/no-lineage/dynamic-SQL text-recognition rows), each tagged `SUPPORTED`/`EXPLICIT_UNPARSED`/`RECOGNISED_NO_LINEAGE`/`UNSUPPORTED`/`N/A`, across the 5 attempted dialects (bigquery/oracle/postgres/snowflake/tsql), plus the 5 named `UnparsedReason` degradation categories read straight off the real enum. Caught one real bug while building the AST-derived extraction itself: the `sp_executesql` branch originally used `type(node).__name__ == "ExecuteSql"` rather than `isinstance`, which the introspection (correctly) could not see — switched to `isinstance(node, exp.ExecuteSql)` in `procedure_lineage.py`, both fixing the code to match every other branch's idiom and making the matrix report it correctly as `EXPLICIT_UNPARSED` rather than `UNSUPPORTED`. Deliberately **not** derived from "the E12 certification corpus" the row's original note named — no such corpus exists yet on this branch; deriving from the dispatchers' own code is the stronger, harder-to-drift proof this row's stated purpose actually needs (a corpus proves what real-world SQL happens to hit; source introspection proves what the parser can and cannot do at all). 11 tests in `tests/test_procedure_capability_matrix.py`: determinism, dialects match the shared map exactly, INSERT is SUPPORTED by both parsers, UPDATE is procedure-only (`N/A` for the view parser), dynamic SQL is always `EXPLICIT_UNPARSED` never silently `SUPPORTED`/dropped, every row's status is one of the five valid values, unparsed reasons match the real enum, the rendered Markdown contains every row — plus three tests proving the introspection primitives themselves against small synthetic dispatcher functions, including one that changes a throwaway dispatcher and asserts the derived output changes with it (the "derived, not hand-typed" claim, pinned directly). `ruff check`/`mypy src` clean; `lint-imports` 8/8 kept. Regenerate via `AIDA_ENVIRONMENT=development uv run python scripts/generate_procedure_capability_matrix.py` after any change to either parser's dispatcher. **2026-09-02 follow-up:** CI's `AU-1` reachability gate correctly flagged that this module had zero live callers (only its own test and the standalone script) — closed by also serving the matrix live at `GET /v1/procedure-lineage/capability-matrix` (`procedure_lineage_api.py`, calling the identical `build_capability_matrix()`), so the published doc is now backed by a real, reachable code path, not only a script. See `06-accomplishment-log.md`'s same-day follow-up entry |

Ph left unassigned (`—`) pending phase-owner triage, per this file's own convention for
unassigned items. Not yet reflected in the §K summary counts.

**Correction, 2026-08-30 (later), from `Docs/Atlan-context.docx`.** A 43-screen capture of
Atlan's actual product UI shows one claim in that review was too strong. It said Atlan has
"no equivalent at all" to AI decision lineage. Atlan in fact ships a **Trace Explorer**: every
agent interaction is captured with a context-retrieval path, the semantic object and version
it resolved, and a correct/flagged status. What is absent from all 43 screens is a **refusal** —
every trace is an answer that was given, never one that was declined and the control that
declined it. So the differentiator is narrower than stated and still real: *they trace what was
used; LN-3 also records what was refused, and a refusal is a free regression test.* Positioning
that leans on "they have nothing here" is wrong and should not be used.


## M. Experience shell rebuild (2026-08-30)

Decision: `10-architecture/adr/ADR-0021-experience-shell-stack-and-strangle-migration.md`.
Plan: `40-engineering/08-experience-shell-rebuild-plan.md`. Spec: `20-modules/21-experience-shell.md`.

**Why these rows exist.** UX-1…UX-9 in §I describe *what* the shell must do; none of them
could be built on `ui/` as it stands — 3,655 lines of imperative DOM assembly with no
component boundary, no build and no tests. The rows below are the *enabling* work, plus the
one backend gap the rebuild exposed: `MetadataTableRead` returns eight fields, so a governed
catalog row costs five further requests per asset (100 rows = 501 requests). That is a
missing read model, not a defect in the API. UX-3 and UX-7 are satisfied for each screen as
it adopts the Catalog pattern, so they are not duplicated here.

| ID | Item | Mod | Ph | Pri | Status | Owner | Exit |
|---|---|:--:|:--:|:--:|:--:|:--:|---|

| UX-11 | Catalog reference screen — virtualized grid, URL-held filter/selection state, single abortable request per view, bulk selection, evidence pane with permalink | 21/04 | A | **P0** | DONE | — | Delivered 2026-08-30. 1,000,000 rows with 30–42 row elements in the DOM at any scroll depth; keyset paging on approach to the loaded window's end; `aria-rowcount`/absolute `aria-rowindex`; no console errors in either theme; no page-level horizontal scroll at 700px |

One request returns description + proposal state (folded into `description_is_proposed`, matching the client type), owner, certification, quality, glossary terms and row estimate per asset in 9 batched queries independent of page size (`row_number() OVER (PARTITION BY table_id ...)`, the same idiom `intelligence_api._latest_table_profiles`/`quality_service` already use) — not one query per row. Preserves the CT-2 `CursorPage` keyset contract including `total: null` under a cursor (reuses `aida.pagination.apply_keyset`/`decode_cursor`/`encode_cursor` verbatim). Permission-filtered by the same gate `list_tables` uses (`aida.authorization_gate.gate`, action `READ_METADATA`, resource_type `datasource`), called once per distinct datasource on the page rather than per row; a row from a denied datasource is dropped rather than failing the whole request. No writes — ADR-0004 untouched. `VITE_USE_FIXTURES` intentionally left at its default: it also gates UX-13's not-yet-built evidence endpoint, so flipping it now would 404 that call rather than just switch data sources (see `00-status.md`'s ui-next shell rebuild row). Two shared hooks (`useUrlState`, `useDatasourcePicker`) were extracted from the pattern six existing screens already carried inline, so these 8 (5 of which needed a datasource picker) reuse rather than re-copy it. **Ask** (`AskScreen.tsx`, nav `analyst`) -- `POST /v1/datasources/{id}/agent-analyses` (single-shot, non-streaming `run_agent_analysis`), `GET .../agent-runs` history, `GET /v1/agent-runs/{id}` + `.../grounding-receipts` to reopen a past run without re-asking; AT-9's ambiguous-definition 409 is parsed into a structured refusal card, not a generic error. **Relationships** (`RelationshipsScreen.tsx`) -- N4's impact-ordered review queue (`GET .../relationship-candidates/review-queue`), RL-6 single/bulk decision, RL-7 confidence calibration. **Semantics** (`SemanticsScreen.tsx`) -- honestly project-scoped, not org-wide: no aggregate "browse every semantic model" endpoint exists, so a project picker (`GET /v1/organizations/{id}/projects`, a real endpoint this row's research surfaced) feeds project-scoped model/metric lists plus UX-18's consumer footer. **Business meaning** (`BusinessMeaningScreen.tsx`, nav `meaning`) -- `GET .../business-annotations` datasource-scoped list, `GET /v1/metadata/tables/{id}/business-annotation` permalink target, `GET /v1/organizations/{id}/business-map` as a secondary org-wide taxonomy tab (a grouped tree, not LN-8's Cytoscape canvas -- that stays in `ui/` until UX-16). **Quality** (`QualityScreen.tsx`) -- `GET .../quality-summary` tiles, `GET .../quality-incidents` filterable list, `POST /v1/quality-incidents/{id}/transition` single-incident acknowledge/resolve (no bulk primitive exists server-side); its permalink resolves from the currently-loaded page, same caveat `ReviewQueueScreen` already has. **Audit ledger** (`AuditLedgerScreen.tsx`, nav `audit`) -- `GET /v1/organizations/{id}/audit-events`, org-wide, no datasource picker. **Sources** (`SourcesScreen.tsx`) -- reuses `fetchOrgDatasources`, adds `GET /v1/datasources/{id}/health` per-source. **Operations** (`OperationsScreen.tsx`) -- composed from `GET .../fleet-summary`, `.../analysis-runs`, `.../outbox-events` (+ `POST .../requeue`), and a secondary per-datasource `.../metadata-ingestion-batches` drill-down; honestly no org-wide ingestion-batch aggregate exists, so that panel stays explicitly scoped to one datasource rather than faking an unbounded org-wide fan-out. All 8 screens' `App.tsx` wiring (imports, `NAV` `ready: true`, view-selection branches) landed in one centralized follow-up PR (#30) rather than each of the 8 parallel sessions touching that shared file, avoiding a merge-conflict pile-up; that PR also fixed two of its own merge-splice bugs (a dropped fixture block, a duplicated function tail) caught by `npm run typecheck` before merging, not shipped. Tests: 35 new across the 8 screens (`AuditLedgerScreen.test.tsx` 5, `RelationshipsScreen.test.tsx` 8, `SourcesScreen.test.tsx` 6, `SemanticsScreen.test.tsx` 3, `BusinessMeaningScreen.test.tsx` 4, `QualityScreen.test.tsx` 5, `OperationsScreen.test.tsx` 4, `AskScreen.test.tsx` 5) plus `useUrlState.test.ts`/`useDatasourcePicker.test.ts` for the two extracted hooks. `cd ui-next && npm run typecheck && npm run test && npm run build` all green after every merge and after the App.tsx wiring pass (91 tests total, 19 test files, `tsc -b && vite build` clean, 82 modules). No `src/aida` file touched -- every endpoint used was already real and already merged. |

| UX-20 | Narrated lineage traversal as the primary lineage surface | 21/09 | B | P1 | DONE | — | Delivered 2026-09-02. `ui-next/src/screens/NarratedLineageScreen.tsx` is the "lineage" nav item's screen now (`legacy` marker removed), built against the real, already-merged `GET /v1/datasources/{id}/unified-lineage/impact/{node_id}` (`unified_lineage_api.py::build_unified_lineage_impact_payload`, the same traversal the native MCP tool `atlas__get_lineage_impact` serves) -- no invented endpoint. Flow: pick a datasource (`GET /v1/organizations/{id}/datasources`, resolving the id `CatalogRowRead` doesn't carry -- see UX-15's `PageOf`/datasource-resolution note), search for the asset the question is about (reuses `fetchCatalogRows`, client-filtered to the chosen datasource's name), then the real impact call runs. Every hop's evidence is server-computed, not client-invented narration: `depth` (how many hops from the question's subject) and `contributing_edge_sources` (which real lineage mechanism produced the hop -- foreign key, dbt dependency, OpenLineage ETL, a view/procedure definition, or a steward-approved suggested relationship) are read directly off `UnifiedLineageImpactNodeRead` and rendered as a plain-language sentence per hop (e.g. "orders_raw is fed by raw_sales, contributed by a foreign key"). "Streams" is client-side pacing over the already-fetched, already-bounded response (the endpoint has no SSE mode) -- hops reveal one at a time (140ms apart) rather than materializing as a wall of text at once, upstream ordered farthest-to-focus and downstream ordered focus-to-farthest so the sequence reads as a walked path. The DAG becomes the supporting view, not the entry point, exactly as specified -- but honestly scoped down from a full interactive graph: LN-8's Cytoscape-based virtualized canvas renderer (`ui/scripts/graph-engine.js`) stays exactly where it is in the legacy shell (UX-16, retiring `ui/`, has not happened), since re-implementing that real, already-shipped, already-tested large-DAG renderer inside this one row would duplicate rather than migrate it. What ships as `ui-next`'s own "Graph (supporting view)" tab is a lightweight depth-swimlane layout over the SAME impact response the narration reads (nodes grouped into real columns by their real `depth`) -- genuine node placement, not a fabricated diagram, but it does not draw connecting edges between hops the way a true DAG would; the ordered, explicit narration is what carries "highlights the path" for this row, not the swimlane view. Tests: `NarratedLineageScreen.test.tsx` (3) walks datasource-pick → asset-search → the real impact fetch → narration end to end (asserting the exact `{depth, nodeLimit}` args reaching the real endpoint), proves the graph tab is reachable but not the default/entry view (`aria-selected` on both tabs, URL `view=graph`), and proves the impact endpoint is never called before a node is chosen. `cd ui-next && npm run typecheck && npm run test && npm run build` all green (see UX-15's row for the full 46-test count, shared verification). No `src/aida` file touched. |
