## N. End-to-end audit findings (2026-08-30)

Full report: `Docs/60-delivery/04-end-to-end-audit-2026-08-30.md`.

> **Read this before trusting any DONE row above.** A four-part audit — test suite, dead-code
> reachability, primary-journey trace, production/security readiness — found that **17 rows in
> sections A–H marked DONE have unreachable implementations**, six of them P0. Roughly 4,600
> lines of code that passes its unit tests has zero call sites outside its own file and its
> own test. Verified against a full AST import graph from five real entry points, with dynamic
> dispatch ruled out.
>
> **The rows affected are: OB-1, OB-2, OB-3, AG-1, AG-2, AG-5, LN-3, TS-6, DQ-3, RT-1, RT-2,
> RT-3, RT-7, RT-9, TL-3, AG-6, SM-2, PG-1, PG-6, PG-8.** Their exit conditions describe
> behaviour the running application does not have. They are left as written rather than
> silently edited, so the correction is visible — but none should be counted as delivered.
>
> **AG-5 / LN-3 deserve separate mention.** `ai_decision_lineage.record_decision` has zero
> callers, so no decision, rejection or refusal is ever recorded and `list_refusals` queries a
> permanently empty table. Section §L positions the refusal record as the differentiator Atlan
> structurally cannot copy. That positioning is currently unsupported by the product.
>
> **Cause, and why it recurs:** rows are written from the module, not from the call graph. A
> module with passing unit tests and no callers is indistinguishable from a healthy one, and
> nothing in CI detects the difference. AU-1 below is the fix; it should land before any
> further DONE row is written.
>
> Separately, this file's own metrics are wrong: ST-02/ST-03 claim "716 passed" and "199
> routes"; actual is 1,458 test functions and 320 routes. `pytest-cov` is declared and invoked
> nowhere — line coverage has never been measured.

It started this session at 13, matching the audit's dead-module list exactly, then dropped in three steps as concurrent commits landed mid-session — `test_allowlist_has_no_stale_entries` caught every one on the next run, none by hand: `injection_defense.py` came off first (`6ed7e2f` wired it into `ingest_screening.screen_text`; `injection_corpus.py` is a separate module — not imported by `injection_defense.py`, only named in a docstring — and remains genuinely orphaned), then `quality_coupling.py`+`trust_scoring.py` (`a635f5f` wired both into `tool_api.py::execute_tool` and `agent_orchestrator.py`, closing DQ-3/RT-7/AG-6/TL-3), then `retrieval.py`+`fusion_ranking.py`+`graph_retrieval.py`+`embedding_provider.py`+`vector_retrieval.py` (`676bbf8` wired the hybrid/vector/graph/fusion stack into live orchestration, closing RT-1/RT-2/RT-3/RT-9/SM-2 — `vector_store.py` stayed behind per that commit's own known-limitations note: it implements a persisted index the change didn't populate, using live per-query embedding instead). `abac.py` (does not exist any more -- deleted 2026-08-31 under PG-1/PG-6/PG-8/AU-11, see that row) and `ai_decision_lineage.py` were *not* on the allow-list and never were: their routers were registered on the live app (`main.py:126-127`), so they were module-reachable even though the audit's deeper finding — specific functions (`record_decision`, real ABAC enforcement) have no live caller despite the module being imported — stands; that finer-grained, function-level claim is explicitly AU-2's scope, not this module-level gate's. `test_allowlist_has_no_stale_entries` re-verifies every allow-list entry on every run so this can't silently drift in either direction. `ruff check .` clean, `mypy src` clean (190 files), full `pytest` green |

| AU-3 | Config fails closed on unknown or missing `AIDA_*` variables | — | A | **P0** | DONE | — | Delivered 2026-08-31, `atlas/platform/config.py`. `model_config.extra` is now `"forbid"` (was `"ignore"`) — closes the kwargs/non-env-source half of C1. Verified experimentally that `extra="forbid"` alone does **not** catch a misspelled env var *name*: pydantic-settings' `EnvSettingsSource.get_field_value` only ever looks up known field names in `os.environ`, so an unmatched key like `AIDA_ENVIRONMNET` never reaches model validation at all — the audit's literal suggested fix is necessary but not sufficient. Added `reject_unrecognized_aida_env_vars` (`model_validator(mode="after")`), which scans the real process environment directly for `AIDA_`-prefixed names and flags any that are a fuzzy near-match (`difflib.get_close_matches`, cutoff 0.84) of a real field's env name — e.g. `AIDA_ENVIRONMNET` → "did you mean AIDA_ENVIRONMENT?". Deliberately *not* a blanket "any unrecognized `AIDA_*` var is an error": the codebase has a second, legitimate, open-ended use of the same prefix — `credential_reference="env://AIDA_SOME_DSN"` (`aida.secrets`) resolves arbitrary operator-named env vars this model never models by design (see `AIDA_SAMPLE_SOURCE_DSN`/`AIDA_SAMPLE_ORACLE_SOURCE_DSN`/`AIDA_SAMPLE_MSSQL_SOURCE_DSN` in `.env.example`/`compose.yaml`) — a blanket check would have broken that mechanism on every real deployment. Verified the near-miss heuristic doesn't false-positive against every such name currently in the repo. Added `reject_implicit_environment_outside_tests`: `environment` must now be present in `model_fields_set` (i.e. explicitly supplied by some source, not just defaulted) everywhere except under pytest (`PYTEST_VERSION` in `os.environ` / `"pytest" in sys.modules`, detected at import time so collection-time `Settings()` construction is covered too, not just individual test bodies) — the shipped bootstrap paths (`.env.example`, `compose.yaml`) already set `AIDA_ENVIRONMENT` explicitly, so this is a no-op for them. 8 new tests in `tests/test_config.py`: the audit's exact repro (`AIDA_ENVIRONMNET=production` → `ValidationError`), a second near-miss name, a regression test proving `AIDA_SAMPLE_SOURCE_DSN`-shaped credential-reference vars are *not* rejected, the missing-environment case (`_running_under_pytest` monkeypatched false) both failing and passing, and a test constructing `Settings()` from the literal `.env.example` file content end-to-end. `ruff check .`, `mypy src` (190 files, strict), and the full `pytest` suite (3125 passed, 5 skipped, 1 xfailed) all pass unmodified elsewhere — no other test file needed a fixture change. Not covered: a typo confined to a local `.env` file (never exported to `os.environ`) — the scan reads the process environment, not the dotenv source `get_settings()` also merges in; the shipped deployment paths inject real env vars, not a `.env` file, so this wasn't judged worth the added complexity in this pass |

This is a clean bill of health, not the absence of a check. `ruff check .` clean, `mypy src` clean (`require_roles_gate` lives in `tests/support/`, outside `mypy src`'s `packages = ["aida"]` scope, so it is ruff-checked but not mypy-checked, consistent with every other `tests/support/` helper), full `pytest` suite green (4,069 collected, 0 failures/errors, 6 skipped — pre-existing, unrelated to this change — in ~196s) |
| AU-8 | Migration↔ORM drift gate | — | B | P1 | DONE | — | Delivered 2026-08-31. `tests/test_migration_orm_drift.py`, wired into CI as its own `migration-drift` job (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`, Postgres 16 service container — the only job that needs one, which is why it isn't folded into `tests`). Resets a Postgres database's `public` schema to empty (not `CREATEDB` — works for any role that owns the target database, matching a service container's default user), runs `alembic upgrade head` through Alembic's Python API (not a subprocess), and diffs the result against `Base.metadata` with `alembic.autogenerate.compare_metadata` — the exit criterion verbatim. Deliberately kept out of the shared ORM-`create_all`-against-SQLite fixture every other DB-backed test uses (real migrations against real Postgres cost ~10s, not milliseconds); it runs once, on its own. Genuinely ran against real Postgres in this sandbox (16, `service postgresql start`, non-superuser `aida` role) — not a "skip, hope CI catches it" delivery — and confirmed both directions before landing: passes clean at HEAD, and fails with a real diagnostic when a column is added to the ORM with no migration (verified by temporarily adding one and reverting). This immediately found real drift, exactly as DQ-1/the audit predicted: (1) **8 ORM tables with zero migration** — `consumption_record`, `negative_assertion`, `search_index`, `vector_embedding`, `freshness_observation`, `freshness_watermark_config`, `procedure_lineage_edge`, `view_lineage_edge`; (2) **`composite_key_candidate` missing 5 columns** (`table_profile_id`, `column_names`, `column_count`, `key_fingerprint`, `estimated_distinctness_ratio`) plus their index/FK, added to the ORM without an accompanying migration; (3) **`data_quality_incident.latest_observation_id`**: migration `1b7e4c9a62d0` created it `NOT NULL` with `ON DELETE CASCADE` against `data_quality_observation`, while the ORM (correctly) declares it nullable with `ON DELETE SET NULL` — the migration's shape was live-broken (`NOT NULL` + `SET NULL` is contradictory; deleting an observation would have raised a constraint violation, not nulled the pointer) — fixed to match the ORM, which reflects the intended schema; (4) **3 columns migrated as `postgresql.JSONB`** (`ai_remediation.resolution_evidence`, `ai_trust_snapshot.factors`/`blockers`, migration `c8a4d3e91f02`) against the codebase's own convention — 43 other migrations use plain `sa.JSON`, this was the one outlier — altered to match; (5) **`audit_archive_record`**'s unique constraint on `archive_id` was created via raw SQL (`audit_archive_record_archive_id_key`, Postgres's default auto-name) instead of the naming-convention name (`uq_audit_archive_record_archive_id`) the ORM expects — renamed. All five fixed in one new migration, `migrations/versions/09be3ab5b008_au8_reconcile_orm_migration_drift.py` (generated via `alembic revision --autogenerate` after the env.py fix below, then hand-corrected: autogenerate double-templated one `CheckConstraint`'s already-explicit name through `op.f()`, and the file's typing style was fixed from `typing.Union` to this repo's `X \| Y` convention to pass `ruff`). A genuine root cause surfaced along the way, fixed separately from the reconciliation migration: **`migrations/env.py` imported `aida.models` but never `aida.envelope_models`**, so the 5 ORM tables that module defines (`metadata_view_definition`, `metadata_routine`, `metadata_routine_parameter`, `metadata_object_description`, `metadata_source_grant` — all real, already correctly migrated) were invisible to `Base.metadata` for *any* Alembic autogenerate run, not just this test's — every future `alembic revision --autogenerate` has been blind to that entire module since it was added. Fixed by importing it alongside `aida.models`. Separately, `src/aida/models.py` had a real, narrow ORM bug this test surfaced: three FK columns (`NotificationEventRecord.incident_id`, `StudioEvalRun.change_set_id`, `CompositeKeyCandidate.table_id`) each declared a redundant `index=True` in addition to an already-covering named `Index` in `__table_args__` — no migration had ever created the second, duplicate index (unlike the structurally-identical `StudioTestRun.change_set_id`, whose migration *does* create both, so that one is not drift and was left untouched); the `index=True` was removed from each, since a second index on the same leading column has no value. One deliberate, documented non-fix: three raw-SQL trigram/expression indexes on `metadata_table` (`f9a2b3c4d5e6_catalog_scale_indexes.py` — `USING gin (lower(name) gin_trgm_ops)` and friends) have no ORM equivalent by design (SQLAlchemy can't cleanly declare a functional GIN index) and are excluded from comparison via an `include_object` filter added to both `migrations/env.py` (so real `alembic revision --autogenerate` runs don't propose dropping them either) and this test file, with a comment requiring the two lists be kept in sync. `ruff check .`, `mypy src` (189 files) and the full `pytest` suite (3,415 passed, 5 skipped, 1 xfailed) all pass; `alembic heads` confirms exactly one head (`09be3ab5b008`) post-landing. **Hardened 2026-09-12 after it reported drift twice that did not exist.** The gate resets a scratch database named after the deployment's own, so two concurrent suite runs resolve the same database and one drops `public` while the other is mid-upgrade or mid-diff; the loser reflects a half-dropped catalog (`could not open relation with OID ...`) and reports drift. That is the worst false failure this suite can produce, because drift is a claim a reader has no independent way to check -- it cost an investigation before the overlap was spotted. Clients now serialize on a session-level advisory lock, and `_reset_schema` takes the *lock-holding* connection rather than opening its own, so a later edit cannot separate the destruction from the exclusion; the upgrade moved to a worker thread because `migrations/env.py` runs its own `asyncio.run`, which would otherwise end the loop holding the lock. `lock_timeout` bounds the wait and names the cause, since a five-minute wait for a ten-second region is a stuck session rather than a queue. Measured, not read: a peer waited 3.33s for a holder's 4s hold, and three concurrent runs of the file now pass in an 11.6/19.2/26.9s staircase where two of three failed before. The four other Postgres files have the same shape and are deliberately documented rather than hardened -- none has been observed to fail, each already takes an override naming its own scratch database, and holding a lock across their module-scoped fixtures is real machinery for an unobserved fault; [the acceptance guide](../40-engineering/14-acceptance-testing-guide.md) section 4.1 lists the overrides and says to re-run a single file alone before reporting a drift or race failure |

| AU-12 | Survive a Temporal outage | — | C | P1 | DONE | — | Delivered 2026-08-31. `main.py`'s `lifespan` used to `await Client.connect(...)` directly (no timeout, no try/except), so an unreachable/slow Temporal server hung or raised right there and took the whole process down with it — before the ASGI server ever started serving traffic, including the read-only, non-Temporal-dependent routes. Fixed with a bounded, non-fatal `_connect_temporal` helper (`main.py:130-160`): `Client.connect` wrapped in `asyncio.wait_for(..., timeout=settings.temporal_connect_timeout_seconds)` inside try/except, turning any failure (timeout, connection refused, DNS failure) into a logged `temporal_connect_failed` warning and a `None` return instead of a raised exception or a hang. `lifespan` (`main.py:206-227`) calls it instead of `Client.connect` directly, leaves `app.state.temporal_client = None` on failure, logs `temporal_unavailable_at_startup`, and starts a background `_temporal_reconnect_loop` task (`main.py:163-188`) — only when the initial connect failed — that polls on `settings.temporal_reconnect_interval_seconds` and publishes the recovered client onto `app.state.temporal_client` once `_connect_temporal` succeeds, so `/health/ready` (`main.py:381-399`, unchanged — it already read `app.state.temporal_client` correctly, it just could never execute) flips back to `temporal: UP` on its own, no restart needed. The reconnect task is cancelled at shutdown (`main.py:271-274`), mirroring `_audit_archive_loop`'s existing started/cancelled-from-`lifespan`, `CancelledError`-re-raised shape (OB-3) for consistency with this module's one existing background-task convention — `worker.py`/`scheduler.py` were checked and have no reconnection pattern of their own to reuse (both are standalone processes that connect once and rely on their process supervisor to restart them). Two new `Settings` fields in `atlas/platform/config.py` (`temporal_connect_timeout_seconds`, default 10s; `temporal_reconnect_interval_seconds`, default 30s). Proven end-to-end in `tests/test_au12_temporal_outage_resilience.py` (5 tests, driving real ASGI `lifespan` via `TestClient` with a fake `Client` standing in for `temporalio.client.Client`, matching `test_observability.py`'s existing lifespan-driving pattern): `test_temporal_outage_at_startup_does_not_crash_app` (a raising fake connect — app still starts, `/health/live` still 200), `test_readiness_reports_temporal_down_during_outage` (`/health/ready` genuinely reports `temporal: DOWN`), `test_temporal_connect_is_bounded_by_timeout_not_hanging` (a 30s-hanging fake connect against a 0.2s timeout — startup returns in well under 5s, not 30s), `test_readiness_recovers_to_up_once_reconnect_succeeds` (fails once then succeeds — `/health/ready` flips from `DOWN` to `UP` on its own once the background loop's retry lands), `test_temporal_reconnect_task_is_cancelled_on_shutdown` (mirrors `_audit_archive_loop`'s shutdown-cancellation contract). `ruff check .` clean, `mypy src` clean (189 files, strict), full `pytest` suite passes (exit 0, zero `FAILED`/`ERROR` lines) — one pre-existing, unrelated failure was investigated and ruled out: `test_config.py::test_environment_must_be_explicit_outside_tests` (AU-3) fails only when `AIDA_ENVIRONMENT` is present in the ambient shell env at test time (confirmed via `git stash` that this reproduces identically on the unmodified branch tip, `fb6fe65`, with or without this change) — a latent conflict with `.github/workflows/ci.yml`'s workflow-level `AIDA_ENVIRONMENT: "development"` (added under AU-3, commit `cf1e65b`) that is out of AU-12's scope (`main.py` Temporal-connect logic, the readiness probe, and their tests only) and is flagged here for visibility, not fixed |
| AU-13 | Dependency and secret scanning in CI; build the image in CI | — | D | P1 | DONE | — | Delivered 2026-08-31. Four new gates added to `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, following the existing checkout/setup/run job pattern exactly: **`dependency-scan`** — `pip-audit` (chosen over `safety`: free, no API-key requirement, reads `uv.lock`-exported requirements directly) runs against `uv export --frozen --no-dev --no-hashes` — the exact non-dev dependency set the Dockerfile now installs, so the scan and the shipped image can't drift apart — and fails on any *unbaselined* known vulnerability. `uv` itself has no native audit subcommand (checked: `uv --help` has no `audit`), so `pip-audit` runs via `uvx --python 3.13` (ephemeral, not added to `pyproject.toml`, keeping this change file-scoped to `ci.yml`/`Dockerfile`); `--python 3.13` is required — pip-audit's default resolve venv used 3.11 and failed to resolve `numpy==2.5.2` (requires >=3.12), a real failure mode caught by testing this locally before landing it. Running the scan against the actual locked set surfaced 16 real, currently-unfixed CVEs across 3 packages already pinned in `uv.lock` (`cryptography` 45.0.7: GHSA-537c-gmf6-5ccf, PYSEC-2026-{2141,35,3552,3553,3554,36}, fixed in 46.0.6/46.0.7; `pyjwt` 2.10.1: PYSEC-2025-183, PYSEC-2026-{120,175,176,177,178,179}, fixed in 2.12.0/2.13.0; `pyopenssl` 25.3.0: PYSEC-2026-{2268,2269}, fixed in 26.0.0). Bumping those pins is a dependency-version change to `pyproject.toml`/`uv.lock`, out of this row's explicit file scope (ci.yml + Dockerfile only) and disruptive to bake in unilaterally on a branch many concurrent sessions push to continuously — so the gate carries a small, dated, named `--ignore-vuln` baseline of exactly those 16 IDs (with the reasoning and the fix-versions recorded inline in `ci.yml`), not a blanket exemption: any *other* known-vulnerable package, or a new CVE against an already-pinned one, still fails the job. A separate follow-up task was queued to bump the three packages and remove the baseline entries. A full, unbaselined `pip-audit` JSON report plus a CycloneDX 1.6 SBOM (`uvx --from cyclonedx-bom cyclonedx-py requirements`, validated locally: 94 components) are uploaded as build artifacts every run, so the 16-CVE baseline stays visible rather than swept away. **`secret-scan`** — `gitleaks` v8.21.2 (chosen over trufflehog: single static binary, no license requirement, `.gitleaks.toml` config is simple), full git-history scan (`fetch-depth: 0`). Running it locally against the real repo history found 6 true findings, all confirmed-synthetic connector-DSN-parsing test fixtures (`dapi0123456789abcdef` in `test_connectors_databricks.py`, a placeholder BigQuery service-account JSON in `test_connectors_bigquery.py`, and a `TEST_DSN_AU4_POSITIVE` env-var *name* tripping the generic-entropy rule in `test_inv6_value_freedom.py`) — no real secrets. New `.gitleaks.toml` (repo root; scoped to enabling this CI job, not application code) extends gitleaks' default ruleset unchanged and adds one narrow, **path**-based (not commit/fingerprint-based) allowlist for exactly those 3 files — path-based specifically because this branch is rebased constantly by concurrent sessions, which rewrites commit hashes and would silently break a fingerprint-based allowlist. Verified clean (`no leaks found`, exit 0) after adding it. **`docker-build`** — runs `docker build` against the (now-fixed) `Dockerfile`, plus a smoke step that runs `python -c "import aida.main"` inside the built image, directly validating AU-9's k8s manifests actually have a working image behind them. **Dockerfile fix**: `Dockerfile:15` (the audit's `:17` reference had already drifted — confirmed by reading the file fresh) was `python -m pip install .`, a fresh, unpinned resolve; changed to install `uv` (pinned `0.8.17`) and run `uv sync --frozen --no-dev` against the copied `pyproject.toml`+`uv.lock`, `UV_PROJECT_ENVIRONMENT=/app/.venv` on `PATH` — the identical `--frozen` contract `ci.yml`'s own `UV_FROZEN=1` documents, so image dependency versions can no longer float away from what CI tests against. **Docker build validation — partial, and here's exactly why**: Docker (client 29.3.1 + daemon, via `sudo dockerd`) is available in this sandbox and a full `docker build .` was attempted; the daemon itself works and (once given proxy env vars) resolves `docker.io` manifests, but the image-layer *blob* download redirects to `production.cloudfront.docker.com`, which this sandbox's egress proxy denies as an org policy CONNECT rejection (confirmed via `/__agentproxy/status`'s `recentRelayFailures`, not a transient error) — the proxy README explicitly says report, don't route around, a 403 policy denial, so the pull was not forced through. This blocks *only local validation*; the real GitHub Actions runners the `docker-build` job runs on have normal internet access and are unaffected. In place of the blocked full build, the Dockerfile's actual novel logic — `uv sync --frozen --no-dev` against the committed `uv.lock`, then `import aida.main` — was validated directly (outside the container, same venv-creation command the image runs): installs cleanly, `aida.main.app` imports as a real `FastAPI` instance. Verification: `ruff check .` clean, `mypy src` clean (189 files), import-linter 4/4 contracts kept, full `pytest` suite green (3,414 passed, 5 skipped, 1 xfailed, 0 failures). **Follow-up (2026-08-31, same day)**: the queued task to bump the three baselined packages landed. `pyproject.toml`/`uv.lock`: `cryptography` 45.0.7 -> 50.0.1, `PyJWT[crypto]` 2.10.1 -> 2.13.0, `pyOpenSSL` 25.3.0 -> 26.4.0. Getting a fixed `pyopenssl` to resolve at all also required bumping `snowflake-connector-python` 3.15.0 -> 4.4.0 — 3.15.0 (and every 3.x/early-4.x release checked) caps `pyOpenSSL<26.0.0`, which made every pyopenssl-CVE fix version unresolvable until the connector's own ceiling was lifted (4.4.0 is the first release pinning `pyOpenSSL>=24.0.0` with no upper bound); its `snowflake.connector.connect(**kwargs)` call site in `src/aida/connectors/snowflake.py` uses only long-stable keyword arguments, so no source change was needed there. Re-running the unfiltered `pip-audit -r requirements-locked.txt` locally after the initial cryptography 46.0.7 bump (the version the baseline comment above named as the fix) still showed 4 of the 7 cryptography CVEs unresolved (PYSEC-2026-{3552,3553,3554}, GHSA-537c-gmf6-5ccf — real CVEs published against cryptography after the baseline was dated, fixed only in 48.0.1/49.0.0/50.0.0), so `cryptography` went to the current latest, 50.0.1, and `pyOpenSSL` to 26.4.0 (the first release whose `cryptography<51,>=49.0.0` window admits it). A second re-scan came back clean: 0 known vulnerabilities in the locked, non-dev dependency set. `.github/workflows/ci.yml`'s `dependency-scan` job's `--ignore-vuln` baseline was emptied out accordingly (comment left in place explaining the mechanism and pointing back here). No direct `cryptography`/`OpenSSL` imports exist in `src/`; the three test files that do (`tests/test_oidc.py`, `tests/test_persona_derivation.py`, `tests/test_token_revocation.py`) use only `cryptography.hazmat.primitives.asymmetric.rsa`, unaffected by the bump. `src/aida/oidc.py`'s `PyJWT` usage (`jwt.get_unverified_header`, `jwt.PyJWK.from_dict`, `jwt.decode`, `jwt.PyJWTError`/`jwt.InvalidTokenError`) is unchanged 2.x API, untouched. Verification: `ruff check .` clean, `mypy src` clean (189 files), full `pytest` suite green aside from one pre-existing, unrelated failure confirmed present on `origin/feature/snowflake-dbt-lineage-mcp` before this change too (`test_doc_claims.py::test_cited_import_linter_contract_name_resolves` misreading this row's own backtick-quoted job names as import-linter contract-name citations — not touched, out of this change's scope) |
| AU-14 | Measure coverage at all | — | D | P2 | DONE | — | Delivered 2026-09-01. `pytest-cov==6.2.1` was a declared dev dependency invoked nowhere -- no `--cov` flag anywhere in `pyproject.toml`'s `[tool.pytest.ini_options]` or `.github/workflows/ci.yml`. Wired in: `pyproject.toml` gets `[tool.coverage.run]` (`source = ["aida", "atlas"]`, branch coverage on, test/migration dirs omitted) and `[tool.coverage.report]`; the `tests` CI job now runs `--cov=aida --cov=atlas --cov-report=term-missing --cov-report=xml --cov-fail-under=69` and uploads `coverage.xml` as a build artifact. Deliberately not folded into `addopts` -- `migration-drift`/`reachability`/`connector-version-fixtures` each run pytest against one file for a fast targeted signal, and `--cov` there would slow them for a meaningless partial number. Real baseline, not estimated: a full run (`AIDA_ENVIRONMENT=development uv run --extra dev pytest --cov=aida --cov=atlas ...`, 6,095 tests, 6,082 passed/3 failed (pre-existing, unrelated -- see the results doc)/9 skipped/1 xfailed) measured **74.10% combined statement+branch coverage** (77.39% statement-only, 59.91% branch-only; 31,433 statements, 7,107 missed). 32 files at exactly 0%: 2 real `aida` modules (`batch_ingestion.py`, `workflows/worker.py`) and all 6 files (`api.py`/`contracts.py`/`events.py`/`repository.py`/`router.py`/`service.py`) of 5 `atlas` module scaffolds (`catalog`/`connectivity`/`identity_tenancy`/`ingestion`/`observability_audit`) -- genuinely unreachable: `main.py` never imports any of them, and their own module-scaffold tests live under `src/atlas/modules/*/tests/`, outside `testpaths = ["tests"]`, so they're never collected. Re-verified the row's own "16 of 36 API modules ... imported by no test" count against real coverage rather than a name-grep proxy: today there are 51 API-surface modules (46 `src/aida/*_api.py` + 5 `src/atlas/modules/*/api.py`, the latter a naming convention the row's original count predates); **0 of the 46 `aida` ones are at 0% coverage** (lowest is `ingestion_api.py` at 15.41%, an 8-module name-grep proxy overstates the gap -- all 8 turn out 17-72% covered via `TestClient`-driven integration tests that never mention the module's file name), while **all 5 `atlas` ones are at 0%** -- the real, current instance of the row's claim. Floor `--cov-fail-under=69` chosen as a 5-point margin below the measured 74.10%, matching AG-8's own quality-baseline gate's 5-point-margin convention for a percentage-point CI floor -- a regression ratchet the suite already clears by 5.10 points today, not a target still to reach. Full results, the complete 51-module coverage table, and the pre-existing-failure analysis published at `Docs/90-reference/coverage-baseline.md` (+ machine-readable `coverage-baseline.json`) |


## O. Agent architecture critical review (2026-09-09)

Full report: `Docs/10-architecture/15-agent-architecture-critical-review.md`, which carries the
per-finding evidence, the operator-visible behaviour changes, and the reproductions.

> **Read this before trusting the agent rows above.** The review found that four controls the
> platform *documents* were not the controls it *ran*: the ADR-0027 T0/T1 ceiling could be widened
> by configuration, bulk items were tiered without their size, a proposal with no confidence was
> the reviewer rule's most permissive input rather than its most restrictive, and a suspension
> raised mid-batch did not stop that batch. Separately, `AgentContract`'s `daily_token_cap`,
> `per_run_token_cap`, `wall_clock_seconds_cap`, `context_product_ids` and `write_lanes` were
> validated on write, stored, and read by no runtime path at all.
>
> **Rows falsified by the remediation, left as written rather than silently edited** (same
> convention as §N): **UX-19**, **N15** and **UX-15**. All three state that `AgentRun` carries no
> foreign key back to `AiAsset`/`AiAssetVersion` and that no proposal pathway has a
> confidence-gated auto-apply branch. Both statements were true when written and are false now:
> `AgentRun.ai_asset_version_id` exists and is populated (`models.py`, set at
> `agent_orchestrator.py::_stage_screen`), the roster is scoped through it, and ADR-0027's
> `reviewer_agent.auto_decide_tier0_tier1` is a real unattended-decision branch governed by
> `reviewer_agent_approve_confidence`. Their delivered work stands; their evidence text does not.
>
> **What no amount of this closes.** AR-09. Enterprise-scale capacity is unmeasured and untouched,
> and nothing in sections A-O licenses a claim about it.

| ID | Item | Mod | Ph | Pri | Status | Owner | Exit |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| AR-01 | The agent tier ceiling is an invariant, not a default | 13 | B | P0 | DONE | — | Delivered 2026-09-09. `review_risk_tiers.HARD_MAX_AGENT_TIER` + `effective_agent_ceiling` clamp the configured value before anything derives from it; live at `reviewer_agent.py:571` (pre-review), `reviewer_agent.py:720` (auto-decision) and `agent_contract_api.py:887,972,1004` (state endpoint), all reachable from `aida.main`. Configuration narrows and cannot widen: `agent_decidable_object_types("T3")` returns only T0/T1 types. The clamp is recorded per item as `pre_review_evidence.max_tier_clamped` and reported as `ReviewerAgentStateRead.max_tier_clamped`, so a refused ceiling is visible rather than merely ineffective. `test_ar01_*` in `tests/test_reviewer_agent.py`, including an end-to-end refusal of a `CONTEXT_PRODUCT_VERSION` under a configured T3 ceiling; each fails against `15f29cd`. **Note the old test's shape:** `test_the_allowlist_is_derived_from_the_tier_table_not_from_config` asserted only that admitted types were *classified*, which a T3 ceiling satisfied while admitting every T2/T3 one — a passing test asserting the wrong property is how this survived |
| AR-02 | Bulk review items are tiered from their authoritative size | 13 | B | P0 | DONE | — | Delivered 2026-09-09. `reviewer_agent._sized_risk_tier` resolves the real count before tiering — `ModelImportBatch.change_count` for workbooks, `len(BulkStewardshipOperation.subject_ids)` for bulk stewardship, both through the review's own back-link, since `GovernanceReview` carries no payload column — and escalates to T2 when the count cannot be established. The tier is recomputed at decision time, not read back from the row, so a batch that grows between passes escalates. `review_risk_tiers.requires_size_evidence` is the predicate that tells an action-gating caller it must resolve size first; `risk_tier_for` keeps its pure type-only answer for the queue-labelling callers. `test_ar02_*`, including the review's 900-change example on both bulk paths and a batch that grows after pre-review |
| AR-03 | An agent approval requires positive, object-specific evidence | 13 | B | P0 | MERGED | — | 2026-09-11: continue only in R11-C3 (section P below); previous status: IN PROGRESS. Completed implementation is retained; only the successor remainder is in scope. **Historical acceptance/evidence:** Code delivered 2026-09-09; **not DONE, and the gap is the interesting half.** `_EVIDENCE_RESOLVERS` reads each proposal's own row (`AssetDescriptionDraft.overall_score`, `GlossaryLinkProposal.confidence`, `MetadataEnrichmentProposal.confidence`, `QueryHistoryMetricCandidate.confidence`, `DocumentClaim.confidence`, verified size for the two bulk types); a type with no resolver abstains, so `TERM_SEMANTIC_BINDING` and `ASSET_DOCUMENTATION_VERSION` — unscored human assertions — are no longer agent-decidable. A missing confidence is now the most restrictive input, not the most permissive. **The review understated this:** the old `_proposal_confidence` read `proposal_confidence` out of `GovernanceReview.pre_review_evidence`, which pre-review alone writes and which pre-review skips already-reviewed rows to reach — so the value was *always* `None`, every tier-eligible item with no prior rejection and no open incident was recommended APPROVE, and `reviewer_agent_approve_confidence` was unreachable dead configuration. **Evaluation delivered 2026-09-11, and it is why this row stays open.** `tests/test_ar03_false_approval_benchmark.py` puts twins — two proposals of one type with the same evidence, one true and one false — through each producer's own scoring function and `reviewer_agent._assess` at the production ceiling and 0.8 threshold. 12 pairs across 7 object types: **no pair is told apart**, and the false twin was approved in 11 of 12 pairs before this pass and 7 after. `DOCUMENT_CLAIM` (name-match certainty, 1.0 for every matched row) and `GLOSSARY_LINK_PROPOSAL` (constants 1.0/0.92, both over the threshold) lost their resolvers; model-inferred `METADATA_ENRICHMENT_PROPOSAL` (`engine_type = LLM_ASSISTED`) now abstains as model-written column drafts already did; `QUERY_HISTORY_METRIC_CANDIDATE` moved T1 → T2 to match `SEMANTIC_METRIC_PROPOSAL`, since approving it publishes a `SemanticMetric`; and the data-dictionary column match stopped using `ILIKE`, under which a `%` cell matched an arbitrary column at 1.0. **Exit condition still not met, now measured rather than suspected:** the 7 remaining false approvals — description drafts on misleading sources, rules-inferred enrichments from a keyword match, bulk operations and workbooks checked only for size — need evidence independent of the proposal. Do not enable unattended review on this row alone. `test_ar03_*`, `tests/test_ar03_false_approval_benchmark.py` |
| AR-04 | Agent decisions rest on evidence read at commit time | 13 | B | P0 | MERGED | — | 2026-09-11: continue only in R11-C4 (section P below); previous status: IN PROGRESS. Completed implementation is retained; only the successor remainder is in scope. **Historical acceptance/evidence:** Code delivered 2026-09-09. `_assess` is shared by pre-review and the decision path, so a stored recommendation only makes an item *eligible*; a fresh verdict contradicting it abstains. `reviewer_agent_evidence_max_age_minutes` (default 60) bounds how stale a selected recommendation may be. Per-organization suspension is re-read before each commit (`reviewer_agent.py:752` region), bounding the stop at one item. **The multi-worker experiment this row asked for has now been run, 2026-09-10: `tests/test_reviewer_agent_postgres_suspension.py`.** Four reviewer agents on four connections to a real PostgreSQL, each with its own organization and eight pre-reviewed items, stopped by a suspension committed from a fifth connection. Measured maximum stop delay at READ COMMITTED: **0 items** (per-worker `[0, 0, 0, 0]`, all four refused `reviewer_agent_suspended`), against the claimed bound of 1. At REPEATABLE READ: **6 items** per worker — the entire remaining batch, all four running to exhaustion and never seeing the suspension. Falsifiable and falsified: disabling Guard 0 turns the READ COMMITTED case into `[6, 6, 6, 6]`/exhausted, i.e. exactly the REPEATABLE READ result, which is what the guard is worth. Two findings the experiment produced rather than assumed: `reviewer_agent` never commits — transaction control belongs to its caller, so a batch's decisions land or roll back as a unit and 'decisions made' is the honest unit of stop delay; and the SQLite reproduction seeds `AssetDescriptionDraft.table_id` with a bare `uuid4()`, which only passes because SQLite does not enforce the foreign key. **Exit condition still not fully met:** each worker had its own organization, so row contention between workers competing for the *same* queue is untested — the loser's conflict handling, and whether a lost race aborts a batch or skips an item, remain open. REPEATABLE READ remains unbounded by design and is now asserted rather than described. `test_ar04_*` |
| AR-05 | Contract token and wall-clock caps are enforced at runtime | 13 | B | P1 | DONE | — | Delivered 2026-09-09, reproduction 2026-09-10. `aida/agent_budget.py` is the runtime consumer the review found missing. Live call sites, all reachable from `aida.main` via `GovernedAgentOrchestrator.run` (line numbers current at 2026-09-10; the live-call-site rule is about the call site existing on a reachable path, and these drift with any edit above them): reservation `agent_orchestrator.py:1288` -> `_reserve_generation_budget`, failure settlement `:1323` -> `agent_budget.settle_unresolved_run_budget`, success reconciliation `:1360`. **A failed generation is settled to the input it demonstrably sent** -- neither released in full, which would let an agent pointed at a broken provider retry for nothing, nor held in full, which was the second implementation and locked the agent out of its own day after five timeouts (reproduced, then fixed, 2026-09-10). The daily cap lives inside a conditional `UPDATE`'s own `WHERE` against the new `agent_budget_window` row (migration `a7c41e93d2b0`, applied and verified against the running PostgreSQL 17), so the database decides who fits; the per-run cap is checked twice (input estimate before the provider call, total after) and the wall-clock cap from the run's own start. `model_gateway.estimate_payload_tokens` is now a named shared function so the number a run is refused on and the number recorded against it cannot drift. **Raced on real PostgreSQL**, the reproduction F05 got: `tests/test_agent_budget_postgres_concurrency.py` — ten racers on ten connections released from one `asyncio.Barrier`, at READ COMMITTED and REPEATABLE READ. Falsifiable and falsified: removing the cap clause from the `WHERE` turns three READ COMMITTED tests red with a 1000-token day holding 2000. **Two honest limits stated in that file rather than hidden:** at REPEATABLE READ removing the guard changes nothing (the isolation level bounds the day by itself, so those cases confirm the invariant without testing the guard), and the loser is refused SQLSTATE 40001 rather than `AgentBudgetExceeded`, which needs a retry loop the application does not supply. Every figure remains an *estimate* — no provider adapter reports billable usage, and `reconcile_run_budget` is the single place a real one would enter |
| AR-06 | The capability envelope is enforced beyond `tool_slugs` | 13 | B | P1 | MERGED | — | 2026-09-11: continue only in R11-C6 (section P below); previous status: IN PROGRESS. Completed implementation is retained; only the successor remainder is in scope. **Historical acceptance/evidence:** Code delivered 2026-09-09. `context_product_ids` is enforced at the boundary where a contracted agent actually consumes a context product — `mcp_server.py:510`, reachable from `aida.main` — via `load_contract_for_principal` + `context_product_violation`, with the fail-closed semantics `tool_slugs` already has: empty allowlist allows nothing, unparseable envelope allows nothing, and a workload identity resolving to zero or to several contracts is *refused* (`agent_contract_unresolved`) rather than treated as uncontracted. That last rule corrects this row's own first implementation, which returned `None` on ambiguity and so let an agent holding two contracts past the envelope check entirely — a fail-open introduced by the fix for this finding and caught in review the same day. `write_lanes` had no consumer anywhere in the codebase, so declaring one is now **refused** at validation (`envelope_write_lane_unenforceable`) rather than stored unenforced — an unenforced control quoted in a governance dossier is the failure this row names, and the refusal forces the first lane-bearing write path to ship its check in the same change. **Matrix delivered 2026-09-11: `Docs/10-architecture/18-agent-capability-enforcement-matrix.md`.** It found the orchestrator's contract enforcement — existence, kill switch, `tool_slugs`, token caps — unreachable from both live paths, because neither MCP `tools/call` nor REST `POST /datasources/{id}/agent-analyses` told the orchestrator which contract applied. Both now resolve the caller's contract (`load_contract_for_principal`) and pass it; an `agent:` identity with none or several is refused, and MCP audits the refusal (`mcp.tool_call.agent_contract_denied`). `tests/test_ar06_contract_on_live_paths.py` fails without the wiring. **Exit condition still not met:** `context_product_ids` is unchecked on `resources/read`, `prompts/get` and the REST context-product paths; the contract kill switch does not reach native MCP tools or REST tool execution; an external agent with the Reviewer role decides reviews outside the in-process agent's tier ceiling and sampling; an `AgentDeveloper` agent can edit other agents' contracts. **Operator action required before enabling:** every contract written before 2026-09-09 has an empty `context_product_ids`, and as of 2026-09-11 an empty `tool_slugs` refuses every governed tool call its agent makes over MCP or REST — update each contract to name the products and tools its agent needs. `test_ar06_*`, `tests/test_ar06_contract_on_live_paths.py` |
| AR-07 | Agent run reporting is attributed, or attributed to nobody | 13/21 | B | P2 | DONE | — | Delivered 2026-09-09. Each roster entry is scoped to its own version's runs through `AgentRun.ai_asset_version_id` (`scope="AGENT_VERSION"`); runs with no agent identity are reported once at the top level as `unattributed` and credited to nobody; a registered agent the platform never executes reports zero rather than borrowing the organization's totals. TL-6's ratio is still reused verbatim — `compute_tool_first_rate` is called directly rather than through `fleet.tool_first_execution_rate`, whose own `WHERE` cannot scope narrower than an organization. The blanket no-auto-apply text is replaced by a per-agent answer read from the version's own `AgentContract`, so the ADR-0027 reviewer agent reports its real branch, threshold and enabled state, and `AgentAutoApplyRead.enabled` distinguishes "no branch" from "branch, switched off". Supersedes the honesty notes in **UX-19** and **N15**. `test_ar07_*` in `tests/test_agent_roster.py` |
| AR-08 | Model-call economics claims match the implementation | 08 | B | P1 | DONE | — | Documentation correction, 2026-09-09. `ceil(N / 25)` — 4,000 nominal batch attempts for 100,000 selected tables — replaced the "~50 model calls" claim in `10-architecture/08-workers-and-workflows.md` and in the review's section 4, and the confidence-to-publication DAG was corrected so confidence no longer flows straight to Publish. This section is the **only current work-status authority**. Sections A?O preserve stable IDs,
design rationale and landing-time evidence; their historical priorities and counts do not
schedule work. `MERGED` closes an old tracking row, not the unfinished capability: follow its
R11 successor below. Dated review documents supply evidence, not separate queues.

Source findings D/B/X/S/P are in the [September 11 review](../review-2026-09-11/REVIEW.md).
The C items preserve omissions from earlier reviews. Read the
[reconciliation decisions](23-review-reconciliation-2026-09-11.md) for ordering and the
September 5 / agent-review crosswalk. R11 prefixes prevent collisions with older D1/P1 IDs.

`TODO` = proceed when predecessors finish; `PARTIAL` = retain code, finish named remainder;
`BLOCKED` = prerequisite named in exit cell (independent preparation may proceed);
`DEFERRED` = outside current execution until its trigger; `CANCELLED` = obsolete task;
`DONE` = acceptance evidenced. Owner cells name accountable areas, not assigned individuals.
Engineering lead must assign a person when a task starts. No task is IN PROGRESS merely
because it appears here. No production configuration, model approval or deletion is performed
by this documentation reconciliation.

| R11-D6 | Use authenticated principal for expiry banner | DONE | Engineering | Landed 2026-09-11: `useSession().me.principal_id` drives mine filtering, with the development principal used only when no session exists (bare unit render). **Closed 2026-09-12 by exercising every transition in a browser under a real OIDC session** (the mock issuer with tokens shortened to 90 seconds, so expiry could be watched rather than waited for). Observed live: the sign-in gate resolving to CONNECTED; **renewal** keeping the session CONNECTED for five minutes on 90-second tokens (`oidcClient.ts` renews at half-life for short tokens); the issuer stopped so a renewal fails, the token lapsing, and the banner reading *The session has expired*; then *Sign in again* returning to CONNECTED, with `/v1/me` going 200 -> 401 -> 200. **Two defects were found doing it, neither by a test.** (1) **A rejected token was reported as expired.** A token the deployment refused for its audience produced the same 401 detail as an expired one, because `oidc.py` collapsed every `jwt.PyJWTError` into one message, and `session.tsx` mapped every 401 to *The session has expired. Sign in again* -- which, for a misconfigured identity provider, sends the operator into a sign-in loop that can never succeed. Now `OidcTokenExpired` (a subclass, so every existing handler still denies) is raised for `ExpiredSignatureError` alone and the API names expiry and nothing else; revocation, signature, audience and issuer stay one generic detail, as `TokenRevokedError` and INV-4 require. Disclosing expiry is safe because the holder can read `exp` for themselves. The banner now says *Sign-in rejected* for the generic refusal, worded so it stays true for a revoked token too, where signing in again does help. (2) **My own first fix regressed the commonest real expiry, and it was caught live.** A lapsed token is never sent, so the API answers *a bearer token is required* and cannot say "expired"; the rebuilt banner fell back to *Sign in to continue* and dropped the one true statement it had. Only the browser knows the token lapsed, so `Session.lapsed` now carries `sessionLapsed()` via `useSyncExternalStore`, and the same live run afterwards showed *The session has expired* again. Also found and stated rather than fixed: the issuer signs with a constant `kid`, and `_load_jwks` refetches only for an unknown `kid`, so restarting the mock issuer leaves the API verifying against a stale key for up to `oidc_jwks_cache_seconds` (300 s) -- a mock-issuer artefact, because a real provider rotates to a new `kid`, which the refetch already handles. Tests: `test_oidc.py` (expired is reported as expired; a rejected token is not), `test_token_revocation.py` (the HTTP detail; revocation still generic), and `session.describe.test.ts` -- the first tests `describeSession` has had, including the lapse regression. Frontend 814/814 and backend security slice green; one accessibility-sweep test failed once under full-suite load and passed alone, in its file, and on a full re-run, recorded as a flake rather than attributed. |

The divergence summary reports what *traffic* showed and is structurally silent about a datasource nobody queried; the new `unresolved_scope` reports what the *inventory* implies -- datasources with no live source binding, or with more than one, which `resolve_workspace` refuses to guess between. Those break the moment `unresolved_workspace_posture` flips to DENY whether or not anyone has ever queried them, so no amount of quiet traffic makes them safe. The two are kept as separate fields rather than summed into one score, and `blockers` states each in a sentence naming what must change. `ready` stays false while unresolved scope proceeds undecided even on an otherwise clean estate, because a request that names nothing bypasses all of it. Value-free (INV-6): counts, reason codes and object names only. The liveness rule mirrors `resolve_workspace`'s own, so an expired binding stops counting on the morning it expires, and the blocker list is capped with the true counts preserved. |

The reader is live (`GET /v1/business-nodes/{node_id}/rollup`) and R11-X5 keeps the hierarchy surface as a missing-UI gap, so retiring would have been the wrong call. `business_graph.run_rollup_rebuild_pass` now runs from `run_scheduler_iteration` -- closure first, then roll-up, because the roll-up aggregates *through* the closure -- stalest-org-first so a bounded batch cannot starve a tenant, with per-organization fault isolation. The rebuild races `extend_closure_for_new_node`, which writes the same primary key incrementally; the pass is made to **lose** that race (per-org rollback, logged, retried next sweep) because a user's write must not fail for a cache rebuild. Node's floor moved to 22.12 or newer, and CI's node 22 satisfies it only because setup-node resolves the newest 22.x, now recorded because nothing else did. **The upgrade introduced a flake, found by running the suite three times instead of once:** the accessibility sweep's `waitFor` sat on the 1s default while the `findByRole` two lines above asked 5s for the same lazy-chunk race, and vitest 5 overlaps files harder. Measured: vitest 2 green 3 of 3; vitest 5 at 1s failed 2 of 3, at 5s failed 1 of 3, at 15s green 5 of 5 -- each failure on a different screen and never when the file ran alone, so the budget was wrong and not the assertion, which is unchanged. **gitleaks judged: false positive.** Reproduced with v8.21.2 over 587 commits: one finding, a literal smoke-test token in the proxy-contract job, occurring once in the repository and sent to a localhost stub that same job starts and which validates nothing. Nothing to rotate. Allowlisted by token value and deliberately **not** by path, because a workflow file is exactly where a real credential would be pasted. Now passes the tuple, hitting the store's own empty guard. Latent only because embeddings are unconfigured; `test_vector_channel_ranks_nothing_when_nothing_is_authorized` covers it. |
| R11-D15 | Dev overlay published no UI port | DONE | Engineering | Found while landing X9 and fixed 2026-09-11: `compose.dev.yaml` used `ports: !reset` above its `5174:5174` entry, and `!reset` empties the list *including* the entry below it, so the development overlay published no ports for `ui-next` at all and the `http://localhost:5174` documented in README, `ui-next/README.md` and `00-status.md` was unreachable. `!override` is the correct tag; the rendered overlay now maps 5174. |
| R11-D16 | Keep routine bodies and unmodelled SQL value-free in storage | DONE | Engineering | Found 2026-09-15 while building R11-FP07; no review listed it. `redact_for_storage` labelled text `PARSED` whenever sqlglot returned a tree, then replaced its `exp.Literal` nodes. sqlglot keeps many values elsewhere: a PostgreSQL or Snowflake `AS $$ ... $$` body is a `Heredoc` or `Block`, an unreadable statement is an opaque `Command`, and a BigQuery JavaScript body is a `RawString`. Each rendered its literals back verbatim. **Every dollar-quoted PostgreSQL routine body ingested carried its source values, labelled PARSED**, because the connector stores `pg_get_functiondef` output. MCP `get_transformation_detail` released those bodies. The same hole reached the gateway's audit redaction and dbt compiled SQL for `Command` statements. Fixed at the one choke point: `PARSED` now also requires that a value-aware lexical scan of the rendered text finds nothing left. Otherwise the text is scrubbed by a lexer and labelled `LEXICAL`. The lexer treats a dollar-quoted span introduced by `AS`/`DO` as program code and any other as a value; it also handles `E'...'` escapes, comments, per-dialect double-quote rules and non-SQL language bodies. For a `Command` statement, dbt now stores no SQL. Correctly labelled PL/pgSQL bodies are now `LEXICAL`, so the routine-body gate accepts `PARSED` or `LEXICAL`: `require_eligible_routine_body`, the lineage agent's filter and MCP routine detail. Both tiers are value-free, and `LEXICAL` exists so procedure lineage works. View gates stay `PARSED`-only. Rows stored before the fix keep their literals until rescanned; `scripts/reredact_stored_sql.py` repairs only rows still holding value-shaped text (dry run by default, counts only). On this repository's platform database the dry run scanned 0 routine bodies, 0 view definitions and 3 dbt resources, none leaking, so nothing needed repair locally. Evidence: `tests/test_sql_redaction_program_bodies.py` covers nine body shapes across PostgreSQL, Snowflake and BigQuery, confirms precise T-SQL and Oracle redaction is unchanged, and covers the repair decision. Suite runs: 48 dependent test files (parser, redaction, lineage agent, dbt, MCP routine detail, query gateway): 5,808 passed, 162 skipped, 1 xfailed. |
| R11-D17 | Nothing could tell whether the deployment matched the source | PARTIAL | Platform lead | Found by review 2026-09-16 (F03), and the finding's real content is the absence of a detector rather than the drift itself. `scripts/openapi_diff.py` generates the spec **in process** and diffs it against the committed baseline; CI's migrations job counts `alembic heads` **source-side**. Neither speaks to a running deployment, so the configured database sat one revision behind head (`b7e2d9c4f158` against `f4a8c1d7e236`) and the running images predated `footprint_metrics_interval_seconds` entirely -- meaning the deployed scheduler did not contain `run_footprint_metrics_pass` at all -- while both health endpoints answered 200. Liveness and readiness do not prove revision parity, which is the whole lesson. `scripts/check_deployment_parity.py` is the missing gate: it compares `SELECT version_num FROM alembic_version` against `alembic heads` and names the unapplied revisions, compares the live `/openapi.json` path and schema sets against the committed baseline by name rather than by count, asserts every `required` readiness dependency, and checks that the deployed image's `Settings` carries every setting the source declares -- the last of which is what surfaced the stale image behind a healthy API. An unreachable deployment reports "cannot tell", which is not the same answer as "parity". [`Docs/40-engineering/16-deployment-alignment-and-enablement-runbook.md`](../40-engineering/16-deployment-alignment-and-enablement-runbook.md) carries the ordered alignment procedure with its hazards named. Evidence: `tests/test_deployment_parity.py`. **Deployed and verified 2026-09-16.** The four migrations authored in parallel were chained to a single head (`c9b3e7f15a48`) first, because `alembic upgrade heads` would otherwise have applied three unreviewed branches as a union; `tests/test_migration_orm_drift.py` then passed against real PostgreSQL, proving all five apply to an empty database and leave a schema matching `Base.metadata` -- run before touching the configured database rather than after, since a failed `migrate` service holds the API down. `docker compose --profile full up -d --build` rebuilt every service, repeating the profile flags so the profiled containers could not be left on the old image. The migrate service applied `b7e2d9c4f158` through `c9b3e7f15a48` in order. `scripts/check_deployment_parity.py` then reported **8 matched, 0 drifted, 0 not measured**: the database at the source head, live OpenAPI 412 paths / 516 schemas equal to the committed baseline, API version 2.0.0, every required readiness dependency UP, and the deployed image declaring all **270** settings the source declares -- the check that had caught the stale image behind a healthy API, where `footprint_metrics_interval_seconds` was missing and so the running scheduler did not contain `run_footprint_metrics_pass` at all. It does now, at its 300s default. All 17 services healthy. **The gate earned its keep on 2026-09-17**: after this cycle's features landed it reported 3 drifted (schema behind `d4a8c1e6b520`, live OpenAPI 412 paths against a 413 baseline, 516 schemas against 518) -- which is the drift it exists to catch, found without anyone remembering to look. Redeployed, and it reads 8 matched / 0 drifted again, with 17 services healthy and the new `metadata_trigger` / `metadata_sequence` tables and the 12-kind capability matrix answering on the deployed API. **The gate had a blind spot, found 2026-09-19.** It reported 8 matched / 0 drifted -- "the deployment is running this tree" -- against an image built 40 minutes before `b76842d`, so the deployed API was missing both of that commit's fixes (R11-D24, D25). Schema, routes and settings are all it compared, and a code-only change moves none of them. A commit hash would not have closed it: the image is built from the working tree, so it can hold edits no commit names, and a tree at the same HEAD can gain more. Fixed by comparing content: `aida.source_identity` hashes every file the Dockerfile copies (line endings normalised, bytecode ignored), readiness publishes it as `build.source_digest`, and the parity script computes the same digest over the checkout as a fifth comparison. Where `docker exec` works it names the differing files. `build.commit` (the `ATLAS_BUILD_COMMIT` build arg, threaded through every backend build in `compose.yaml`) is only a label printed beside the verdict. An image that publishes no digest is UNKNOWN, never MATCH. Measured against the running pre-fix image: 8 matched, 0 drifted, **1 not measured**, exit 2 -- the same deployment the old script called parity. Evidence: `tests/test_source_identity.py` (pins the digest to the Dockerfile's COPY lines) and `test_a_stale_image_with_matching_schema_routes_and_settings_is_not_parity`. **First live match, then first live catch, 2026-09-19.** At 10:10:02 a peer session deployed `de84027` from a clean `git worktree add --detach` checkout (0 changes). It reported that parity run from that checkout read **9 matched, 0 drifted**, the source digest included. That is the peer's measurement; nobody could re-run it, because it lasted 31 seconds. At 10:10:33 a plain `compose up --build` from the shared working tree, run by a session not identified (not the peer, whose images share one ID across services, and not this app's terminal), built a separate image per service. At 10:12:00 it recreated the containers, and its migrate step applied `0871649b2bb1 -> f7c2d9a4b61e`. That is an **uncommitted**, untracked R11-OKF02 migration, written at 10:09:43 and edited after it was applied. Parity from a clean checkout of committed `1295c63` then read **4 matched, 5 drifted**, and named each difference: the uncommitted revision in the database; 433 paths against 432 and 571 schemas against 566; 290 settings against 277; and a digest mismatch naming 20 files plus one untracked module. From the shared tree it read 5 matched, 4 drifted. The settings and migration comparisons would have drifted without the digest, but only the digest names the files. `build.commit` read `unknown`, because neither build passed `ATLAS_BUILD_COMMIT`. A second untracked head, `92b060bb2e10` (R11-REV01 playbook dry-runs, written 10:15:22), leaves the shared tree with two unchained heads. Committed HEAD cannot be deployed now: its migrate step would stop on the unknown revision and hold the API down. Images built by this session from a clean worktree at `8d3009c`, after the full suite passed there (13,011 passed, 0 failed), never ran: both later builds replaced their `:latest` tags. **Remaining:** the owner of `f7c2d9a4b61e` and `92b060bb2e10` chains and commits them, or downgrades the database off the uncommitted one. Then deploy committed code, built from a clean checkout with `ATLAS_BUILD_COMMIT` and with build and `up` in one command, and have parity read a MATCH that stays up. After that the row is kept open only until the parity check runs somewhere other than one developer's machine. **The runbook's enablement half was carried out on 2026-09-17** (F04): both intervals set to their documented values and the sample source given the credential its scans needed, with the loop observed acting on a real change -- see [26-maintenance-loop-enablement-2026-09-17.md](26-maintenance-loop-enablement-2026-09-17.md). Shipped defaults are unchanged: the intervals still ship at 0. |
| R11-D18 | The documented bootstrap produced a config that could not load | DONE | Platform lead | Found 2026-09-17 by two sessions independently, an hour apart, each thinking it was the other's breakage. `.env.example` ships `AIDA_SAMPLE_SOURCE_DSN`, a `credential_reference="env://..."` target that is deliberately **not** a `Settings` field -- `reject_unrecognized_aida_env_vars` says so in its own comment and points at `.env.example` as the place that does it. But it tolerated the name only in the *process environment*, because pydantic-settings' env source drops an unknown key before validation. The **dotenv** source does not: it hands the key to the model, where `extra="forbid"` refuses it. So `cp .env.example .env` -- the documented bootstrap -- produced a `Settings(_env_file=".env")` that raised `extra_forbidden` for **every** host-side script, while the identical name exported into the environment worked fine. It shipped because the existing test loads `.env.example` into the environment and passes `_env_file=None`: it exercised the source that always worked and never the one that was broken. The dotenv source now applies the same fuzzy rule the env path already used -- a name that is not a close match of a real field is a credential reference and is dropped; a name that *is* a close match is a typo and `extra="forbid"` still refuses it loudly. Narrowed, not weakened: `extra="forbid"` was already catching a misspelling in `.env`, and that is kept; the defect was that it could not tell a typo from a credential reference and refused both. Evidence: `tests/test_config.py` gains `test_env_example_boots_as_an_actual_dotenv_file`, which reads the shipped template *as a dotenv file* and fails with `extra_forbidden` against the old code, plus a guard that a near-miss of a real setting is still refused; 17 passed. |
| R11-D19 | The catalog listing counted tables the caller may not see | DONE | Engineering | Found 2026-09-18 by R11-GQL01's REST/GraphQL parity test, which is the kind of defect parity exists to find. `GET /v1/organizations/{organization_id}/catalog-rows` computed its `total` over the whole organization **before** the per-datasource authorization gate ran, and the gate then dropped refused rows from the page only. So a caller who could read one source of three was told how many tables the other two held -- the existence and approximate size of estate they are not permitted to see, which is what the design's "filter forbidden nodes before counts" rule exists to prevent. GraphQL filtered first, so the two surfaces disagreed. The test covering the denied datasource asserted the page's items and **never asserted `total`**, which is how the leak survived every run. The count now runs only over datasources the gate admits, sharing one decision per datasource with the page filter so neither pays twice, and the test asserts `total == 1` where the old code returned 2. **Page composition and cursors are deliberately unchanged**: restricting the page query too would be cleaner, but it would change documented pagination behaviour and break the cursor-for-cursor parity with GraphQL that had just been proven. Evidence: `tests/test_catalog_rows_read_model.py`; the GraphQL, INV-4 and INV-5 suites, 269 passed. |
| R11-D20 | An Ask run's evidence after the plan stage was never stored | DONE | Engineering | Found 2026-09-18 while proving R11-OKF02's Ask receipts: the run held them in memory and the stored row did not. `RunLedger.publish_plan_evidence` reassigned the *same*, in-place-mutated dict to the JSON column `AgentRun.plan_evidence`; SQLAlchemy compares a reassigned value with itself and writes nothing, so only the plan stage's first assignment ever reached the database. `model_call_evidence`, `budget_evidence` and `lineage` were confirmed missing from the stored row of a completed run, and every other key `_generate_statement` adds the same way shared the fate -- so a run reopened from history, or one refused part-way through generation, had lost the evidence the method's own docstring says must persist. The one test that read such a key read the in-memory run, which is why it passed. Fixed with `flag_modified` on every publish. `tests/test_okf_context.py` now reads the stored column directly and compares the stored step trace too (which was unaffected); both Ask tests fail with the fix removed and pass with it. |
| R11-D21 | The lineage agent failed to re-examine a redefined routine that kept any of its lineage | DONE | Engineering | Found 2026-09-18 while making decided lineage follow the body (R11-FP01). R11-FP16 re-examines a routine the source redefined structurally, and the agent's routine path then inserted every edge of the new body afresh -- so an edge the new body still wrote collided with its decided row on the table's unique natural key, and the item FAILED with an IntegrityError. A redefinition usually keeps most of what a routine writes, so the common case failed and nothing new was proposed. The existing test passed because its seeded edge matched nothing the body produced. A re-examination now replaces the routine's undecided proposals, writes only keys not already decided, reconciles decided edges (R11-FP01), and declines as `lineage_already_known` when everything the body writes is already decided. Evidence: `tests/test_decided_lineage_reconciliation.py::test_a_redefinition_that_keeps_its_lineage_is_known_lineage_not_a_failure`, which fails with the old path restored. |

The console's own test mocks the call, which is why it passed. The request model now accepts every capability the spec declares; `tests/test_lineage_agent.py` holds the literal to the spec and to the console's exact body, and fails with the old literal. | R11-D23 | A context product admitted any retrieval hit kind it did not recognise | DONE | Engineering | Found 2026-09-18 by the trigger-downstream work, which deliberately added no trigger retrieval candidate because of it. `ContextProductScope.admits` handled each known kind explicitly and ended in a fallthrough that **admitted** any kind carrying no `table_id` -- so the product boundary was safe only for the kinds someone remembered to list, and every future candidate kind was admitted until someone noticed. This had already happened once: review 2026-09-16 F02 was a ROUTINE hit admitted exactly this way, and the fix then was to add a branch; the default that let it through was never changed. Now every kind retrieval emits has a named rule and an unknown kind is **refused**, the same direction R11-FP12's gateway scope takes for an unresolved reference. A structural test derives the emitted kinds from the code -- every hit constructor across `src/aida` and `src/atlas`, reading the kind argument and failing rather than guessing on anything it cannot classify -- and checks both directions, so adding a candidate kind without a rule fails the build; it is itself tested against made-up producers including a new TRIGGER one. **One behaviour change:** a dbt resource Atlas could not match to a catalog table is now refused under a product, since products have no dbt reference group and an unmatched relation is an unresolved reference. The unused `METRIC` alias was removed rather than left to pre-admit whatever later took that name. Evidence: `tests/test_context_product_hit_types.py`. Also found, not fixed: an admitted routine, metric or ontology concept still passes the tables it reads or maps to into the model's context even when they are outside the product -- R11-FP12's gateway scope still refuses any statement that reads them, but the model sees their schema. That is a design question for R11-FP12, not a defect in this row. |
| R11-D24 | Redaction dropped dynamic SQL, so a routine it could not understand read as understood | DONE | Engineering | Found 2026-09-18 while building R11-FP07's source ranges; fixed 2026-09-19. In T-SQL, `END EXEC(@sql);` with no semicolon after `END` parses as one sqlglot `Command` that **re-renders as just `END`**. `redact_for_storage` accepted a render if it contained no value-shaped text -- and a render that drops text passes that check trivially, because text that is gone contains no values. So the stored body lost its dynamic SQL, was labelled PARSED, and the lineage parser reported a fully understood routine whose real behaviour is decided at runtime: exactly what review 2026-09-16 F06.4 said must never happen. The module's own docstring already stated the rule -- `Command` text is scrubbed lexically and labelled LEXICAL -- but it was enforced only through the value scan. A parse containing any `Command` now falls back to the lexical scrub. **Measured before it went in**: over the adversarial corpus it moves exactly 2 of 104 PARSED bodies to LEXICAL, both `EXECUTE IMMEDIATE` in BigQuery and Snowflake -- the same defect in two more dialects -- and nothing else; a check keyed on lost words flipped 16, mostly cosmetic, and was rejected. **It cannot weaken value-freedom**: its only failure mode is LEXICAL, which is in `VALUE_FREE_REDACTION_STATUSES`, and the tests assert both halves -- the construct survives and a planted value beside it is still removed. The routine now reads `is_fully_parsed: False` with reason DYNAMIC_SQL. **Existing rows:** `scripts/reredact_stored_sql.py` is the wrong tool -- it only touches rows still holding value-shaped text, and the dropped text is not in storage anyway. A rescan repairs the body, and does so safely: `_upsert_routine` rewrites the redacted body unconditionally, while `code_change_signal` and the definition-version logic key on the raw source fingerprint, so no spurious change signal or version is created. The configured deployment holds no T-SQL, BigQuery or Snowflake routine, so nothing there was exposed. Evidence: `tests/test_sql_redaction_program_bodies.py` (four tests, all failing against the previous redaction). |
| R11-D25 | A product that pinned meaning refused the meaning it pinned | DONE | Engineering | Found 2026-09-18 by R11-D23's inventory; fixed 2026-09-19. `ContextProductScope.admits` decides a GLOSSARY_TERM hit on `term_version_id` and a SEMANTIC_METRIC hit on `semantic_model_version_id` -- and retrieval put neither on the hit. Each read as `None`, which is in no pinned set, so a product that pinned a glossary term or a semantic model **refused every hit of that kind, including the ones it pinned**: pinning meaning silently disabled the very meaning it was meant to select. The existing test passed throughout because it built the hit's metadata by hand, so it tested the rule and never the data the rule is fed. Both ids were already in scope where the hits are built -- a metric version carries its model version as a foreign key -- so the fix is carrying them. Evidence: `tests/test_semantic_glossary_binding.py::test_a_product_that_pins_meaning_admits_the_meaning_it_pinned`, which retrieves the hits for real and fails with `KeyError: 'term_version_id'` against the previous code. |
| R11-D26 | Classifications never propagated through captured stored procedures | DONE | Engineering | Found 2026-09-18 while adding trigger lineage to classification propagation. The collector read view edges, a legacy pasted-SQL procedure table nothing has written since R11-X5, and OpenLineage -- and **never the captured-routine lineage table**. So a PII column read by a stored procedure that writes another table did not mark the target: the classification silently stopped at the procedure, and every masking and authorization control keyed on classification treated the target as ordinary data. A governance defect, not a quality one. It now reads captured-routine lineage under the same rules as triggers: reviewed edges only, intermediate temp-table hops followed across rather than tagged, only ever raising, and an edge that cannot be resolved to columns recorded as a declared gap rather than tagged at table grain. Proven end to end on live PostgreSQL and SQL Server. Evidence: `tests/test_procedure_classification_propagation.py`, `tests/test_procedure_propagation_live.py`. |
| R11-D27 | A dropped connection could retire an axis, and a driver's message reached storage | DONE | Engineering | Found 2026-09-18 while bringing the warehouse adapters under R11-FP02's refusal rule. Oracle, Snowflake, BigQuery and Databricks absorbed **any** failure on an optional axis, not only a refusal -- so a dropped connection left an empty axis, and a FULL run then **retired** every object on it. The facet mechanism had been built to re-raise anything that is not a refusal for exactly this reason; these four predated it. They now absorb refusals only. Separately, their per-axis unavailable reasons embedded the driver's message, which was written to stored `unavailable_reason` columns -- an INV-6 violation, since a driver message can quote a row value. Those reasons are now fixed text. The one retry kept is Databricks' narrower view read. |
| R11-D28 | Lineage named a restricted datasource's tables to a caller its workspace refuses | DONE | Engineering + Security | Found 2026-09-19 while building R11-GQL01's lineage reads; fixed the same day (code in `2caa213`, tests and regenerated artifacts in the commit after it). `GET /v1/datasources/{id}/unified-lineage/graph`, `.../impact/{node_id}`, its `/export`, `GET /v1/data-domains/{id}/unified-lineage/graph` and the MCP tools `get_lineage_graph`, `get_lineage_impact`, `resolve_entity` and `get_transformation_detail` checked the caller's roles and tenant only -- the surface-control matrix showed workspace `no` on each route -- while every catalog read of the same datasource asks the workspace gate (`READ_METADATA`). Each surface now asks that gate before it builds anything: the routes and the export through `gate_read` (403 with the gate's reason); the MCP tools answer a refusal exactly as they answer a missing datasource; the domain graph withholds a refused datasource -- no node, no edge, no cross-source candidate -- and counts it in the new `withheld_datasource_count`, never naming it, and the Lineage screen's domain view says how many sources it left out and why. Under the default SHADOW posture the gate allows and records, so nothing changes until a workspace enforces. Verified: `tests/test_lineage_workspace_gate.py` 19 (each route and the export refuse a non-member with `NO_WORKSPACE_MEMBERSHIP` before the graph is built, admit a member under an ALLOW policy and read a workspace-free datasource as before; the domain graph withholds and counts; three MCP tools refuse exactly as for a missing datasource; the matrix now shows workspace `yes` on the four routes), 7 of 7 mutants caught. The MCP suite's fake-session lineage tests now record the gate call and assert its shape rather than bypass it; `UnifiedLineageScreen.test.tsx` 17 (the notice shows the server's count and stays hidden at zero). Deployed at `de84027`: the routes read as before under SHADOW and the domain graph reports `withheld_datasource_count` 0. | R11-D29 | Routine lineage named the wrong table for a column, and dropped cursor reads | DONE | Engineering | Found 2026-09-19 by the R11-FP07 and R11-FP03 slices, fixed in `9c73ef3`. **Wrong facts:** an unqualified column resolved against any single table in the statement, never the statement's own target, so in `DELETE FROM dbo.final WHERE id IN (SELECT r.id FROM dbo.rejects r)` the outer `id` was recorded as `dbo.rejects.id`. A cursor parameter was recorded as a column (`ops.flags.p_id`), as were declared locals, record fields, sequences (`seq.NEXTVAL`), `EXCLUDED` and derived-table aliases. An INSERT with a column list took its rows from the first SELECT anywhere in the statement -- a UNION inside a WHERE became the inserted rows -- and dropped a UNION's second branch. **Silent drops:** `OPEN c FOR SELECT ...` read nothing; T-SQL `DECLARE cur CURSOR FOR SELECT` read nothing while the module docstring said it was captured; PL/pgSQL DECLARE sections were never walked. **Now:** a scope resolver (`procedure_column_owners.py`) records each column's owning table on the reference, resolving at its own query level and the outer levels it can correlate to; two candidate owners read `UNRESOLVED`, never the first. A PL/pgSQL declared name is the variable and produces no edge (PostgreSQL rejects the ambiguity by default; `#variable_conflict use_column` makes it `UNRESOLVED`); a PL/SQL declared name is `UNRESOLVED`, because Oracle lets a column win. `OPEN ... FOR <query>`, T-SQL cursor declarations and PL/pgSQL cursor and query-default declarations read into routine-local state, located, with token ranges; `OPEN ... FOR <string>` and `FOR EXECUTE` are `DYNAMIC_SQL`; simple `CASE x WHEN` and `<<label>>` parse instead of producing `PARSE_ERROR`. Evidence: `tests/test_lineage_attribution_and_cursor_reads.py` (33; 31 failed on the previous tree, the other 2 are guards), and one FP07 test that had asserted the wrong `dbo.rejects.id` now asserts the two right facts. Lineage and parser-dependent suites 6,486 passed. **On re-parse** stored edges change: wrong owners are re-attributed or become `UNRESOLVED`, fake tables disappear and decided edges no longer produced go SUPERSEDED as usual. A server-wide `plpgsql.variable_conflict = use_column` cannot be seen from the body, and a PL/pgSQL name declared in one nested block is treated as a variable throughout the routine. |
| R11-D30 | Routine and trigger lineage named a restricted datasource's tables to a caller its workspace refuses | DONE | Engineering + Security | Found 2026-09-19 by the R11-D28 follow-up: D28 gated the unified-lineage surfaces, but `POST /v1/datasources/{id}/procedures/{routine_id}/lineage/parse`, `GET .../procedures/{routine_id}/lineage`, `GET .../procedures/{routine_id}/parse-coverage` and `GET .../triggers/{trigger_id}/parse-coverage` still checked roles and tenant only -- the surface-control matrix showed workspace `no` on each -- so a caller an enforcing workspace refuses could read which tables a routine or trigger reads and writes.
