| R11-A1 | Make the task agents reachable on a seeded estate | DONE | Engineering | Landed 2026-09-12, found by trying to follow the acceptance guide's own instruction. **Setting an interval does not start a task agent.** `task_agent_schedule.registered_organizations` starts one only where its workload identity holds an `AgentContract` whose `AiAssetVersion` is APPROVED, and a freshly seeded estate has neither -- zero AI asset versions, zero contracts -- so all three agents were correctly and silently inert while the guide said to set an interval and watch one work. Nothing in the repository registered one. `scripts/seed_task_agent.py` is the missing path, and it drives the real handlers with three identities rather than writing an APPROVED row: create the asset and its v1, a steward authors the evaluation-gate corpus, submit, approve as a **different** identity, then the contract is written as the version's registered `owner_principal` -- which is the binding R11-C6 added, so the script exercises it rather than working around it. `--same-identity` and `--fail-one` exist so the two controls can be *watched* refusing, and both were verified live: "maker-checker separation is required", and a failing gate leaves the version unapproved with no contract written. **The script's own create path was broken and a test caught it**: `create_ai_asset` registers the asset *and* its first version and returns the version, so asking for another version off that return value answers 404 -- masked on every live run because the reuse branch took over once the first attempt had committed the asset. Verified end to end against the real deployment afterwards: steward and lineage agents registered and APPROVED, and `steward_agent.run` recorded SUCCESS twice with `proposed: 0`. **That zero is correct and is now documented as expected**: the seeded estate already carries an open description draft for all 27 tables and the worklist excludes a table whose description is already proposed, so there is nothing to propose -- and the run is visible in the audit ledger rather than the scheduler log, which logs nothing per agent run. 10 tests, asserting against `registered_organizations` itself so "registered" means "the scheduler would start it" rather than "some rows exist". |
| R11-FP01 | Scoped discovery selection and per-kind capability report (footprint FP-01) | PARTIAL | Connector lead | **Landed 2026-09-15 (`362b2d0`).** A `DiscoverySelection` names object kinds (TABLE, VIEW, MATERIALIZED_VIEW, PROCEDURE, FUNCTION) plus schema and `schema.object` include/exclude globs. Matching ignores case, because PostgreSQL folds identifiers to lower case and Oracle and Snowflake to upper; excludes win over includes. It is stored on `datasource.discovery_selection` and applied to connector output before anything is persisted, so all six connectors honour it identically. **The false-deprecation trap is closed.** A FULL run retires every existing object it did not see, so out-of-scope objects are now counted as seen first (`workflows.activities.out_of_scope_existing`). A control test runs the same scan without a selection and shows those objects retired. Each run records the fingerprint it applied and its `excluded_objects` count. New routes: GET/PUT `/v1/datasources/{id}/discovery-selection` and POST `.../preview`. The preview counts over the last completed scan (labelled `LAST_SCAN`), names include patterns that match nothing, and reports per-kind inventory and code facets that keep UNSUPPORTED apart from NOT_APPLICABLE. Source administration gains a Discovery scope panel. Migration `4c9e2a7f5b13` was verified up, down and up again on scratch PostgreSQL. Evidence: `tests/test_discovery_selection.py` and `SourcesScreenDiscoveryScope.test.tsx`; 162 affected backend test files, 7,109 passed; UI, 831 passed. **Schema scope pushed into source queries, 2026-09-15.** The PostgreSQL and SQL Server connectors now take a selection's schema includes and excludes into their own metadata queries (`Connector.scope_discovery`, `aida.connectors.schema_scope`), so an excluded schema's tables, columns, view definitions, routine bodies, comments and grants are never read, on the single-shot and the streaming paths alike. Every schema-bound discovery query already carried one system-schema predicate; the scope extends exactly that predicate, with the patterns bound as parameters, never interpolated. Only what `LIKE` expresses exactly is pushed: `*` and `?` translate with `%`, `_` and the escape character escaped; an exclude with a character class is not pushed, and an include list is pushed only if all of it translates. The pushed scope is therefore a superset of the selection, and the selection is still applied to every batch, so pushing down can only avoid reading. The receipt records `selection_pushed_down`. Evidence: `tests/test_schema_scope.py` (the translation matches `fnmatch` name for name; what cannot be translated never narrows; parameter numbering on every predicate; no schema-bound discovery query of either connector lacks the predicate); `tests/test_discovery_pushdown_live.py` against the live PostgreSQL and SQL Server sample sources (an excluded schema holding a view and a routine is never returned, an include list returns only its schema, and PostgreSQL's streaming batches agree); connector, discovery, receipt, envelope, ingestion and journey suites. **The per-engine capability matrix landed 2026-09-16 (review §5).** The review asked for one matrix at six facets -- inventory, definition retrieval, parsing/lineage, profile access, candidate generation, execution -- and found four differently-scoped artifacts, none of which was it: a two-facet discovery capability read, a flat connector bool dict, a parser matrix keyed by *dialect* rather than engine (with no Databricks row at all, because the parser refuses it), and the unrelated surface-control matrix. `aida.engine_capability_matrix` now generates one, derived from code at generation time --  the registry's definitions, `ConnectorCapabilities`' own fields, method identity against the base class, the parsers' dialect map, and a scan of each adapter for the catalog objects a facet must read -- published as [`Docs/90-reference/engine-capability-matrix.md`](../90-reference/engine-capability-matrix.md), served by `GET /v1/engines/capability-matrix`, with a `--check` staleness mode. It keeps native identity: an Oracle PACKAGE, a PostgreSQL materialized view and a SQL Server indexed view each keep their own row. It keeps the two facts the review said must not merge in separate columns -- a facet state says a code path exists, `maturity` is the registry's certification word, and `live_validation` says whether any test exercises that engine live, which is `EXERCISED` for PostgreSQL and SQL Server only. Teradata and Db2 render PLANNED / NOT_CERTIFIED with every facet UNSUPPORTED, and Databricks' parser row reads **refused**. `aida.capability_states` is the one shared vocabulary the five divergent enums lacked, and it adds the state the review named and the code did not have anywhere: **`PERMISSION_DENIED`**, so a facet whose read the source refuses is recorded rather than failing its whole run. `source_resolved` and `truncated` stay booleans in storage and are mapped at the reporting boundary -- the comment in `procedure_lineage_models` is right that a cosmetic sentinel must not become the stored truth. Evidence: `tests/test_capability_states.py`, `tests/test_engine_capability_matrix.py` including `test_capability_matrix_matches_certification`, which had been listed as Planned since 2026-08-30 in both ADR-0006 and the testing strategy and is now written and asymmetric in the direction that matters. **Triggers and sequences became native object kinds, 2026-09-17, and the push path finally applies a selection.** Neither kind was discovered by any of the six adapters and neither existed in `ObjectKind`, so a trigger that writes another table was a data path the footprint could not see at all. Modelled as themselves: a trigger has a firing table, an event, a timing and either a body or a named function; a sequence has a declaration and an owning column. The per-engine answers are per-engine rather than blanket -- PostgreSQL trigger *definition* is PARTIAL because a PG trigger has no body of its own and the function it names carries it; Oracle and SQL Server SUPPORTED; Snowflake and BigQuery have no trigger object and Databricks has neither, each NOT_APPLICABLE for its own stated reason. A sequence's current position is never read on any engine, and PostgreSQL reads `pg_sequence` rather than `pg_sequences` so `last_value` is unreachable rather than merely unselected (INV-6). **Discovery alone would have been half the feature**, so they are persisted, reconciled and counted, with a control per way a FULL pass can read silence as deletion. The one with no precedent: no envelope version carries a trigger or a sequence, so a FULL *push* is as silent about them as a 1.0 push is about views -- without that default the first nightly push after shipping would have tombstoned every trigger a pull scan had found. **The push path applies discovery selection for the first time**; it previously applied none, so a pushed snapshot was in scope by definition. The contract is ignore-but-report, chosen deliberately: rejecting turns an Atlas-side scoping edit into a broken nightly feed, and keeping means Atlas serves what the operator excluded. Real-engine evidence on live PostgreSQL and SQL Server: create, scan, assert fact by fact, drop, rescan, and the same row id reads DEPRECATED rather than vanishing. Migration `d4a8c1e6b520`, deployed and parity-verified the same day. **A trigger's write path is now parsed, 2026-09-17.** Discovery told us a trigger existed; it did not tell us what the trigger did to the data, which is the reason the feature exists. `parse_trigger_lineage` resolves the implicit subject a parser cannot see: PostgreSQL's `NEW`/`OLD` and SQL Server's `INSERTED`/`DELETED` bind to the firing table (including through an alias such as `FROM inserted i`), so a trigger writing `audit` from `NEW.*` records a path *from the firing table* -- which appears nowhere in the body text. Oracle and Db2's `:NEW` is **recorded unresolved rather than guessed**, because sqlglot reads the leading colon as a bind placeholder and there is no table-qualified reference to attach to. PostgreSQL needed the awkward case its PARTIAL rating already implies: a PG trigger has no body, so the parse follows `action_routine` to the function that does, and that join belongs on the trigger axis because a trigger function's `NEW` is meaningless without a firing table and one function may serve several tables. Edges land in their own `trigger_lineage_edge` (migration `b7c2f4d9e315`) rather than in `deep_procedure_lineage_edge`, whose NOT NULL `routine_id` *is* its identity -- filing a PostgreSQL trigger's edges under the function's id would make the function look done and stop the routine axis from examining it. Edges arrive PROPOSED, like every other agent proposal, so nothing steers retrieval on arrival. **A pre-existing false-clean defect came out with it, and it is the more serious find:** `INSERT INTO audit (customer_id) VALUES (NEW.customer_id)` produced **zero edges with `is_fully_parsed=True`**, because the insert extractor had no `VALUES` branch at all -- a write path reported as fully understood with nothing recorded, which is exactly what the review's "never label every path understood merely because an object was inventoried" rule exists to catch. Evidence: `tests/test_trigger_lineage.py` (37), with eight separate mutations each caught by a named test. Oracle PACKAGE became a selectable kind in FP03. **Verified live once Docker was back, 2026-09-17:** `tests/test_migration_orm_drift.py` passed against real PostgreSQL, so `b7c2f4d9e315` applies to an empty database and matches `Base.metadata` -- the argument-diff cross-check it stood on before was narrower than that gate, and is now superseded by it. `tests/test_trigger_lineage_live.py` passed on both engines: a real trigger on `orders` writing a second table, scanned, parsed and persisted, with the write edge's source asserted to be the firing table by the ids that same scan created. The migration then applied on the deployment (`d4a8c1e6b520` to `b7c2f4d9e315`) and parity reported 8 matched, 0 drifted. **Trigger edges became decidable, connected and self-refreshing, 2026-09-18.** The edges above were correct and inert: nothing could approve one, and a lineage edge nobody can approve steers nothing. The functional change is one line -- `"TRIGGER": TriggerLineageEdge` in `parsed_lineage_review_service.EDGE_TYPE_TO_MODEL` -- so a trigger edge goes through the same queue, the same single decision, the same maker-checker and the same audit as every other parsed edge, and no second approval path exists. ACTIVE edges now join the unified graph as `TRIGGER_DEFINITION`, so impact analysis follows a write a trigger performs; `trigger_parse_coverage` (migration `c5e9a2f7d314`) records whether each was fully understood. **Re-examination is derived rather than signalled:** on PostgreSQL the object that changes is the function, and routine ingestion already records that change once as a `ROUTINE` signal, so a trigger is re-examined when the routine its last parse read has a structural redefinition newer than that parse -- the same R11-FP16 rule the routine axis uses. A TRIGGER subject kind would have widened a CHECK on a shared table and written a duplicate fact per trigger naming the function. The agent also now proposes only table-to-table edges; a body's internal `SELECT ... INTO` a local variable used to land in the review queue as noise. **Live, on both engines:** a real trigger's edge is proposed, the agent is refused as its own reviewer (409), a person approves it, it becomes ACTIVE, and impact from `orders` reaches the audit table; on PostgreSQL a real `CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION` then leaves the trigger row unchanged, records a `ROUTINE` signal, and the trigger is re-examined. **A binding defect in the shared parser came out with it.** An *aliased* firing-row reference -- `SELECT i.x INTO #t FROM inserted i` -- recorded its source as a table literally named `inserted`: `_bind_subject` put `i -> dbo.orders` in the alias map correctly, and `sql_lineage_parser._extract_edges_from_select` then re-collected the statement's own tables and overwrote it. It survived because the unaliased form and an aliased `INSERT ... SELECT` resolved by other routes; the report that raised it described the unaliased shape, which worked. The `MERGE` extractor had the sibling bug, accepting the binding and never passing it on. The fix fills in the caller's map rather than overwriting it, which is a no-op for every caller that binds nothing -- **489 lineage tests pass unchanged** -- and five new cases fail against the old parser. Evidence: `tests/test_trigger_lineage_decidable.py` (30), 27 of which fail against the pre-change tree. **Decided lineage follows the body, 2026-09-18.** A re-parse under `require_review` -- the lineage agent's routine and trigger paths and a person's parse alike -- now reconciles what a person decided (`routine_lineage_edges.reconcile_decided_edges`): an ACTIVE edge the new body no longer writes becomes SUPERSEDED, **only after a complete parse**, since a statement the parser could not read may be exactly where it is still written; a SUPERSEDED edge the body writes again returns to PROPOSED for a person to decide, because what was approved was an earlier body; a REJECTED edge stays rejected. Only ACTIVE edges reach impact analysis, so retired lineage stops steering it and stays on record. Proving it exposed a worse defect on the routine axis, R11-D21. `GET /v1/datasources/{id}/triggers/{id}/parse-coverage` serves the coverage record, naming the function the body was read from; 404 means not measured. Evidence: `tests/test_decided_lineage_reconciliation.py` (6; four mutants -- the old routine path, never superseding, superseding after a partial parse, never reviving -- each caught by the tests that name it) and `tests/test_trigger_parse_coverage_route.py` (3). **Pushdown reached the four warehouse adapters, and triggers reached every downstream reader, 2026-09-18/19.** Oracle, Snowflake, BigQuery and Databricks now push object kinds and `schema.object` patterns into the reads whose rows belong to one object, every value as a bound parameter in the engine's own syntax, verified by scanning each engine pushed and unpushed over 11 selections with identical results. The inventories that establish which schemas exist deliberately still filter after reading: a FULL run retires a schema it does not see, and pushing kinds into the Oracle roster made 8 tests fail including the empty-schema retirement case. The discovery activity now actually passes the object scope, which the published matrix had said it did not. **Triggers reach retrieval** (PostgreSQL, via the function the trigger calls), **description drafting** and -- the one that mattered -- **classification propagation**, along reviewed edges only, only ever raising, with an edge that cannot be resolved to columns recorded as a declared gap (`TABLE_STAR`, `SOURCE_UNRESOLVED`, `COLUMN_NOT_IN_CATALOG`, `COLUMN_AMBIGUOUS`) rather than tagged at table grain. `get_transformation_detail` returns a SQL Server or Oracle trigger's own body through the same screening gate a routine body uses. Proven on live PostgreSQL and SQL Server. **Remaining:** SQL Server and Oracle triggers are not retrieval candidates -- deliberately, until R11-D23 made the product boundary fail closed, because a new candidate kind would otherwise have been admitted past it by default; trigger propagation gaps are logged and returned but not yet counted in the gap register. |
| R11-FP02 | Scoped scan receipts and per-facet completion (FP-02) | PARTIAL | Connector lead | **Landed 2026-09-15.** Each discovery run now stores a value-free receipt on `analysis_run.discovery_receipt` (`aida.discovery_receipt`). It holds the selection fingerprint; per kind, what was discovered and what the selection excluded; per facet, SUPPORTED or UNSUPPORTED from the connector's own capability flags; for view definitions and routine bodies, how many were captured, withheld and truncated; the stream state; and what reconciliation did, or why it did not run (INCREMENTAL_MODE, STREAM_NOT_FINISHED). The receipt is rewritten inside every batch's own commit, so it never claims a batch the catalog does not hold. It is marked COMPLETE at finalize, and INTERRUPTED on failure or on cancel. Retry stays the existing resume path, which reserves a new run with its own receipt. `AnalysisRunRead` serves the receipt, and Source administration shows one line per run: code captured or withheld, facets the connector does not collect, and an unfinished stream. Migration `8e1d4b6a2c90` was verified up, down and up again on scratch PostgreSQL. Evidence: `tests/test_discovery_receipt.py`, including a stream that fails after its first batch; the discovery, streaming, continue-as-new, envelope, OpenAPI and ORM-drift suites, 99 passed; `SourcesScreenAdmin.test.tsx`, 29 passed with the scope panel. **A run says how much of the source it may not see, 2026-09-16.** The receipt answered how completely each facet was read, and every one of its counts was right about what came back -- but a source's metadata is itself permission-filtered, so a login granted one schema of five reads that schema and reports a complete-looking run over a fifth of the estate. `Connector.count_invisible_objects` asks the source the unfiltered question where it can be asked: PostgreSQL's `pg_class` is readable by every role, so the connector counts the tables and views this login holds no privilege on -- the exact negation of `information_schema`'s own visibility rule, which is what its roster query reads. Materialized views and routines are read from `pg_class`/`pg_proc` directly and so are never hidden from discovery; counting the ones the login cannot SELECT or EXECUTE would report a gap that does not exist, and the live test pins that a routine in an ungranted schema is still discovered. The receipt carries it per kind beside `discovered` and `excluded`, and `null` means *the source could not be asked* -- never a zero. Every other connector inherits that `None`, honestly: SQL Server filters `sys.objects` by permission with no unfiltered view behind it. A schema the selection excludes is not counted as hidden: it was not asked for. `SOURCE_OBJECTS_INVISIBLE` is a footprint gap routed to the source administrator, counted from the last completed run's own receipt, and the run card in Source administration says it in words. Evidence: `tests/test_invisible_objects_live.py` against real PostgreSQL (a least-privilege role told how much it cannot see, the owner told nothing is hidden, an excluded schema not counted); `tests/test_discovery_receipt.py` (counted apart from what was returned; asked-and-none is not never-asked); `tests/test_footprint_gaps.py` (the gap follows the last completed run, and a source that could not be asked raises none); `SourcesScreenAdmin.test.tsx`, 20 passed. **A pushed snapshot keeps the same receipt, 2026-09-16.** The chunked push path wrote none, so a bank delivering its estate through the ingestion API got counters and nothing else -- the one path where Atlas has least idea what it was not sent. `process_metadata_ingestion_batch` now carries one receipt across the batch: each chunk is observed once, on the pass that records changes, so the reapply pass that resolves cross-chunk keys does not report an estate twice its size; a FULL batch records its reconciliation and an INCREMENTAL one records that it reconciled nothing, with the reason; a batch that fails keeps the chunks that landed, marked as a stream that did not finish. `selection_fingerprint` is null because this path applies no discovery selection -- it persists what the sender sent -- and `invisible` stays null because a sender cannot be asked what it left out. Evidence: `tests/test_push_ingestion_receipt.py` (a two-chunk FULL batch counted once with its reconciliation recorded; an INCREMENTAL batch naming why it reconciled nothing); IN-2 batch control suite, 39 passed. **Both closed 2026-09-17.** A refused facet used to fail the whole scan, so one missing grant lost an entire run; it is now classified from the driver's own SQLSTATE, recorded on the receipt, and the run completes with every other facet intact. Adopted across all six adapters -- including the four synchronous drivers, whose reads are captured in a thread and replayed into the classifier because passing one connection between pool threads is what pytds and databricks-sql forbid. Per-engine honesty about the classification itself: asyncpg and snowflake-connector report SQLSTATE and classify as refusals; pytds, oracledb and google-api-core report none, so their refusals record UNAVAILABLE, the under-claiming direction INV-9 wants; databricks-sql *does* report 42501, in `exc.context["sqlState"]` rather than as an attribute, so the classifier now reads a mapping-borne SQLSTATE -- still SQLSTATE and nothing else, never the message, which can quote a value. **A refused inventory is recorded and then still re-raised**, because a FULL run completing over zero objects would retire the estate. And `refused_facet_existing` keeps a refused facet's existing rows out of the deprecate-missing pass: a negative control proved that without it, a refused grants read tombstones every grant a previous run captured. Proven live -- `EXECUTE` on `pg_get_viewdef` revoked from a least-privilege login, the run COMPLETED, `view_definitions` read PERMISSION_DENIED, and the other six facets captured normally, with no driver message in the receipt. The push path's selection is closed under R11-FP01 above. The other five connectors answer UNKNOWN, and that is the design rather than a gap: each filters its metadata views by permission and keeps the unfiltered catalog behind a grant a least-privilege scanning principal is not meant to hold, so asking would mean widening exactly the access this count exists to measure. The hook is on the base connector for a deployment that does grant it. Incremental change-token discovery stays R11-B16 (DEFERRED). **PERMISSION_DENIED became a real per-facet outcome 2026-09-16 (review §5):** the state this row recorded as remaining existed nowhere in the code, so a facet whose read the source refused failed its run instead of being recorded. `aida.capability_states` defines it and the receipt carries it. The UNKNOWN invisible count stays UNKNOWN for five connectors, for the reason this row already gives -- that is still a design decision, not a gap. **A failed read stopped reading as an empty one, 2026-09-17.** A facet recorded `PERMISSION_DENIED` already led the run card as "unread, not empty"; one recorded `UNAVAILABLE` with reason `FACET_QUERY_FAILED` still printed `0 captured`, which is the same lie one state along -- the read failed, so of course nothing was captured, and the card said there was nothing there. The four states now have a written precedence, **UNSUPPORTED → PERMISSION_DENIED → UNAVAILABLE → the counters**, taken from the server's own `facet_state` order rather than invented client-side, because a card and a receipt disagreeing about one run would mean one of them is lying. Read as "what does the operator do next" it is narrowest remedy first: nothing, one grant, a retry, then the numbers. Two sub-decisions: `UNAVAILABLE` with `ADAPTER_NOT_IMPLEMENTED` is classified **with** UNSUPPORTED, since it is how `object_visibility` reports an adapter with no unfiltered catalog to ask and a rescan cannot fix it; and a non-zero `withheld` count is stated rather than replaced. It also fixed a quieter case -- the counter-less facets (indexes, grants, constraints) were not mis-stated, they were *silent*, so the card read as a clean scan. **Refusals classified from structured vendor codes, and proven live on SQL Server, 2026-09-18.** The classifier read SQLSTATE only, so pytds, oracledb and google-api-core recorded every refusal as UNAVAILABLE. It now reads each driver's numeric code as a field -- never the message, which can quote a value (INV-6): SQL Server 229/230/262/297/300/916/15247, each checked against `sys.messages` on the live container; Oracle ORA-01031/01045; BigQuery HTTP 403 with reason `accessDenied`, because a bare 403 is also quota and billing. ORA-00942 alone is **not** a refusal, since a genuinely missing table gives the same code; it counts only where the reader vouches every relation it read always exists, which the Oracle adapter can because it reads only `ALL_*` views. Live proof: a least-privilege login under `DENY SELECT ON sys.database_permissions`, error 229 with no SQLSTATE, and the real discovery run completing with `grants` the only facet PERMISSION_DENIED and the grant a control run captured still ACTIVE. See R11-D27 for the defect the same work closed. **Remaining:** Oracle, Snowflake, BigQuery and Databricks refusals are proven against their drivers' own error classes only -- no live instance of any of them exists here, so their SQL has never been parsed by a real engine. |
| R11-FP03 | Identity for packages, members and definition versions (FP-03) | PARTIAL | Metadata lead | **First slice landed 2026-09-15.** **Definition versions:** `metadata_routine_definition_version` keeps every captured definition instead of overwriting it (migration `b2d6e8f0a417`, which backfills version 1 per existing routine; verified up, down and up, with no ORM drift, on scratch PostgreSQL). A version is appended on first capture and whenever the raw-text fingerprint or availability moves, classed LITERAL_ONLY or STRUCTURAL. An identical rescan appends nothing, and rows are never updated. **Packages:** an Oracle PACKAGE was stored by the pull path but refused on push with a 422. Any restricted discovery selection dropped it, its grants were scoped as a TABLE, and it could reach tool generation as one callable thing. Now the push envelope accepts PACKAGE routines and grants; PACKAGE is a selectable kind whose grants follow it, and it reads NOT_APPLICABLE on engines without packages; tool generation refuses it with `PACKAGE_NOT_CALLABLE` before reading the body. Lineage parsing of a package body is unchanged. **Kind drift:** BigQuery's `SCALAR_FUNCTION` and `TABLE_FUNCTION` count as FUNCTION for selection, instead of matching no kind and being silently dropped. Evidence: `tests/test_routine_identity.py`; the change-signal, selection, envelope, procedure-tool, tool-agent and ingestion suites, 151 passed; `SourcesScreenDiscoveryScope.test.tsx`. **Second slice landed the same day.** **Member-level lineage and package-following selection, 2026-09-18/19; and this row's remainder had drifted.** Its previous remainder still listed PostgreSQL aggregate and window functions, Databricks views and routines, and a definition-history read route -- all three closed on 2026-09-17, missed because this is a multi-line row a cell-level edit did not reach. An Oracle package body is now split into member subprograms, each member's edges tagged MEMBER and linked to its captured routine by parameter names (NULL rather than guessed when ambiguous), with an explicit PACKAGE_FALLBACK recorded with its reason when the body cannot be split -- the two grains are never mixed in one parse. A package member now enters and leaves a scan with its package, and the scan and the retirement rule both call the same function, so a member the scan drops is exactly a member retirement counts as out of scope. **Third slice, 2026-09-19 (`24be852`, then `9c73ef3`).** A bare PL/SQL call (`p(x);`, `pkg.p;`, `s.pkg.p(a => 1)`, `p@link(x)`) had read as no lineage, so a body calling a writer looked fully parsed and read-only; it is now a `NESTED_PROCEDURE_CALL` gap naming the callee, which catalog descent reads through. Assignments, control keywords, `RAISE_APPLICATION_ERROR` and the `DBMS_OUTPUT`/`DBMS_APPLICATION_INFO`/`DBMS_LOCK`/`DBMS_SESSION`/`DBMS_STATS` packages are not calls; `DBMS_SQL.*` is `DYNAMIC_SQL`; an END label is not a call (without that guard every member of an unsplittable package became a false call). A call to a sibling member resolves by argument count, `=>` names and defaults, `(AMBIGUOUS)` when overloads differ only by type, and a local nested subprogram shadows the member as PL/SQL does; the callee's lineage is spliced in at the call site at most PARTIAL. Declaration sections are walked for routines, members, nested subprograms, package spec and body, and Oracle `DECLARE` blocks: `CURSOR c [(p)] IS <query>` is a `CURSOR_DECLARATION` read with statement and token ranges; `%TYPE`/`%ROWTYPE` are deliberately **not** reads (they depend on structure, not rows, and would put the routine in every who-reads-this-table answer); an unreadable declaration is a located gap. Also fixed: a routine with a nested subprogram took that subprogram's BEGIN as its own body, so its statements were never seen. The lineage agent now passes member routine ids (`resolve_package_member_ids`); its proposals had carried NULL. `9c73ef3` then made `x.DELETE/TRIM/EXTEND` a collection method only when `x` is declared, otherwise a package call. Evidence: `tests/test_r11_fp03_member_calls_and_declarations.py` (26; 25 failed on the previous tree on substance, each guard removed in turn failed a test). Re-parse moves statement ordinals for Oracle routines with cursors or nested subprograms, and a routine with a bare call is no longer fully parsed, so tool generation refuses it. **Qualified routine headers, 2026-09-19.** `_consume_subprogram` now reads `PROCEDURE ops.p(...)` as `p` with its parameters, where it read a unit named `ops` with none (previously worked around by reading the header). Evidence: the OUT ref-cursor tests in `tests/test_r11_fp07_loop_records_and_result_cursors.py`, with the FP07 slice above. **Remaining:** a spliced edge carries the caller's member id and `via_routine`, not the callee's routine id (needs a `via_routine_id` column, its ORM field and `routine_edge_row`, chained after `92b060bb2e10`); a sibling that itself calls out keeps the caller's gap marker, because the in-package pass runs before catalog descent (the parser would expose which members each sibling call reaches, and descent re-check the marker once those resolve); calls into another package stay gaps (`NOT_CAPTURED` in descent -- resolve `pkg.member` by `package_name` and take only that member's edges and the members it reaches, AMBIGUOUS when overloaded); functions called inside expressions are not calls, in every dialect; descent's final hop pass runs across a whole package at once, not member by member. |
- **Package members:** the Oracle connector reads the subprograms a package declares (`ALL_PROCEDURES` with `SUBPROGRAM_ID` and `OVERLOAD`), and member arguments are keyed by subprogram, so two overloads keep separate parameter lists. Each member is its own routine, with its package in `package_name`. Its body is absent, with the reason that its source is the package's. It is not counted as withheld code.
- **Routine identity:** the key is now `(schema_id, package_name, name, signature)`, so a standalone `SCORE(NUMBER)` and `RISK_PKG.SCORE(NUMBER)` coexist.
- **Native subtypes:** `native_subtype` stores SQL Server's SCALAR, INLINE_TABLE and MULTI_STATEMENT_TABLE, and BigQuery's `*_FUNCTION` kinds, which now store as FUNCTION with the native kind as subtype.
- **Push producers:** both carry through the reserved `attributes.package_name` and `attributes.native_subtype`.
- **Migration** `c4e1a9d3b758` swaps the unique key and converts stored BigQuery kinds. Verified up, down and up with no ORM drift.
- **Evidence:** `tests/test_routine_identity.py` covers the members and overloads round-trip, a member coexisting with a standalone routine, and subtypes; connector, envelope, change-signal, selection, gap, tool and ingestion suites, 260 passed.

Remaining: selection patterns that follow a member's package; PostgreSQL aggregate and window functions (still not discovered); Databricks views and routines; member-level lineage (the package body is parsed as one); a read route and UI for definition history. Original gaps: | Gaps: the Oracle connector captures `PACKAGE`, but `MetadataRoutineEnvelope.routine_type` accepts only FUNCTION/PROCEDURE and packaged-member parameters are skipped; SQL Server collapses FN/IF/TF into FUNCTION; Databricks advertises `views=False`/`routines=False`. Add typed references, package members and immutable definition versions that distinguish exact from structural change. **Queue note:** this needs a new envelope type, which R11-C12 parks; the product owner's 2026-09-15 request reopens C12 for this footprint scope only. Depends on FP02. |
| R11-FP04 | Bounded profile evidence and governed sample access (FP-04) | PARTIAL | Profiling + Security lead | **Split 2026-09-16 (review F06.2).** One BLOCKED row was hiding shippable work: the block is on sample *rows*, and the design authority already separates them -- [the footprint design](../10-architecture/20-database-footprint-and-agent-context.md) says "**separately** design governed sample-row requests" and requires FP-04 to "remain useful when row sampling is disabled". **The value-free half landed 2026-09-16.** `ColumnProfile` gained uniqueness as a stored facet (`distinct_ratio`, `effectively_unique`, `cardinality_class`), blank and whitespace-only counts, a length-bucket distribution and frequency entropy -- **counts only; a bucket boundary, exemplar, mode value or pattern literal is a value and none is persisted**. The 0.98 uniqueness rule that `relationship_validation` and `composite_key_inference` each re-derived differently now has one home. **Observation scope became a stored facet, and two engines were reporting it wrongly**: BigQuery set `row_count_estimate` from `sampled_row_count`, so a `LIMIT`-bounded profile of a large table claimed FULL, and Snowflake issued no bound at all yet reported SAMPLE -- both corrupted the join-approval evidence `ProfileBounds.scope` feeds and the line the Relationships screen renders. A facet no engine can compute now reports a reason (UNSUPPORTED / NOT_APPLICABLE / PERMISSION_DENIED / UNAVAILABLE) instead of a bare null. **FP-04's UI half existed only as a typed, gated, uncalled endpoint**: `GET /v1/tables/{id}/profile` had shipped long before and no screen read it, so every statistic the platform measured was invisible to the people it was measured for. `ProfilePanel` renders it inside the evidence pane, limitation first. Evidence: `tests/test_profiling_facets.py`, `tests/test_connectors_profile_facets.py`, `tests/test_table_profile_api.py`, `ProfilePanel.test.tsx` (6, covering the three rules the component states); the INV-6 naming ratchet and value-freedom suites pass unchanged, with **no new entry in `_COLUMN_NAME_EXEMPTIONS`**, and a new test asserts an APPROVED `ProfilingExceptionPolicy` grants no additional aggregate visibility and no row access -- the anti-inference rule the design states and nothing had enforced. Migration `c9f3b6a1d472`. **Remaining, and still BLOCKED: sample-row access only** -- its own ADR-0014 addendum covering masking, expiry and destinations. No reader for `ColumnValueProfileArtifact` was added: it has none today, and adding one is a new egress of value-bearing data that the capture policy does not cover. The R11-B8 freshness addendum permits exactly one statistic and is not a precedent for rows. **The addendum that would unblock the other half is now drafted, and deliberately not adopted (2026-09-17).** This row had been blocked on "its own ADR-0014 addendum" with no addendum written, so the block had no exit; drafting the exit is not taking it. [ADR-0014](../10-architecture/adr/ADR-0014-value-free-control-plane.md) now carries a **PROPOSED, NOT ADOPTED** section covering the approved-policy scope, per-classification masking before the rows leave the gateway, non-persistence, expiry, the destinations a sampled row may never reach, and the audit record. It is explicit that this is a bigger decision than the freshness addendum it follows rather than a larger instance of the same one: that one permits a single aggregate of a single temporal column, this would permit whole rows of arbitrary columns, and the earlier addendum's own closing sentence says it is not a precedent. It also names the three questions a reviewer has to answer -- whether the purpose justifies the category change at all given the value-free half already ships, whether "nothing is persisted" is enforceable or whether a bounded retention is the more honest contract, and who owns residency. **Remaining:** that decision. Until Architecture and Data Governance accept it, sample-row access stays BLOCKED, which is the correct state rather than a gap. |
| R11-FP05 | Knowledge gaps and bounded investigation plans (FP-05) | PARTIAL | Agents lead | **Gap register landed 2026-09-15.** `aida.footprint_gaps` counts, per datasource the caller may read, every gap the footprint work already records where it occurs, and gives each kind the one route that closes it. The kinds: withheld, truncated or quarantined code; views and routines waiting for the lineage agent; statements lineage cannot read; undecided lineage; source-change holds; unprocessed change signals. The routes: AGENT (a bounded capability of an existing task agent), HUMAN_REVIEW (an existing queue), SOURCE_ACCESS (outside Atlas, never retried), OPERATIONS (a pass that is off or behind), or EXPLAINED (terminal, re-examined only when the source changes). That route is the investigation plan: it runs through machinery that exists, with no new orchestrator and no retry loop. A package is not counted as awaiting lineage. `GET /v1/organizations/{id}/footprint-gaps` serves the register, and the Operations screen shows a Knowledge gaps panel. Evidence: `tests/test_footprint_gaps.py` covers one of each gap, each route, and a denied datasource contributing to no count or total; `OperationsScreen.test.tsx`. **Unresolved callees named, 2026-09-16.** A call `routine_call_descent` could not read through because the callee is not captured here, or its body is withheld, is counted as `LINEAGE_UNRESOLVED_CALLEE` and routed SOURCE_ACCESS: widening the discovery selection or granting read access, then rescanning, closes it. An ambiguous overload, a cycle or the descent bound stays explained, inside the unparsed-statement count it already had. Evidence: `tests/test_footprint_gaps.py` (an actionable callee gap counted apart from a cycle nobody can act on). **Each count expands into its objects, 2026-09-16.** The register told a steward there were seven withheld definitions and left them to find which seven; a source administrator asking for read access had to name the objects and had only a number. `GET /v1/datasources/{id}/footprint-gaps/{kind}` is that list, per source and per kind: identity and the qualified name the catalog already shows, plus the stable code the record itself carries -- a signal type, a hold's severity, the code inside an UNPARSED marker -- and never a definition, a body, or the free text a source wrote about why it withheld something. It is bounded at 200 objects with `truncated` saying when a source has more, because an estate that far behind is closed by a grant or a narrower selection rather than one object at a time. **A caller who may not read the source gets the answer a source that does not exist gets** -- the same `READ_METADATA` gate the summary applies, and a 404 either way, so the route never says a source is there. An unrecognised kind is refused rather than answered with an empty list that would read as none. `SOURCE_OBJECTS_INVISIBLE` is the honest empty case: those objects were counted in the source's own catalog and never read, so there is nothing to name and the route says so in a sentence. In Operations, a count is a button that expands into the list, fetched when asked for rather than with the summary. Evidence: `tests/test_footprint_gap_detail.py` (each kind's objects and codes; another source's objects never in the answer; the unnameable kind's sentence; an unknown kind and a denied source both refused as not found), 9 passed; `OperationsScreen.test.tsx`. **Uncertain grain is a counted gap, 2026-09-18.** `GRAIN_UNCERTAIN` counts every table or view whose row grain the platform cannot establish from evidence it already holds -- a declared key or unique index, or a steward-approved composite key; a pending candidate or profile uniqueness does not count -- routed to human review, with a drill-down code per object (`AGGREGATE_WITHOUT_GROUPING`, `AMBIGUOUS_KEY`, `KEY_UNCONFIRMED`, `KEYS_NOT_READ`, `NO_KEY`). It is the gap that makes an answer double-count. **Remaining:** the agent capability that would propose composite keys for these tables. It waits on the write path, not the register: an agent may write only into a queue `task_agent._HUMAN_ONLY_QUEUES` lists, and `COMPOSITE_KEY_CANDIDATE` has no ADR-0027 tier, so it defaults to T3, above every agent's ceiling -- a framework decision. |
| R11-FP06 | Relationship validation with source-side checks (FP-06) | PARTIAL | Metadata lead | **Validation gate landed 2026-09-15.** `aida.relationship_validation` works out, when a decision is made, what supports a proposed join. It uses only the catalog as it now is and value-free profile counts, and records: evidence classes, key columns, uniqueness per side, cardinality and direction, optionality of the referencing columns (declared and observed), and the profile behind each statistic, marked full or sample. Approval is refused with `RELATIONSHIP_NAME_MATCH_ONLY` unless something links the tables: a declared foreign key, joins in query history, or a key on exactly one side (declared, unique index, approved key candidate or profiled unique) for a column name more specific than a bare identifier. Two keys sharing a name, or a match on `id`, are not evidence. The gate covers single, bulk (a FAILED item) and composite decisions. An approval stores its validation in the candidate's evidence; `GET /v1/relationship-candidates/{id}/validation` and its composite twin compare today's validation with that record (UNCHANGED, CHANGED, CORROBORATION_LOST), and the Relationships detail panel shows it. The referential inclusion check needs a source query and is recorded as NOT_RUN. Fixed on the way: composite-key discovery never set four NOT NULL columns, so every database insert failed; the in-memory test double hid it. Evidence: `tests/test_relationship_validation.py` (the pure evidence matrix, refusals on all three decision paths, a recorded validation losing corroboration when its key is dropped, composite-key discovery on a real session); two relationship review fixtures now declare the target key their rule name claims; 181 tests across the relationship, composite-key and review suites; `RelationshipsScreen.test.tsx`. **Policy note for the Metadata lead:** a declared key on one side counts as corroboration today; say if a source-side signal should also be required. **Object-level authorization 2026-09-15.** Organization membership no longer reads a validation: both validation reads apply the authorization gate to every datasource they read, and a join across data domains is read only under an ACTIVE cross-boundary grant, checked per read. The decision refusal carries the outcome only; the evidence is served by the gated read. Evidence: `tests/test_relationship_validation_authorization.py` (a denying gate answers 403; a cross-domain join is refused without a grant and read with one; the refusal carries no evidence). **Drift acts on approved joins, 2026-09-15.** `aida.relationship_drift` re-validates an approved single or composite join once a scan of its datasource finishes after the join was last decided or checked. A join whose corroboration is lost, or a column it joins on is gone, is suspended back to PENDING: every consumer of approved joins (multi-table tool drafting, column-description evidence, impact analysis) reads `status == "APPROVED"` and stops using it, and it is back in the review queue, where approval re-runs the same evidence gate. The approval it held is kept in `evidence["drift"]`. A suspended join whose validation fingerprint again equals the one it was approved on is restored under its original approver. A join whose evidence only changed stays approved, as the validation read already reports. The suspension is a safety action, like a source-change hold, never a decision on anyone's behalf. It runs in the context rebuild pass, and such organizations are brought into the scheduled pass. **Fixed on the way:** validation counted a retired column as present, so a join could be approved, or stay approved, on a column the source dropped; only ACTIVE columns count now. Evidence: `tests/test_relationship_drift.py` (nothing checked before a newer scan and once per scan; a dropped key suspends and its return restores the original approval; a changed join stays approved while one whose column left is suspended; a composite join; the rebuild pass and organization selection); relationship, composite-key, multi-table, knowledge-graph, rebuild and column-description suites, 188 passed. **Batching landed 2026-09-17**, following `resolve_mapping_targets`' precedent of splitting the batched read from the pure evaluation: at most six reads whatever the batch size, measured on live PostgreSQL at 200 candidates as **1,200 statements to 6** and **1,416ms to 54ms**, with the verdicts pinned identical across six shapes and authorization, the grant check and maker-checker untouched -- a performance change, not a semantics change. **Remaining:** the inclusion check only, still a source-side query gated on R11-FP04's undecided sample-access policy. Original scope: \| Record composite keys, direction, cardinality, optionality and sample bounds for proposed joins; a name match alone never becomes an approved join. Confidence calibration stays with R11-C10 (BLOCKED on a labelled corpus). Depends on FP04/FP05. **Object-level authorization completed 2026-09-15 (`db4a1c1`).** The gates the validation read applies now also gate the decision: single, bulk and composite approvals authorize every datasource they read and require an ACTIVE cross-boundary grant, so approving is no longer the way around a read the gates refuse, and a cross-domain join is never created without a grant. The grant check names its edge kind, so a grant covering only dbt dependencies no longer admits a proposed join. The validation an approval records is served by the gated read alone: `public_relationship_evidence` is what the candidate lists, the review queue, a decision response and the lineage and graph edges serve, applied at the read models so a new surface inherits it. Evidence: `tests/test_relationship_validation_authorization.py`; relationship, lineage, graph and composite suites, 181 passed. |
| R11-FP07 | PL/pgSQL and remaining program lineage (FP-07) | PARTIAL | Engineering | **Landed 2026-09-15.** The footprint sample pack measured these gaps against live PostgreSQL 17.10; each was silent or wrong rather than merely incomplete. `CREATE TEMP TABLE ... ON COMMIT DROP AS` fell back to an opaque `Command`. A plain `CREATE TEMP TABLE ... AS` was never treated as an intermediate, so no end-to-end edge reached the table the procedure writes. `EXECUTE format(...)` and `EXECUTE v_sql` were reported as nested calls to procedures named `format` and `v_sql`. `PERFORM`, `RETURN QUERY` and `EXCEPTION WHEN` did not parse, so every set-returning PL/pgSQL function lost its result query. `SELECT ... INTO v` was a write to a table named `v`. `FOR rec IN SELECT ... LOOP` discarded its query silently. What changed, for a routine whose declared language is plpgsql: temp tables are intermediates with transitive edges; `EXECUTE` is `DYNAMIC_SQL`; `PERFORM fn()` is a nested-call gap; `RETURN QUERY` is the result set and a blueprint candidate; `SELECT INTO`, `:=` and query `PERFORM` read into `<LOCAL>`, which is never proposed or resolved as a table; any dollar tag is recognised (`pg_get_functiondef` returns `$function$`). The capability matrix is regenerated with seven new rows. T-SQL `EXEC` and `SELECT INTO #t` keep their meaning. **This pass also exposed R11-D16.** Evidence: `tests/test_procedure_lineage_plpgsql.py` includes stored-form equivalence: the redacted text the lineage agent reads yields the same edges as the raw body. The sample pack reruns on stored forms, and PostgreSQL and SQL Server now both produce the `orders.discount -> customer_totals.net_revenue` transitive edge. **Nested calls read through, 2026-09-15.** `aida.routine_call_descent` resolves a `CALL`, `EXEC` or `PERFORM` gap to the routine captured under that name in the caller's datasource (a qualified name by its schema; a bare name in the caller's schema first, then unique across the datasource; an overload is AMBIGUOUS), parses the callee through the same body gate, and descends at most three levels and 25 bodies, never through a routine already on the call path. The callee's edges become the caller's at the call site, marked `via_routine` (migration `f4a8c1d7e236`, verified up, down and up on scratch PostgreSQL; the parse route returns it), at most PARTIAL; a PostgreSQL callee's result set is local to the caller, while SQL Server's `EXEC` streams it. The gap goes only when the callee was fully parsed all the way down; otherwise it stays and names why (NOT_CAPTURED, AMBIGUOUS, BODY_WITHHELD, CYCLE, DEPTH_LIMIT, CALLEE_LIMIT, CALLEE_NOT_FULLY_PARSED). The lineage agent and a person's parse both use it; tool generation still refuses a routine with a nested call, because reading a call through proves what it touches, not that invoking it is safe. Evidence: `tests/test_routine_call_descent.py` (read through at the call site; each reason a gap stays; local against streamed result sets; the lineage agent proposing a callee's edges on the caller); `tests/test_nested_callee_lineage_live.py`, where the sample pack's `nested_revenue` is read through `refresh_totals` on live PostgreSQL and `read_revenue` on live SQL Server with no gap left; lineage agent, procedure lineage, review, blueprint, OpenAPI and ORM-drift suites, 130 passed. **Table functions, anonymous blocks and a live agent run, 2026-09-16.** A `FROM s.fn(...)` source was read as a table named after the function's *schema* -- a wrong fact, stated silently. It is now an intermediate named after the function, with an explicit TABLE_FUNCTION_READ gap, and `routine_call_descent` reads it through the captured routine, so the function's own sources reach whatever the caller writes through the hop propagation that already existed. A T-SQL inline table-valued function's body (`AS RETURN (...)`) and a PL/pgSQL anonymous block (`DO $$ ... $$`) parse like any other body. A statement whose only source is one table now attributes its unqualified columns to it, so `SELECT customer_id FROM s.customer_revenue` is lineage rather than an unresolved source; a statement's own target is not a source, so an INSERT ... SELECT from one table counts. The capability matrix is regenerated with the two new recognition rules. Evidence: `tests/test_routine_call_descent.py` (a function source is never a table; read through to what it selects from; an overload keeps its gap; an anonymous block reads like a body); `tests/test_nested_callee_lineage_live.py`, where on live SQL Server the caller's write traces back to `customer_revenue` through the inline function while PostgreSQL's overloaded `customer_net` stays an honest AMBIGUOUS gap, and the lineage agent runs against the live estate and proposes what it read -- the live run this row asked for; parser, agent, blueprint, gaps, review, coverage, steward, benchmark and matrix suites, 291 passed. **Also fixed:** the sample-pack test and README still said an ontology mapping to a ROUTINE is refused, which FP09 made valid. **Bounded coverage reporting landed 2026-09-16 (review F06.4).** The review's rule was "never label every path understood merely because an object was inventoried", and three things were being labelled understood. `is_fully_parsed` and `is_read_only` were computed and returned but **stored nowhere**, so "was this routine fully understood?" had to be re-derived by hunting for UNPARSED edges; a per-object parse-coverage record now persists it alongside the statement count and the unparsed reasons, building on the per-edge `unparsed_reason` rather than duplicating it (migration `a3f71c58d94b`, route `GET /v1/datasources/{id}/procedures/{routine_id}/parse-coverage`). dbt **macros and hooks are now visible as an explicit limitation**: `macros` is still deliberately not ingested, so a model's `depends_on.macros` count and a project's `on-run-start`/`on-run-end`/`pre_hook`/`post_hook` presence are reported as coverage evidence, and nothing claims a macro's expansion was resolved into lineage -- claiming that resolution is exactly the failure this finding warns about. **Precise source mapping is recorded as unsupported, with its reason**, rather than left ambiguous: the only positional information anywhere is `statement_ordinal`, an integer statement index, and the capability matrix says so. Evidence: `tests/test_routine_parse_coverage.py`, `tests/test_dbt_macro_hook_coverage.py`. **Source-range maps exist, 2026-09-18.** Every lineage edge now carries the line and column span, in the **stored redacted body**, of the statement it came from, with a `statement_text_digest` so a reader can tell whether the range is still current -- the stored T-SQL body is re-rendered by redaction, so the same INSERT sits at line 8 column 9 in the source and line 2 column 77 in what is stored, and a test pins it. An unparsed statement reads `GAP_STATEMENT` with the span of the unreadable text; a body that could not be reached reads `NOT_LOCATED` with every position NULL, never zero. Building it also found and fixed five parser defects, the worst being that a `CASE` expression inside a procedure body **cut the body short and silently dropped every later statement** from lineage. See R11-D24 for the redaction defect the parser work exposed. **Token-grain ranges, 2026-09-19 (`6777681`).** Every routine and trigger lineage edge can now point at the exact token of each end -- the column read (or the table, for `*` edges) and the column or table written -- beside the statement range, as `source_token_range` and `target_token_range` (`kind`, `start_offset`, `end_offset`) on both deep-lineage routes. Offsets index the stored redacted body, the same text as the statement range, and alignment is checked rather than assumed: every identifier in a statement must slice out of the stored text unchanged, or none of that statement's tokens are kept. A token is recorded only when exactly one reference can be the evidence -- a table named twice, a column read twice in one expression and a MERGE naming its target column in both branches all read NULL, never the first occurrence. Three PL/pgSQL rewrites (`ON COMMIT`, `INTO STRICT`, `RETURNING ... INTO`) now blank text instead of cutting it, so nothing after them moves. Migration `cd1d15b6705b` adds six nullable columns to each edge table, no backfill; up, down and up and the ORM drift test were run on a private scratch PostgreSQL database. Evidence: `tests/test_r11_fp07_token_ranges.py` (20; 19 failed on the previous tree, the twentieth calls the new module directly). **Wrong facts found alongside, fixed in `9c73ef3`** (see R11-D29). **Loop records, result cursors and column-less reads, 2026-09-19.** A cursor FOR loop's query was recorded as the routine's result set and the loop's writes had no source. Each loop's rows are now routine-local state of their own (`<LOCAL:rec@N>`, bound from the header to its `END LOOP`, two loops reusing `rec` never joined); inside the loop `rec.col` reads that state, so the hop pass links the loop query's sources to what the loop writes, for inline queries and declared cursors alike, and tool generation no longer offers a loop's query as the output. Catalog descent renames a callee's records (`<LOCAL:schema.callee:rec@N>`) -- without it a caller's and callee's `rec` collided into false edges, which a test reproduced. `OPEN c FOR <query>` on a ref cursor handed back to the caller (an OUT/IN OUT `SYS_REFCURSOR`, a `REF CURSOR` type the text declares, a PL/pgSQL OUT/INOUT `refcursor`, or what a function RETURNs) is now the result set, as `RETURN QUERY` is, so such a procedure qualifies for tool generation the same way. A table a statement reads without naming a column gets one `TABLE_ROWS` edge (`*` at both ends, PARTIAL, token-grained under the one-reference rule), never from the write target, an INTO variable, a CTE, a table function or `DUAL`; query-bearing IF/ELSIF/WHILE conditions are read (context `CONDITION`). Fixed on the way: in T-SQL an `END` followed by the next statement's `IF`/`WHILE` was read as `END IF`/`END WHILE`, parsing the whole definition as the body. Re-parse impact: loop edges move from `<RESULT>` to the record, so decided edges into `<RESULT>` go SUPERSEDED through R11-FP05's reconciliation. No migration (`TABLE_ROWS` is a new value in an unconstrained `String(30)`); the procedure and engine capability matrices regenerated ("7 of 42"). Verified: `tests/test_r11_fp07_loop_records_and_result_cursors.py` 44 (30 of the first 42 failed on the previous parser; the other 12 are guards), 13 of 13 mutants caught; the 22 lineage suites 608 passed at integration on a clean worktree of `eff507e` plus this change. Live lineage tests not re-run. **Remaining:** positional loop targets (`FOR a, b IN`), explicit `FETCH c INTO rec` loops, a PL/pgSQL record's value after its loop, a bound cursor opened and then RETURNed, `FOREACH` loops (an inner `END LOOP` can close the wrong loop), and ref cursor types declared in another package are not modelled; a table named only in a JOIN or MERGE `ON` clause still has no edge; T-SQL `SET @v = (SELECT ...)` is dropped as lineage-free; two table-function markers in one statement share a statement number, so `_dedupe_edges` keeps one; a `count(*)` view is still edge-less (the view parser is unchanged). |
| R11-FP08 | Evidence-backed routine and view descriptions (FP-08) | PARTIAL | Semantics lead | **Views landed 2026-09-15.** Views already received table description drafts, and every one of them opened "X is a table" without ever looking at the captured definition. Evidence (`asset_description_service.AssetEvidence`) now records the object kind and the definition's state: CAPTURED, TRUNCATED, QUARANTINED, WITHHELD or NOT_CAPTURED. It also stores a digest of the stored value-free definition the draft was written against. The draft names a view or materialized view as such, says what its definition state lets a reader rely on, and never quotes the definition or one of its constants. For scoring, a captured definition counts as a view's structural evidence, because views rarely declare keys and were marked down as keyless tables. Steward-agent and manual drafts pick this up unchanged, through the same service. Evidence: `tests/test_asset_description_views.py` covers all five states against SQLite, with no definition text in the draft; `tests/test_asset_description.py` covers the new composition, scoring and payload cases. **Routines: needs a decision.** A routine has nowhere to store an Atlas-authored description (`AssetDocumentation` is keyed by `table_id`), so it needs a draft, a versioned store and a review type. That is a new governed-artifact family, which R11-S4 freezes, so it waits on architecture sign-off that it extends the description-draft family. Packages wait on FP03. **Moved definitions refused 2026-09-15.** Approving a view's draft rechecks the definition's state and digest. A draft written against a definition that has since moved is refused with `DEFINITION_MOVED` (409; a FAILED item in a bulk decision; skipped by the reviewer agent), so it can only be rejected and drafted again. Evidence: `tests/test_asset_description_views.py` (a moved definition refused; a standing view and a table published); the description, steward, reviewer and governance suites, 194 passed. **Routines gained a description of their own, 2026-09-16 (review F06.1).** The blocker recorded on this row was that `AssetDocumentation` is keyed by `table_id`, so a routine had nowhere to store an Atlas-authored description. That is now answered the way R11-S4's freeze permits -- by extending the existing description family, not by building a second governance engine. `RoutineDocumentation`, `RoutineDocumentationVersion` and `RoutineDescriptionDraft` follow the shape the table and column pairs already use, and the version additionally names the `metadata_routine_definition_version` its prose was written against: routines have a real immutable definition-version table that views lack, so the description can cite the body rather than only digest it. Review reuses one adapter entry, one T0 risk tier and the queue's existing decision path; the withdrawal CHECK gained `'ROUTINE'` exactly as `d8a3f1c6b204` added `'ANNOTATION'`. **The body is never quoted** -- it is the largest indirect-injection surface in the estate -- and the five body states reuse `tool_source_binding`'s routine vocabulary rather than a third one. A table-less object had no authorization precedent in this family; it follows `ontology_api`, which gates a ROUTINE subject on its datasource. `PACKAGE` is refused by name rather than silently skipped, matching every other routine-consuming path. Deliberately left narrow: `DocumentClaim`, `DocumentMapping` and `ModelImportChange` keep `('TABLE','COLUMN')` -- a data dictionary and the model workbook describe tables and columns. Evidence: `tests/test_routine_description.py`, `tests/test_routine_description_body_states.py` (all five body states, no body text in the draft, `DEFINITION_MOVED`, `PACKAGE` refused), routine cases in `tests/test_description_withdrawal.py`, a row in `tests/test_ar03_false_approval_benchmark.py`, and `ReviewQueueScreen.test.tsx` (the routine row names the routine by its signature and says when the body was not captured). Migration `b7d24e1c8f03`. **Both closed 2026-09-17, which is what makes the feature pay off** -- a reviewed description an agent cannot retrieve, and that no worklist ever asks anyone to write, is a governed artifact nobody reads. An APPROVED routine description now joins the lexical stage with its own reason codes (`BM25_ROUTINE_DESCRIPTION`, `ROUTINE_DESCRIPTION_APPROVED`), carrying a digest and never the prose; only APPROVED ranks, so a draft, a SUPERSEDED and a WITHDRAWN version all rank at nothing. No new channel, per R11-S3. The routine worklist ranks on a **borrowed usage** signal rather than a copy of the table one: nobody queries a procedure, so a table-shaped score rates every routine zero, while the query volume of the tables its ACTIVE lineage says it *writes* is a real measure of whether describing it matters. Exposed as `subject_type` on the existing endpoint, with table behaviour byte-identical. `PACKAGE` is refused by name in SQL and in the ranker. The quality-benchmark gate is unmoved and its baseline deliberately not accepted (the run reports `path=LIVE_EMBED`, so the figure depends on a provider being configured -- the R11-FP09 lesson). **The steward draws from the ranking now, 2026-09-17.** `_undescribed_routines` ordered by name and truncated, so on any estate larger than the run's limit the agent described whatever sorted first. Its docstring **argued for** that, and the argument was sound: "no equivalent usage signal is keyed by routine anywhere in this codebase, and inventing one would be a priority order that looks measured and is not... until a real routine-demand signal exists". That precondition was met by this row's own ranker, so the capability is wired to it and the docstring now records the **expiry** rather than the argument -- a stale rationale is how the next person re-derives the wrong answer. One property is deliberately preserved: usage is a *term of a product*, so a routine whose written tables nobody has touched scores zero, sorts behind every routine with a signal and tie-breaks by name -- **an estate with no query history is worked in exactly the order it was before**, and arbitrary order is confined to what there is nothing to order by. The *retired*-description exclusion is not delegated to the worklist and the docstring says why: a WITHDRAWN version is not APPROVED, so the worklist puts it back in front of a human -- right there, wrong here. No new scan budget: the same two volume aggregates the table capability already pays for in the same run. Evidence: `tests/test_steward_routine_worklist.py` (10), of which 4 fail on the pre-change file. **The vector stage reads approved descriptions too, 2026-09-18.** One composer, `vector_index_service.compose_vector_texts`, now builds embedded text for both the persisted index and the live path, with a test that the two agree for the same object -- the invariant that had silently diverged before. A changed or withdrawn description is caught by comparing text fingerprints at query time, so a stale vector is embedded live from current text from the next question on, and a withdrawn description stops steering retrieval. Previously dropped candidates -- routines and columns discovered after the last build, and glossary terms -- now get a vector score. **Remaining:** the index has **never held a glossary term**: its builder filters on a status value (`PUBLISHED`) that nothing in the code writes, so glossary candidates are always embedded live and never indexed. Nothing goes unscored, but it costs a provider call per term per question. Packages still wait on FP03. |
| R11-FP09 | Typed ontology mappings and ontology-bound context (FP-09) | PARTIAL | Semantics lead | **Typed mappings and drift landed 2026-09-15.** An ontology concept can now be mapped to a VIEW (a view or materialized view) or a ROUTINE (a stored procedure or function), as well as a TABLE or COLUMN. The kind must match the catalog object, so a view written as TABLE, or a table written as VIEW, is refused. `resolve_mapping_targets` checks every mapping in at most three batched reads and reports VALID, TARGET_MISSING (another organization's object reads the same as one that never existed), TARGET_DEPRECATED (with the successor a rename detection recorded) or KIND_MISMATCH. **Defect fixed:** the version listing re-validated every mapping on every read and raised on the first bad one, so deprecating a single mapped table made the whole ontology history answer 422. The listing now reports `mapping_validity` per version instead. Create, submit and approve still require every mapping VALID and readable; a routine is authorized against its datasource. The ontology manager's help text names the four kinds, and each version shows its mapping drift. Evidence: `tests/test_ontology_typed_mappings.py` covers all four kinds, kind mismatch in both directions, a foreign routine, and history staying readable with drift reported; ontology suites, 17 passed; `OntologyManager.test.tsx`, 5 passed. **Ontology-bound retrieval also landed 2026-09-15.** An ONTOLOGY_CONCEPT candidate joins the lexical stage in `hybrid_retrieve` (no new channel, per R11-S3). It is drawn only from each ontology's published, APPROVED version: never a draft, a deprecated concept or a deprecated ontology. It is scored on name, aliases and description with no boost. A concept stands only on mappings still valid in the asking datasource: ACTIVE, of the declared kind, per the rule now shared as `aida.ontology_kinds`. The graph stage adds ONTOLOGY_CONCEPT_MAPS_TABLE edges, so a question worded the business's way reaches the table the ontology says carries that meaning. The trust stage scores the concept on those tables. The SQL model is given the mapped tables. The approved version's id travels in the hit metadata, which grounding receipts already hash. Evidence: `tests/test_retrieval_ontology_concepts.py` covers invalid mappings (a view mapped as TABLE, a deprecated table, another datasource's table), draft and deprecated exclusion, graph expansion, model context, and moving the published pointer; retrieval, orchestrator and ontology suites, 146 passed. **Context products bind ontology versions (2026-09-15).** `ontology_version_ids` is a sixth reference group (migration `7b4e2d9a6c13`, verified up, down and up on scratch PostgreSQL). It is pinned by version id, so a later publication never changes what an existing product says. Validation accepts only APPROVED versions of the product's own organization. A product binding none fingerprints and compiles exactly as before. Bound ids reach the compiled references and MCP's `governed_references`. Create draft gains an ontology-version picker that offers only approved versions. **Found and fixed by the surface-control gate:** the earlier drift change had also dropped the per-target read authorization from the version listing. It is restored through the same helper the writes use, so reporting drift never becomes a way to learn about objects the caller may not read. Evidence: `tests/test_context_product_ontology_binding.py`; context product, compiler, MCP, Studio, surface and ontology suites, 360 passed; UI, 16 passed. **Pinned meaning delivered, 2026-09-15.** No retrieval runs inside a context product: Ask, the governed retriever and MCP are scoped by datasource and use each ontology's published version by design. What was missing was the meaning itself: a consumer received only the bound version ids, so the only meaning it could read was the ontology's current one. `context_product_coverage.load_ontology_meaning` now reads each bound version, never the head, and the compile, download and drift routes (Atlas-native targets only) and MCP's context-product read carry it as an `ontology` section. Each version gives its key, version, name and lifecycle; its non-deprecated concepts with aliases and with mappings cut to the product's own tables, their columns and its routines; and the relations between delivered concepts. Free text passes egress screening, and withheld text is reported with its verdict (AR-10). Compile refuses (409) a version that no longer resolves as approved in the organization, as it does an unresolved routine; MCP's read shows only the versions that still resolve. A product binding none compiles byte-identically. Evidence: `tests/test_context_product_ontology_meaning.py` (Atlas-native targets only; nothing bound unchanged; the bound version read over a later publication; scope cutting; withheld text; another organization's version); context product, compiler, MCP, exemplar and envelope suites, 129 passed. Remaining: package-member mappings, which wait on FP03; live ontology publication, which is still unverified (see the R11-C1 note). |
| R11-FP10 | Claim corrections and rejection suppression (FP-10) | PARTIAL | Governance lead | **Rejection suppression landed 2026-09-15.** Corrections and reversals stay R11-C8's withdrawal and reinstatement lifecycle in the existing queue; there is no second approval engine. Suppression used to compare only `text_fingerprint`, and three kinds of refused proposal came back. (1) An edited then rejected draft: the edit rewrote the fingerprint, so the original machine text returned. It is now also matched on `original_fingerprint`. (2) The same facts in new words, for example after FP08's view wording: a digest of the evidence signals, ignoring who drafted, the run, rank, edit history and list order, now refuses them until the evidence changes. (3) Words approved once and then withdrawn are not proposed again. The steward agent reports these as `identical_text_withdrawn`. For columns, `origin` is part of the evidence, so a rejected metadata draft does not suppress a model rewrite of the same facts. The steward agent and both generate routes (table and column) use this single rule (`asset_description_service.refusal_reason`). Evidence: `tests/test_description_refusal.py`; the steward, description and reviewer suites. A view draft whose definition moved is now refused at approval (FP08, 2026-09-15). Relationship proposals are covered twice over: a rejected one is suppressed by N4 negative knowledge, and R11-FP06 refuses a name-only approval. **Glossary-link proposals decided, 2026-09-15.** They need no second rule. A proposal's key is (table, term, annotation), and a table carries at most one business annotation, which follows the table through a merged rename (`identity_merge`) and is not deleted by any path; so a link a reviewer rejected, or approved and a steward later removed, is never proposed again, in any status, even after the annotation's labels change. The description rule's other cases do not arise, because a link has no wording to vary. The one deliberate difference: a refused link does not return when its evidence changes (a new label or term version), which would need a new proposal key; a steward links it directly instead. Evidence: `tests/test_glossary_stewardship.py` (a rejected link and a removed one, both with changed labels, are not proposed again). |
| R11-FP11 | Routines and views reachable in retrieval and MCP (FP-11) | PARTIAL | Retrieval lead | **Landed 2026-09-15 (`f5c091a`).** ROUTINE is an eighth `hybrid_retrieve` candidate, added inside the lexical stage with no new channel (R11-S3). It is found by name, parameter names and the source's description, never ranked by its body. Its tables come only from ACTIVE procedure-lineage edges, so an agent's undecided proposal steers nothing, and it gets no boost. Downstream: the graph stage adds ROUTINE_READS_TABLE and ROUTINE_WRITES_TABLE edges, so a question reaches a table none of its words names; the trust stage scores the routine on those tables; the SQL model gets the tables, never the routine as a callable; MCP `resolve_entity` accepts ROUTINE. **Found and raised as a separate task:** `SqlGuard` does not refuse a user-defined function called in a SELECT list, and SQL Server's `readonly` flag is only a client hint. Evidence: `tests/test_retrieval_routines.py`, including graph expansion end to end; retrieval, MCP and orchestrator suites, 265 passed. **A view is found by what it is built from, 2026-09-16.** The lexical stage scores a view's stored definition, so a view named `v_rev_ltd` that selects `net_revenue` from `orders` answers the revenue question it used to miss. Only the value-free stored text is read, and only where screening allows it: a quarantined definition and one the source withheld are never scored. The hit carries a digest of the definition, never the text. A definition match scores at 0.6 of its overlap and never displaces a name match already found, because it says what a view is built from rather than what it is. The candidate query now also fetches a table whose source description matches -- the name-only prefilter never returned one, so its description could not score however well it read -- and the hit says which matched (`BM25_TABLE_DESCRIPTION`). Evidence: `tests/test_retrieval_view_definitions.py` (found by its definition; ranked under a name match; a digest, not the text; quarantined and withheld never scored; another datasource's view never read; a description-only table fetched); retrieval, ranking, stages, orchestrator, MCP, benchmark and journey suites, 217 passed. **Routines joined the persisted index, 2026-09-16.** They were the one candidate kind the vector index did not carry, so every question that reached a routine paid a provider call to embed it live -- the coverage gap `retrieval_vector_index_gap` exists to report. `_indexable_objects` now embeds each ACTIVE routine under the same text the live path composes for it (`ROUTINE schema.name`, its display name), so the move changes what a routine costs to rank and never where it ranks; a retired routine is left out, as a deprecated table is. Evidence: `tests/test_vector_index_service.py` (the indexed text pinned by its hash, a DEPRECATED routine never embedded); vector index, store, schedule, stages and routine-retrieval suites, 56 passed. A context product now scopes routines as well as tables and tool versions, so a routine reachable by a question is not evidence outside the product that references it (FP12, 2026-09-16). |
| R11-FP12 | Typed object references in context products (FP-12) | PARTIAL | Context lead | **Landed 2026-09-15.** Context products can now name stored procedures and functions, and they report what those routines and their views depend on. A version gains `routine_ids`, a fifth governed reference group (migration `5d7a3c9e1f24`, verified up, down and up on scratch PostgreSQL). Validation matches the other groups: only ACTIVE routines on the product's own project, otherwise the create is refused with a 422. Views need no list of their own: a view is already a table id. `aida.context_product_coverage` resolves both kinds for every door a version is read through (compile, download, drift, and MCP's resource read), and MCP renders them with the compiler's `coverage_section`, so the two doors cannot disagree. Each routine reports its identity and status; whether its body would be released (same gate as `get_transformation_detail`); lineage ACTIVE, PROPOSED or NONE; whether parsing was complete; the tables it reads and writes; and a digest of the stored value-free text.
