**Added 2026-09-15: database footprint program.** Rows R11-FP01–R11-FP18 match the FP task numbers in the [database footprint design](../10-architecture/20-database-footprint-and-agent-context.md#16-product-enhancement-backlog-api-ui-and-incremental-understanding) one-to-one; R11-D16 is the defect its first sample exposed. They are not part of the 2026-09-12 counts above. The design's [queue reconciliation](../10-architecture/20-database-footprint-and-agent-context.md#queue-reconciliation-2026-09-15) records how they sit against existing rows: FP03 reopens C12's "new envelope types" for this scope only; B16, B5 and C10 keep incremental discovery, certification and calibration; S3 limits FP11 to existing retrieval stages; S4 holds FP10/FP14 to existing lifecycles.

**Added 2026-09-19: the third build cycle.** Section P's remainders again, and **five new defects (R11-D23 to D27)**, each found while building something else rather than by a test that failed -- which is the recurring shape worth recording. Three were silent in the specific sense that every test was green: a product boundary that admitted any kind it did not recognise (D23), redaction that dropped dynamic SQL and so made a routine read as understood (D24), and pinned meaning that refused the meaning it pinned, which passed only because its test built the metadata by hand (D25). Two agents were cut off by a session rate limit mid-cycle; one had been mutation-testing, so the working tree was checked for a deliberately broken file before anything was built on it, and the redaction fix -- which touches INV-6 code -- was done directly rather than handed to an agent that could stop half-way through it. **Where the work is in history:** nearly all of it -- 76 files, the fail-closed boundary, trigger propagation, pushdown and vendor-code refusals, meaning signals, source ranges and both migrations -- was committed in `8be0771` by a session that staged the whole working tree, under the message "Add tests for downstream lineage and vector routine descriptions", which describes two test files. Nothing was lost and the content was covered by the full suite, but that commit's message does not say what it contains; the rows above are the accurate record. This is the hazard CLAUDE.md rule 4 names, and it recurred because every session shares one git identity, so the author field cannot say which session swept the tree. The two fixes made after it (R11-D24, D25) are in `b76842d`.

**Added 2026-09-17: the follow-on build cycle.** Seven parallel workstreams against section P's own remainders, then a finishing pass. What is worth recording beyond the rows themselves is that **two of the features were half-built when their workstream ended, and the halves were invisible from the outside**: trigger and sequence discovery built row shapes that nothing persisted, and the per-facet refusal mechanism was complete and wired while no adapter called it, so a real source's refusal still failed the run. Both were found by reading the handover notes rather than by a failing test, because neither gap breaks anything a test asserts -- a feature that discovers and discards is green all the way down. The agents that left them said so plainly in their reports, which is the only reason they were closed the same day. Three **reverse-guards** -- tests written to assert a gap exists and to fail when it closes -- did their job and were flipped to positive assertions. R11-D18 is new, for a defect in the shipped bootstrap that two sessions hit independently.

**Added 2026-09-16: the 2026-09-16 review, folded in.** The [product, implementation and deployment review](../review-2026-09-16/REVIEW.md) was measured against `bf9b909`, and three of its findings were already closed by commits that landed after that baseline: F02 (a routine hit outside a product is refused -- the review's own reproduction was re-run and now answers `admitted=False`), F09 (the retrieval calibration test passes) and the request-wiring half of F08. Its remaining findings are carried on the rows they belong to rather than in a second queue: F01 and F08 on R11-FP12, F03 on the new R11-D17, F04 and F05 on R11-D17's runbook and on R11-B10, F06.1 on R11-FP08, F06.2 on R11-FP04 (split from BLOCKED to PARTIAL, because one blocked row was hiding shippable value-free work), F06.3 on R11-FP01/FP02, F06.4 on R11-FP07, F06.5 on R11-FP13, F06.6 on R11-FP17, and §5 on R11-FP01. [The reconciliation](25-review-reconciliation-2026-09-16.md) records the disposition of every finding and what was deliberately not done. **Row convention, per F07:** a row touched from 2026-09-16 reads as implemented / wired / configured / verified, then exactly one `Remaining:` clause, with historical evidence left in place rather than rewritten -- the older dated paragraphs are evidence, and a claim in one can be stale without being wrong. Rows not touched this cycle keep their existing shape and are normalised on touch, the same convention R11-P10 and R11-S12 already use; reformatting 103 rows in one pass would have rewritten evidence nobody re-measured.

Bank decisions BD-1?BD-12 and drill requirements in sections I/J remain open requirements,
not cancelled features. Their current execution owners are B5/B6/B9/B10/B15/C6/C9/C11/C12/C13
as applicable. A deferred optional integration does not waive a required release control.

### 22-item context and workspace assessment: 2026-09-17

The [22-item assessment and API/UI acceptance design](../10-architecture/22-context-enrichment-and-review-workspace.md) maps every request to this queue. This is design/pickup scope, not a completion claim. Existing FP04 owns key-aware distinct profiling and policy-aware distribution UI; FP06/07 own join and lineage evidence; FP08/09 own descriptions and provenance presentation; FP12 and OKF02 own persona projections of approved context; S13 owns Stewardship/Playbooks grouping and the logical Agent Gateway hub. UX16, GQL01/02 and OKF01/02/03 retain their existing remainders. OpenRouter evaluation stays under C11 and encoder evaluation under FP11; neither is a new required integration. FP18 native routine invocation remains deferred. Automatic tool draft creation stays FP14 and does not imply automatic publication.

Only two new delivery slices are added:

| ID | Task | Status | Owner | Evidence and remaining work |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| R11-SQL01 | Shared generated/pasted SQL review workspace (items 9/10) | PARTIAL | Query gateway + Frontend | **Implemented:** commit `53c5ab1` adds generation-only draft/validation, caller-bound expiring SQL receipts, explicit Run, edit invalidation, product/workspace binding and conditional single-use execution through the gateway. **Wired:** Ask embeds SqlWorkspace; generated and pasted SQL share the draft/run APIs. **Configured:** receipt migration `f4b8d2a6c913` applied and local API/UI/workers refreshed on 2026-09-18. **Verified:** current OpenAPI/workspace/Markdown-safety tests (50 combined) passed; complete frontend suite passed 954 tests; migration-ORM drift passed; new PostgreSQL and SQL Server footprint journey assertions prove pasted SQL validates without an execution, Run returns expected revenue and receipt retry cannot execute twice. Deployment parity reports eight matches and zero drift (423 paths, 539 schemas, 273 settings). **Verified 2026-09-18 on the deployed stack, no model calls:** a pasted-SQL API journey on Northwind's PostgreSQL and SQL Server sources passed 32 of 32 checks -- no `query_execution` row before Run, an edited statement 409 `REVALIDATION_REQUIRED`, another principal 403 `RECEIPT_NOT_YOURS`, exactly one execution on Run, a second Run 409 naming it, a `DELETE` refused with no receipt, and the statement's literal absent from the receipt and every `sql_draft.*` audit record; the browser journey (Ask, Review SQL first: paste, Validate, edit locks Run, validate again, Run returns rows, Run locks again) was walked on the deployed UI and found the badge still reading "Valid -- not run" after the run, fixed with a regression in `SqlWorkspace.test.tsx`; the full backend suite on the `53c5ab1` tree passed 12,188 with 0 failed (176 skipped, 1 xfailed). **History added 2026-09-18, deployed 2026-09-19:** `GET /v1/datasources/{datasource_id}/sql-drafts` lists the caller's own receipts there, newest first -- redacted shape, state and execution, never a literal -- and the workspace shows them as "Your recent reviewed SQL", read again after every validate and run (`test_r11_sql01_sql_workspace.py` 22 passed; `SqlWorkspace.test.tsx` 11 passed); on the deployed stack the list returned the earlier live run's receipts with no literal, the deployed workspace showed the browser journey's statements as "Ran" and "Expired", and the 32-check pasted-SQL journey passed again. **Parameter values added 2026-09-19 (not yet deployed):** a pasted statement names `:placeholders` and sends `parameters` (name, governed-tool type STRING/INTEGER/NUMBER/BOOLEAN/DATE, value; at most 50) beside the text on both `sql-drafts` and `run`; sent with a question it is a 422. Values are bound by the governed-tool binder `render_tool_sql`: each placeholder node of the parsed statement becomes one escaped, typed literal, and the gateway validates and executes that bound statement, so an injection attempt inside a value stays one string. JSON values are not coerced (`"5"` for an INTEGER is a mismatch). The receipt digest covers the declared types and a keyed `sign_value` fingerprint of the values -- the one a tool execution records -- so another value, type or name at Run is 409 `REVALIDATION_REQUIRED`; a raw statement's digest is unchanged, so existing receipts still match. No value is stored: the receipt keeps the template's redacted shape and the `sql_draft.validated`/`sql_draft.run` audits carry only `parameter_count` and the fingerprint. A value that does not bind is a 200 with a blocking `PARAMETER_*` finding (`UNDECLARED`, `UNUSED`, `VALUE_MISSING`, `TYPE_MISMATCH`, `TOO_LONG`, `INVALID`) naming the parameter, never the value, and no receipt. **Fixed on the way:** a placeholder nobody declared used to pass the guard and earn a receipt; it is now refused before the gateway. The workspace gains a Parameters editor (declare from the SQL, per-type messages, Validate held until values fit, a value change locks Run with a revalidation notice). Verified: `tests/test_r11_sql01_parameters.py` 49, 10 of 12 mutants caught (two equivalent: the Run-time refusal the digest also catches, and strict value types that match pydantic's JSON union handling); `tests/test_r11_sql01_parameters_live.py` 2 on real PostgreSQL and SQL Server, none skipped -- an INTEGER and a NUMBER return customer 1's 150.0, a changed value is refused with no execution, `East' OR '1'='1` returns 0 rows where a splicing binder returns both customers, and a scan of the platform database finds no value; `tests/test_r11_sql01_sql_workspace.py` 23; `SqlWorkspace.test.tsx` 18 and `SqlWorkspaceParameters.test.tsx` 25, all 7 UI mutants caught; OpenAPI baseline and `ui-next/src/lib/types.ts` regenerated with the change. **Remaining:** generated-SQL live-model acceptance and the model-drafted half of the browser journey (both need paid model calls, not authorised); a deployed browser walk-through of the parameter editor; final UX16 visual/reflow acceptance; and driver-level bind parameters, which need a parameter channel through `QueryExecutionGateway` and every connector (`execute_read_query` takes SQL text only, so values bind as escaped literals in the parsed statement, the governed-tool binding). Noticed, not fixed: the gateway's redactor leaves PostgreSQL `TRUE`/`FALSE` in stored `normalized_sql` (sqlglot does not treat booleans as literals; `sql_redaction.py`), and a raw statement's `statement_digest` is an unkeyed sha256 over text that includes its literals, so a short literal could be confirmed by guessing -- `sign_value` would close it. Generated-model unit fixtures do not close the live-model criterion. See [verification follow-through](27-functional-review-fixes-2026-09-18.md). |
| R11-REV01 | Scale evidence-based review and playbook previews (items 20/22) | PARTIAL | Governance + Frontend | **First slice, 2026-09-19 (`b78384f` backend, `2db35d5` UI).** `GET /v1/governance/reviews/change-queue` pages a filtered queue on an opaque keyset cursor ordered by `(created_at, id)` -- status, object type, family, change kind, object, table scope and only-what-I-can-decide -- with `/details` for up to 50 opened rows. `POST /v1/governance/review-batches` freezes a selection: explicit ids with the evidence fingerprint the reviewer saw on each page, or a capped filter snapshot (migration `0871649b2bb1`, `review_batch` and `review_batch_item`, ids, codes, counts and hashes only). `POST .../review-batches/{id}/decision` re-checks every member and decides each through `governance_decision_service.decide_review` in its own savepoint -- the permission check, compare-and-set claim, agent-oversight guard and per-type adapter the single and bulk endpoints use, so there is no new write path -- and reports each member applied or refused (`ALREADY_DECIDED`, `STALE_EVIDENCE`, `EVIDENCE_NOT_SHOWN`, `INDIVIDUAL_DECISION_REQUIRED` for T3, `RATIONALE_REQUIRED`, `CONCURRENT_DECISION`, `NOT_AUTHORIZED`, `TARGET_REFUSED`) with a correction where one exists. AGENT principals are refused at freeze and decide. `GET /v1/playbooks/{id}/dry-run` writes nothing and reports per action that all four (TAG, CLASSIFY, OWN, CERTIFY) auto-apply with no human when `0 < matched <= auto_apply_max_items`, no model involved, and that an auto-applied run has no governed reversal; auto-apply itself is unchanged. The UI is a Batch review tab (`?queue=batch`) on the existing review screen -- no new route for R11-S13 to reconcile -- on the existing `VirtualList`, with selection kept across pages, a freeze preview and per-member outcomes. **Measured on in-memory SQLite, not PostgreSQL:** a 100-row queue page is 3 statements at 10 and at 2,000 queued; freezing 1,000 members is 7 statements, 0.44s; deciding 1,000 real column-description approvals took 12.8s at 14.01 statements per member (14.09 at 100). Evidence: `tests/test_review_batches.py` (20), `tests/test_review_batches_scale.py` (3, the 1,000-table and 1,000-column fixtures), `tests/test_playbook_dry_run.py` (10), `ReviewBatchQueue.test.tsx` (5, with an axe check). **Generated UI contracts closed 2026-09-19:** `reviewBatches.ts` now aliases generated OpenAPI request/response types instead of maintaining duplicate interfaces. Demo and test payloads include the required filters, batch ownership/timestamps, frozen evidence fields and detail diff. Existing screen-facing names remain compatible. Verified: production TypeScript/Vite build and 25 review/batch-review tests passed. **Remaining:** per-type evidence gates (approval checks only that evidence was shown and the change is not T3, so types with no composed evidence can only be batch-rejected); relationship, cross-source and parsed-lineage reviews are not in this queue; dry-run results are not stored or bound to a later run, and have no UI; corrections exist only for description withdrawals, and links are text, not actions; a batch decision is one transaction with no resumable progress; no PostgreSQL timings or batch-against-batch race proof; no browser run; frozen members show as counts. |
